\displaystyle\mathbb{P}^{1,k,(\ell,r),a^{+},a^{-}}_{\textup{free}}(\sup_{x\in[\ell,q]}|B_{j}^{[\ell,q]}(x)|\leq 4^{-1}(1-\mu)\lambda T^{1/2})

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}(\sup_{x\in[0,1]}|B^{[0,1]}(x)|\leq 4^{-1}(1-\mu)\lambda T^{1/4})\geq C^{-1}.

Combining the above estimates, we conclude that

\mathbb{P}^{1,k,(\ell,r),a^{+},a^{-}}_{\textup{free}}(\mathsf{H})\geq C^{-1}e^{-CT^{5/2}}.

Case 2:
r-\ell<2T^{1/2}
.
Set
q=2^{-1}(\ell+r)
. Under the law
\mathbb{P}^{1,k,(\ell,r),a^{+},a^{-}}_{\textup{free}}
,
B_{j}(q)
are Gaussian random variables with mean and variance

\displaystyle m_{j}=2^{-1}(a^{-}_{j}+a^{+}_{j}),\ \sigma^{2}=4^{-1}(r-\ell).

For
x\in[\ell,q]
and
y\in[q,r]
, it holds that

\displaystyle B_{k}(x)-g(x)\geq\lambda_{1}T-|B_{k}(q)-(m_{k}+2^{-1}(r-\ell)b_{1}T)|-\sup_{x\in[\ell,q]}|B_{k}^{[\ell,q]}(x)|,

\displaystyle B_{k}(y)-g(y)\geq\lambda_{1}T-|B_{k}(q)-(m_{k}+2^{-1}(r-\ell)b_{1}T)|-\sup_{y\in[q,r]}|B_{k}^{[q,r]}(y)|.

We have used the assumption (
5.3
) in the above inequality.

Similarly, for
x\in[\ell,q]
,
y\in[q,r]
and
j\in[1,k-1]_{\mathbb{Z}}
, it holds that

\displaystyle B_{j}(x)-B_{j+1}(x)\geq

\displaystyle\lambda_{0}T^{1/2}-|B_{j}(q)-(m_{j}+2^{-1}(r-\ell)b_{1}T)|-\sup_{x\in[\ell,q]}|B_{j}^{[\ell,q]}(x)|

\displaystyle-|B_{j+1}(q)-(m_{j+1}+2^{-1}(r-\ell)b_{1}T)|-\sup_{x\in[\ell,q]}|B_{j+1}^{[\ell,q]}(x)|.

\displaystyle B_{j}(y)-B_{j+1}(y)\geq

\displaystyle\lambda_{0}T^{1/2}-|B_{j}(q)-(m_{j}+2^{-1}(r-\ell)b_{1}T)|-\sup_{y\in[q,r]}|B_{j}^{[q,r]}(y)|

\displaystyle-|B_{j+1}(q)-(m_{j+1}+2^{-1}(r-\ell)b_{1}T)|-\sup_{y\in[q,r]}|B_{j+1}^{[q,r]}(y)|.

We have used the assumption (
5.3
) in the above inequality.

In order to ensure
\mathsf{H}
, it is sufficient to require that for
j\in[1,k]_{\mathbb{Z}}
, it holds that

|B_{j}(q)-(m_{j}+2^{-1}(r-\ell)b_{1}T)|\leq 4^{-1}(1-\mu)\lambda T^{1/2},

\sup_{x\in[\ell,q]}|B_{j}^{[\ell,q]}(x)|\leq 4^{-1}(1-\mu)\lambda T^{1/2},

and

\sup_{x\in[q,r]}|B_{j}^{[q,r]}(x)|\leq 4^{-1}(1-\mu)\lambda T^{1/2},

where
\lambda=\min\{\lambda_{0},\lambda_{1}\}
. Let
N
be a Gaussian random variable with mean
0
and variance
1
. It holds that

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}^{1,k,(\ell,r),a^{+},a^{-}}_{\textup{free}}(|B_{j}(q)-(m_{j}+2^{-1}(r-\ell)b_{1}T)|\leq 4^{-1}(1-\mu)\lambda T^{1/2})

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}(|N-(r-\ell)^{1/2}b_{1}T|\leq 2^{-1}(1-\mu)\lambda(r-\ell)^{-1/2}T^{1/2}).

Because
r-\ell<2T^{1/2}
, it holds that

(r-\ell)^{1/2}b_{1}T<2^{1/2}b_{1}T^{5/4}

and

2^{-1}(1-\mu)\lambda(r-\ell)^{-1/2}T^{1/2}>2^{-3/2}(1-\mu)\lambda T^{1/4}.

Therefore,

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}(|N-(r-\ell)^{1/2}b_{1}T|\leq 2^{-1}(1-\mu)\lambda(r-\ell)^{-1/2}T^{1/2})

\displaystyle\geq

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}(|N-2^{1/2}b_{1}T^{5/4}|\leq 2^{-3/2}(1-\mu)\lambda T^{1/4})

\displaystyle\geq

\displaystyle C^{-1}e^{-CT^{5/2}}.

Next, we bound the event
\sup_{x\in[\ell,q]}|B_{j}^{[\ell,q]}(x)|\leq 4^{-1}(1-\mu)\lambda T^{1/2}
. By a direct computation,

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}^{1,k,(\ell,r),a^{+},a^{-}}_{\textup{free}}(\sup_{x\in[\ell,q]}|B_{j}^{[\ell,q]}(x)|\leq 4^{-1}(1-\mu)\lambda T^{1/2})

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}(\sup_{x\in[0,1]}|B^{[0,1]}(x)|\leq 2^{-3/2}(r-\ell)^{-1/2}(1-\mu)\lambda T^{1/2})

\displaystyle\geq

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}(\sup_{x\in[0,1]}|B^{[0,1]}(x)|\leq 4^{-1}(1-\mu)\lambda T^{1/4})\geq C^{-1}.

We have used
r-\ell<2T^{1/2}
. Similarly,

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}^{1,k,(\ell,r),a^{+},a^{-}}_{\textup{free}}(\sup_{x\in[q,r]}|B_{j}^{[q,r]}(x)|\leq 2^{-2}(1-\mu)\lambda T^{1/2})\geq C^{-1}.

Combining the above estimates, we conclude

\mathbb{P}^{1,k,(\ell,r),a^{+},a^{-}}_{\textup{free}}(\mathsf{H})\geq C^{-1}e^{-CT^{5/2}}.

∎

We are ready to prove Theorem
5.1
for a special case
P\subset(\ell,\ell+T^{1/2})\cup(r-T^{1/2},r)
.

Lemma 5.5
.

Assume that
P\subset(\ell,\ell+T^{1/2})\cup(r-T^{1/2},r)
. Then there exists a constant
C
depending on constants in (
5.1
) and
\mu
such that

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}_{\mathcal{L}}(\mathsf{H})\geq C^{-1}e^{-CT^{5/2}}.

Proof.

The idea is to decompose the Boltzmann weight into
W=\widetilde{W}\cdot\hat{W}
. Roughly speaking,
\widetilde{W}
and
\hat{W}
activate the interaction at the pole points in
(r-T^{1/2},r)
and
(\ell,\ell+T^{1/2})
respectively. Then we apply Corollary
5.4
twice to finish the argument.

Let
\tilde{P}=P\cap(r-T^{1/2},r)
and
\hat{P}=P\setminus\tilde{P}
. If
\tilde{P}
is empty, the assertion of Lemma
5.5
follows from Corollary
5.4
. From now on we assume
\tilde{P}
is non-empty.

Let
\mathbb{P}_{\tilde{\mathcal{L}}}
be the law of
\mathbb{P}^{1,k,(\ell,r),a^{+},a^{-}}_{\textup{free}}
conditioned on jumping over
g(x)
at pole points in
\tilde{P}
. In terms of the Radon-Nikodym derivative relation, it holds that

\displaystyle\frac{{d}\mathbb{P}_{\tilde{\mathcal{L}}}}{{d}\mathbb{P}^{1,k,(\ell,r),a^{+},a^{-}}_{\textup{free}}}(\mathcal{L})=\frac{\widetilde{W}(\mathcal{L})}{\mathbb{E}^{1,k,(\ell,r),a^{+},a^{-}}_{\textup{free}}[\widetilde{W}]}.

Here the weight
\widetilde{W}
is defined by

\displaystyle\widetilde{W}(\mathcal{L})\coloneqq\left\{\begin{array}[]{cc}1,&\mathcal{L}_{j}(p)>g(p)\ \textup{for all}\ p\in\tilde{P}\ \textup{and}\ j\in[1,k]_{\mathbb{Z}},\\
0,&\textup{otherwise}.\end{array}\right.

Correspondingly, we define the remaining weight

\displaystyle\hat{W}(\mathcal{L})\coloneqq\left\{\begin{array}[]{cc}1,&\mathcal{L}_{j}(p)>g(p)\ \textup{for all}\ p\in\hat{P}\ \textup{and}\ j\in[1,k]_{\mathbb{Z}},\\
0,&\textup{otherwise}.\end{array}\right.

Since
W=\widetilde{W}\cdot\hat{W}
, we have

\displaystyle\frac{{d}\mathbb{P}_{\mathcal{L}}}{{d}\mathbb{P}_{\tilde{\mathcal{L}}}}(\mathcal{L})=\frac{\hat{W}(\mathcal{L})}{\mathbb{E}_{\tilde{\mathcal{L}}}[\hat{W}]}.

Consider the following events
\tilde{\mathsf{G}}
and
\hat{\mathsf{G}}
,

\begin{split}\tilde{\mathsf{G}}\coloneqq&\{\mathcal{L}_{j}(p)-\mathcal{L}_{j+1}(p)\geq 2^{-1}(1+\mu)\lambda_{0}T^{1/2}\ \textup{for all}\ p\in\tilde{P}\ \textup{and}\ j\in[1,k-1]_{\mathbb{Z}}\}\\
&\cap\{\mathcal{L}_{k}(p)-g(p)\geq 2^{-1}(1+\mu)\lambda_{1}T\ \textup{for all}\ p\in\tilde{P}\},\end{split}

and

\begin{split}\hat{\mathsf{G}}\coloneqq&\{\mathcal{L}_{j}(p)-\mathcal{L}_{j+1}(p)\geq\mu\lambda_{0}T^{1/2}\ \textup{for all}\ p\in\hat{P}\ \textup{and}\ j\in[1,k-1]_{\mathbb{Z}}\}\\
&\cap\{\mathcal{L}_{k}(p)-g(p)\geq\mu\lambda_{1}T\ \textup{for all}\ p\in\hat{P}\}.\end{split}

Roughly speaking,
\tilde{\mathsf{G}}
and
\hat{\mathsf{G}}
require conditions in
\mathsf{H}
to hold in
\tilde{P}
and
\hat{P}
respectively. The requirements in
\tilde{\mathsf{G}}
is stronger than the ones in
\mathsf{H}
. The reason is that we will apply Corollary
5.4
and Corollary
5.3
to
\hat{P}
conditioned on the occurrence of
\mathsf{\tilde{G}}
.

Because
\hat{W}\leq 1
, we have

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}_{\mathcal{L}}(\mathsf{H})\geq\mathbb{P}_{\mathcal{L}}(\mathsf{H}\cap\tilde{\mathsf{G}})

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}_{\mathcal{L}}(\mathsf{H}|\tilde{\mathsf{G}})\times\mathbb{P}_{\mathcal{L}}(\tilde{\mathsf{G}})=\mathbb{P}_{\mathcal{L}}(\mathsf{H}|\tilde{\mathsf{G}})\times\frac{\mathbb{E}_{\tilde{\mathcal{L}}}[\mathbbm{1}_{\tilde{\mathsf{G}}}\cdot\hat{W}]}{\mathbb{E}_{\tilde{\mathcal{L}}}[\hat{W}]}

\displaystyle\geq

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}_{\mathcal{L}}(\mathsf{H}|\tilde{\mathsf{G}})\times\mathbb{E}_{\tilde{\mathcal{L}}}[\mathbbm{1}_{\tilde{\mathsf{G}}}\cdot\hat{W}]

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}_{\mathcal{L}}(\mathsf{H}|\tilde{\mathsf{G}})\times\mathbb{P}_{\tilde{\mathcal{L}}}(\tilde{\mathsf{G}})\times\mathbb{E}_{\tilde{\mathcal{L}}}[\hat{W}|\tilde{\mathsf{G}}].

Since
\tilde{P}\subset(r-T^{1/2},r)
, we can apply Corollary
5.4
to obtain

\mathbb{P}_{\tilde{\mathcal{L}}}(\tilde{\mathsf{G}})\geq C^{-1}e^{-CT^{5/2}}.

Next, we turn to
\mathbb{P}_{\mathcal{L}}(\mathsf{H}|\tilde{\mathsf{G}})
. Conditioned on a realization of
\tilde{\mathsf{G}}
, the law of
\mathbb{P}_{\mathcal{L}}
restricted to
[\ell,r-T^{1/2}]
is given by the law of free Brownian bridges conditioned on jumping over
g
at those pole points in
\hat{P}
. Therefore, we can apply Corollary
5.4
to bound
\mathbb{P}_{\mathcal{L}}(\mathsf{H}|\tilde{\mathsf{G}})
.

We proceed to establish estimates that are uniform in any realizations of
\tilde{\mathsf{G}}
. To simplify the notation, we fix a realization of
\tilde{\mathsf{G}}
. That is, we fix
\mathcal{L}_{j}(p)
for
j\in[1,k]_{\mathbb{Z}}
and
p\in\tilde{P}
which satisfy the requirements in
\tilde{\mathsf{G}}
. We denote the event of such realization by
\tilde{\mathsf{G}}_{0}
. Because
\hat{\mathsf{G}}\cap\tilde{\mathsf{G}}_{0}\subset\mathsf{H}
, it holds that
\mathbb{P}_{\mathcal{L}}(\mathsf{H}|\tilde{\mathsf{G}}_{0})\geq\mathbb{P}_{\mathcal{L}}(\hat{\mathsf{G}}|\tilde{\mathsf{G}}_{0})
.

In order to apply Corollary
5.4
, we define the following quantities. Let
p_{0}
be the smallest element of
\tilde{P}
. Define

\displaystyle\hat{\ell}\coloneqq\ell,\ \hat{r}\coloneqq p_{0},\ \hat{a}^{-}_{j}\coloneqq a^{-}_{j},\ \hat{a}^{+}_{j}\coloneqq\mathcal{L}_{j}(p_{0}).

Conditioned on
\tilde{\mathsf{G}}_{0}
, the law of
{\mathcal{L}}|_{[\hat{\ell},\hat{r}]}
, denoted by
\hat{\mathbb{P}}_{\mathcal{L}}
, is the law of
\mathbb{P}_{\textup{free}}^{1,k,(\hat{\ell},\hat{r}),\hat{a}^{+},\hat{a}^{-}}
conditioned on jumping over
{g}(x)
at pole points in
\hat{P}
. In terms of the Radon-Nikodym derivative relation, it holds that

\displaystyle\frac{d\hat{\mathbb{P}}_{\mathcal{L}}}{d\mathbb{P}_{\textup{free}}^{1,k,(\hat{\ell},\hat{r}),\hat{a}^{+},\hat{a}^{-}}}(\mathcal{L})=\frac{\hat{W}(\mathcal{L})}{\mathbb{E}_{\textup{free}}^{1,k,(\hat{\ell},\hat{r}),\hat{a}^{+},\hat{a}^{-}}[\hat{W}]}.\

To apply Corollary
5.4
, we check the assumption (
5.3
).
\tilde{\mathsf{G}}_{0}
implies that

\begin{split}&\hat{a}^{\pm}_{j}-\hat{a}^{\pm}_{j+1}\geq 2^{-1}(1+\mu)\lambda_{0}T^{1/2}\ \textup{for all}\ j\in[1,k-1]_{\mathbb{Z}},\\
&\hat{a}_{k}^{-}-g(\hat{\ell})\geq 2^{-1}(1+\mu)\lambda_{1}T,\ \textup{and}\ \hat{a}_{k}^{+}-g(\hat{r})\geq 2^{-1}(1+\mu)\lambda_{1}T.\\
\end{split}

Therefore, the assumption (
5.3
) holds for these data with slightly different bounds. By Corollary
5.4
, it holds that
\mathbb{P}_{\mathcal{L}}(\hat{\mathsf{G}}|\tilde{\mathsf{G}}_{0})\geq C^{-1}e^{-CT^{5/2}}.
This ensures

\mathbb{P}_{\mathcal{L}}(\mathsf{H}|\tilde{\mathsf{G}}_{0})\geq\mathbb{P}_{\mathcal{L}}(\hat{\mathsf{G}}|\tilde{\mathsf{G}}_{0})\geq C^{-1}e^{-CT^{5/2}}.

Because this holds for any realization
\tilde{\mathsf{G}}_{0}
of
\mathsf{\tilde{G}}
, we obtain

\mathbb{P}_{\mathcal{L}}(\mathsf{H}|\tilde{\mathsf{G}})\geq C^{-1}e^{-CT^{5/2}}.

Next, we turn to
\mathbb{E}_{\tilde{\mathcal{L}}}[\hat{W}|\mathsf{G}_{0}]
. Conditioned on
\tilde{\mathsf{G}}_{0}
, The law of
\tilde{\mathcal{L}}|_{[\hat{\ell},\hat{r}]}
is identical to
\mathbb{P}_{\textup{free}}^{1,k,(\hat{\ell},\hat{r}),\hat{a}^{+},\hat{a}^{-}}
. The assumption (
5.3
) is already checked above. Applying Corollary
5.3
, it holds that

\mathbb{E}_{\tilde{\mathcal{L}}}[\hat{W}|\mathsf{G}_{0}]=\mathbb{E}_{\textup{free}}^{1,k,(\hat{\ell},\hat{r}),\hat{a}^{+},\hat{a}^{-}}[\hat{W}]\geq C^{-1}e^{-CT^{5/2}}.

Because this holds for any realization
\tilde{\mathsf{G}}_{0}
of
\mathsf{\tilde{G}}
, we obtain

\mathbb{E}_{\tilde{\mathcal{L}}}[\hat{W}|\tilde{\mathsf{G}}]\geq C^{-1}e^{-CT^{5/2}}.

Putting the above estimates together, we conclude that

\mathbb{P}_{\mathcal{L}}(\mathsf{H})\geq C^{-1}e^{-CT^{5/2}}.

∎

5.2.
The general case.

In this subsection, we consider the general case of
P
and prove Theorem
5.1
. The approach is similar to the one in proving Lemma
5.5
. We decompose the Boltzmann weight into
W={W}^{\prime}\cdot\check{W}
. The weights
{W}^{\prime}
and
\check{W}
activate the interaction at the pole points in
[\ell+T^{1/2},r-T^{1/2}]
and
(\ell,\ell+T^{1/2})\cup(r-T^{1/2},r)
respectively. Lemma
5.5
is then applicable to
\check{W}.

Let
{P}^{\prime}=P\cap[\ell+T^{1/2},r-T^{1/2}]
and
\check{P}=P\setminus P^{\prime}
. Suppose
{P}^{\prime}
is empty, then Theorem
5.1
follows from Lemma
5.5
. From now on we assume
P^{\prime}
is non-empty.

Let
\mathbb{P}_{\mathcal{L}}^{\prime}
be the law of
\mathbb{P}^{1,k,(\ell,r),a^{+},a^{-}}_{\textup{free}}
conditioned on jumping over
g(x)
at pole points in
{P}^{\prime}
. In terms of the Radon-Nikodym derivative relation, it holds that

\displaystyle\frac{{d}\mathbb{P}_{\mathcal{L}^{\prime}}}{{d}\mathbb{P}^{1,k,(\ell,r),a^{+},a^{-}}_{\textup{free}}}(\mathcal{L})=\frac{{W^{\prime}}(\mathcal{L})}{\mathbb{E}^{1,k,(\ell,r),a^{+},a^{-}}_{\textup{free}}[{W^{\prime}}]}.

Here the weight
W^{\prime}
is defined by

\displaystyle{W}^{\prime}(\mathcal{L})

\displaystyle\coloneqq\left\{\begin{array}[]{cc}1,&\mathcal{L}_{j}(p)>g(p)\ \textup{for all}\ p\in P^{\prime}\ \textup{and}\ j\in[1,k]_{\mathbb{Z}},\\
0,&\textup{otherwise}\end{array}\right.

Correspondingly, we define

\displaystyle\check{W}(\mathcal{L})

\displaystyle\coloneqq\left\{\begin{array}[]{cc}1,&\mathcal{L}_{j}(p)>g(p)\ \textup{for all}\ p\in\check{P}\ \textup{and}\ j\in[1,k]_{\mathbb{Z}},\\
0,&\textup{otherwise}\end{array}\right.

Since
{W}^{\prime}\cdot\check{W}=W
, we have

\displaystyle\frac{{d}\mathbb{P}_{\mathcal{L}}}{{d}\mathbb{P}_{\mathcal{L}^{\prime}}}(\mathcal{L})=\frac{\check{W}(\mathcal{L})}{\mathbb{E}_{\mathcal{L}^{\prime}}[\check{W}]}.

We further define
\mathcal{L}^{\prime}_{+}
to be ordered at pole points in
P^{\prime}
. Let
\mathbb{P}_{\mathcal{L}_{+}}^{\prime}
be the law of
\mathbb{P}_{\mathcal{L}^{\prime}}
conditioned being ordered at pole points in
{P}^{\prime}
. In terms of the Radon-Nikodym derivative relation, it holds that

\displaystyle\frac{{d}\mathbb{P}_{\mathcal{L}^{\prime}_{+}}}{{d}\mathbb{P}^{1,k,(\ell,r),a^{+},a^{-}}_{\textup{free}}}(\mathcal{L})=\frac{{W^{\prime}_{+}}(\mathcal{L})}{\mathbb{E}^{1,k,(\ell,r),a^{+},a^{-}}_{\textup{free}}[{W^{\prime}_{+}}]}.

Here the weight
W^{\prime}_{+}
is defined by

\displaystyle{W}^{\prime}_{+}(\mathcal{L})

\displaystyle\coloneqq\left\{\begin{array}[]{cc}1,&\mathcal{L}_{k}(p)>g(p),\,\ \mathcal{L}_{j+1}(p)>\mathcal{L}_{j}(p)\ \textup{for all}\ p\in P^{\prime}\ \textup{and}\ j\in[1,k]_{\mathbb{Z}},\\
0,&\textup{otherwise}\end{array}\right.

Since
W^{\prime}_{+}=W^{\prime}\cdot W^{\prime}_{+}
, we have

\displaystyle\frac{{d}\mathbb{P}_{\mathcal{L}^{\prime}_{+}}}{{d}\mathbb{P}_{\mathcal{L}^{\prime}}}(\mathcal{L})=\frac{{W}^{\prime}_{+}(\mathcal{L})}{\mathbb{E}_{\mathcal{L}^{\prime}}[{W}^{\prime}_{+}]}.

Lemma 5.6
.

There exists a constant
C
depending on constants in (
5.1
) such that

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}_{{\mathcal{L}^{\prime}}}[{W}^{\prime}_{+}]\geq C^{-1}e^{-CT}.

Proof.

If
a^{\pm}_{j}=a^{\pm}_{j+1}
for
j\in[1,k-1]_{\mathbb{Z}}
,
\{\mathcal{L}^{\prime}_{j}(p),j\in[1,k]_{\mathbb{Z}}\,\}
are identically distributed and it follows that

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}_{{\mathcal{L}^{\prime}}}(\mathcal{L}^{\prime}_{j}(p)>\mathcal{L}^{\prime}_{j+1}(p)\ \textup{for}\ j\in[1,k-1]_{\mathbb{Z}})=\frac{1}{k!}.

Since
a^{\pm}_{j}>a^{\pm}_{j+1}
for
j\in[1,k-1]_{\mathbb{Z}}
, we have for any
p\in P^{\prime}
,

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}_{{\mathcal{L}^{\prime}}}(\mathcal{L}^{\prime}_{j}(p)>\mathcal{L}^{\prime}_{j+1}(p)\ \textup{for}\ j\in[1,k-1]_{\mathbb{Z}})\geq\frac{1}{k!}.

Because
|P^{\prime}|\leq b_{0}T
, we inductively get

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}_{{\mathcal{L}^{\prime}}}[{W}^{\prime}_{+}]\geq(k!)^{-b_{0}T}.

∎

Consider the event
\mathsf{G}
,

\begin{split}\mathsf{G}\coloneqq&\{\mathcal{L}_{j}(p)-\mathcal{L}_{j+1}(p)\geq 2^{-1}(1+\mu)\lambda_{0}T^{1/2}\ \textup{for all}\ p\in P^{\prime}\ \textup{and}\ j\in[1,k-1]_{\mathbb{Z}}\}\\
&\cap\{\mathcal{L}_{k}(p)-g(p)\geq 2^{-1}(1+\mu)\lambda_{1}T\ \textup{for all}\ p\in P^{\prime}\}.\end{split}

Compared to
\mathsf{H}
, the event
\mathsf{G}
requires a stronger gap at pole points in
P^{\prime}
.

Lemma 5.7
.

There exists a constant
C
depending on constants in (
5.1
), (
5.2
) and
\mu
such that

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}_{\mathcal{L}^{\prime}_{+}}(\mathsf{G})\geq C^{-1}e^{-CT^{5/2}}.

We postpone the proof of Lemma
5.7
and prove Theorem
5.1
below.

Proof of Theorem
5.1
.

Because
\check{W}\leq 1
, we have

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}_{\mathcal{L}}(\mathsf{H})\geq\mathbb{P}_{\mathcal{L}}(\mathsf{H}\cap\mathsf{G})

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}_{\mathcal{L}}(\mathsf{H}|\mathsf{G})\times\mathbb{P}_{\mathcal{L}}(\mathsf{G})=\mathbb{P}_{\mathcal{L}}(\mathsf{H}|\mathsf{G})\times\frac{\mathbb{E}_{\mathcal{L}^{\prime}}[\mathbbm{1}_{\mathsf{G}}\cdot\check{W}]}{\mathbb{E}_{\mathcal{L}^{\prime}}[\check{W}]}

\displaystyle\geq

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}_{\mathcal{L}}(\mathsf{H}|\mathsf{G})\times\mathbb{E}_{\mathcal{L}^{\prime}}[\mathbbm{1}_{\mathsf{G}}\cdot\check{W}]

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}_{\mathcal{L}}(\mathsf{H}|\mathsf{G})\times\mathbb{P}_{\mathcal{L}^{\prime}}(\mathsf{G})\times\mathbb{E}_{\mathcal{L}^{\prime}}[\check{W}|\mathsf{G}].

\mathbb{P}_{\mathcal{L}}(\mathsf{H}|\mathsf{G})
and
\mathbb{E}_{{\mathcal{L}}^{\prime}}[\check{W}|\mathsf{G}]
could be bounded similarly as in the proof of Lemma
5.5
. Conditioned on a realization of
\mathsf{G}
, the law
\mathbb{P}_{\mathcal{L}^{\prime}}
behaves like the law of free Brownian bridges. Furthermore, the law
\mathbb{P}_{{\mathcal{L}}}
is given by the law of those free Brownian bridges conditioned on jumping over
g
at pole points in
\check{P}
. Therefore, we can apply Corollary
5.3
and Corollary
5.4
to bound
\mathbb{E}_{\tilde{\mathcal{L}}}[\check{W}|\mathsf{G}]
and
\mathbb{P}_{\mathcal{L}}(\mathsf{H}|\mathsf{G})
respectively. From Corollary
5.4
, we have
\mathbb{P}_{\mathcal{L}}(\mathsf{H}|\mathsf{G})\geq C^{-1}e^{-CT^{5/2}}.
From Corollary
5.3
, we have
\mathbb{E}_{\mathcal{L}^{\prime}}[\check{W}|\mathsf{G}]\geq C^{-1}e^{-CT^{5/2}}.
We omit the detail here and refer the readers to the proof of Lemma
5.5
for a similar argument.

It suffices to show
\mathbb{P}_{\mathcal{L}^{\prime}}(\mathsf{G})\geq C^{-1}e^{-CT^{5/2}}
. Note that
W^{\prime}_{+}=1
when
\mathsf{G}
holds. Therefore, we have

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}_{\mathcal{L}^{\prime}}(\mathsf{G})=\mathbb{E}_{\mathcal{L}^{\prime}}[\mathbbm{1}_{\mathsf{G}}\cdot W^{\prime}_{+}]=\frac{\mathbb{E}_{\mathcal{L}^{\prime}}[\mathbbm{1}_{\mathsf{G}}\cdot W^{\prime}_{+}]}{\mathbb{E}_{\mathcal{L}^{\prime}}[W^{\prime}_{+}]}\times\mathbb{E}_{\mathcal{L}^{\prime}}[W^{\prime}_{+}]=\mathbb{P}_{\mathcal{L}^{\prime}_{+}}(\mathsf{G})\times\mathbb{E}_{\mathcal{L}^{\prime}}[W^{\prime}_{+}]

Lemmas
5.6
and
5.7
implies that there exists
C>0
such that

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}_{\mathcal{L}^{\prime}}(\mathsf{G})\geq C^{-1}e^{-CT^{5/2}}.

Putting the above estimates together, we conclude that

\mathbb{P}_{\mathcal{L}}(\mathsf{H})\geq C^{-1}e^{-CT^{5/2}}.

∎

5.3.
Proof of Lemma
5.7

The proof of Lemma
5.7
is based on a modification of an approached of Hammond. The key idea is the following simple calculation of normal distribution. Let
N
be a Gaussian random variable with mean
0
and variance
\sigma^{2}
and let
M_{1}>>M_{2}>>\sigma
. The probability that
N\geq M_{1}+M_{2}
, conditioned on
N\geq M_{1}
is given by

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}(N\geq M_{1}+M_{2}\,|\,N\geq M_{1})\approx e^{-\sigma^{-2}M_{1}M_{2}}.

In the current context, the curve
\mathcal{L}^{\prime}_{+}
behaves like
N
conditioned on greater than
T^{2}
. This is because the largest possible value of
g(x)
is of order
T^{2}
. The variance is of order
T^{1/2}
because
P^{\prime}\subset(\ell+T^{1/2},r-T^{1/2})
. The event
\mathsf{G}
behaves like
N\geq T^{2}+T
because it requires
\mathcal{L}_{k}\geq g+T
. Therefore, we obtain

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}_{\mathcal{L}^{\prime}_{+}}(\mathsf{G})\approx e^{-T^{5/2}}.

We carry out the above heuristic argument in the rest of this subsection. We may assume without loss of generality that
g(\ell)=g(r)=0
. See the discussion at the beginning of the proof of Lemma
5.2
.

Lemma 5.8
.

There exists a constant
C_{1}
depending on constants in (
5.1
) such that

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}^{1,k,(\ell,r),a^{+},a^{-}}_{\textup{free}}[{W}^{\prime}_{+}]\geq C_{1}^{-1}e^{-C_{1}T^{3}}.

Proof.

The proof involves direct computations of Brownian bridges and is similar to the one for Lemma Lemma
5.2
.

Set
q=2^{-1}(\ell+r)
. Under the law
\mathbb{P}^{1,k,(\ell,r),a^{+},a^{-}}_{\textup{free}}
,
B_{j}(q)
are normal distributions with mean and variance

\displaystyle m_{j}=2^{-1}(a^{-}_{j}+a^{+}_{j}),\ \sigma^{2}=4^{-1}(r-\ell).

For all
x\in[\ell,q]
and
y\in[q,r]
, we have

\displaystyle B_{k}(x)-g(x)\geq\lambda_{1}T-|B_{k}(q)-(m_{k}+2^{-1}(r-\ell)b_{1}T)|-\sup_{x\in[\ell,q]}|B_{k}^{[\ell,q]}(x)|,

\displaystyle B_{k}(y)-g(y)\geq\lambda_{1}T-|B_{k}(q)-(m_{k}+2^{-1}(r-\ell)b_{1}T)|-\sup_{y\in[q,r]}|B_{k}^{[q,r]}(y)|.

Similarly, for
1\leq j\leq k-1
,
x\in[\ell,q]
and
y\in[q,r]
, we have

\displaystyle B_{j}(x)-B_{j+1}(x)\geq

\displaystyle\lambda_{0}T^{1/2}-|B_{j}(q)-(m_{j}+2^{-1}(r-\ell)b_{1}T)|-\sup_{x\in[\ell,q]}|B_{j}^{[\ell,q]}(x)|

\displaystyle-|B_{j+1}(q)-(m_{j+1}+2^{-1}(r-\ell)b_{1}T)|-\sup_{x\in[\ell,q]}|B_{j+1}^{[\ell,q]}(x)|.

\displaystyle B_{j}(y)-B_{j+1}(y)\geq

\displaystyle\lambda_{0}T^{1/2}-|B_{j}(q)-(m_{j}+2^{-1}(r-\ell)b_{1}T)|-\sup_{y\in[q,r]}|B_{j}^{[q,r]}(y)|

\displaystyle-|B_{j+1}(q)-(m_{j+1}+2^{-1}(r-\ell)b_{1}T)|-\sup_{y\in[q,r]}|B_{j+1}^{[q,r]}(y)|.

Therefore, to ensure for all
x\in[\ell,r]
,

B_{1}(x)>B_{2}(x)>\dots>B_{k}(x)>g(x),

it suffices to require that for all
j\in[1,k]_{\mathbb{Z}}
,

|B_{j}(q)-(m_{j}+2^{-1}(r-\ell)b_{1}T)|<4^{-1}\lambda T^{1/2},

and

\sup_{x\in[\ell,q]}|B_{j}^{[\ell,q]}(x)|\leq 4^{-1}\lambda T^{1/2},\ \sup_{x\in[q,r]}|B_{j}^{[q,r]}(x)|\leq 4^{-1}\lambda T^{1/2}.

Here
\lambda=\min\{\lambda_{0},\lambda_{1}\}
. Let
N
be a normal distribution with mean
0
and variance
1
.

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}^{1,k,(\ell,r),a^{+},a^{-}}_{\textup{free}}(|B_{j}(q)-(m_{j}+2^{-1}(r-\ell)b_{1}T)|<4^{-1}\lambda T^{1/2})

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}(|N-(r-\ell)^{1/2}b_{1}T|<2^{-1}\lambda(r-\ell)^{-1/2}T^{1/2}).

Because
r-\ell\leq b_{0}T
,

(r-\ell)^{1/2}b_{1}T\leq b_{0}^{1/2}b_{1}T^{3/2}

and

2^{-1}\lambda(r-\ell)^{-1/2}T^{1/2}\geq 2^{-1}\lambda b_{0}^{-1/2}.

Thus

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}^{1,k,(\ell,r),a^{+},a^{-}}_{\textup{free}}(|B_{j}(q)-(m_{j}+2^{-1}(r-\ell)b_{1}T)|<4^{-1}(1-\mu)\lambda T^{1/2})

\displaystyle\geq

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}(|N-b_{0}^{1/2}b_{1}T^{3/2}|<2^{-1}\lambda b_{0}^{-1/2})\geq C^{-1}e^{-CT^{3}}.

Next, we bound the event
\displaystyle\sup_{x\in[\ell,q]}|B_{j}^{[\ell,q]}(x)|\leq 4^{-1}\lambda T^{1/2}
. It holds that

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}^{1,k,(\ell,r),a^{+},a^{-}}_{\textup{free}}(\sup_{x\in[\ell,q]}|B_{j}^{[\ell,q]}(x)|\leq 4^{-1}\lambda T^{1/2})

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}(\sup_{x\in[0,1]}|B^{[0,1]}(x)|\leq 2^{-3/2}(r-\ell)^{-1/2}\lambda T^{1/2})

\displaystyle\geq

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}(\sup_{x\in[0,1]}|B^{[0,1]}(x)|\leq 2^{-3/2}\lambda b_{0}^{-1/2})\geq C^{-1}.

Similarly,

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}_{\textup{free}}(\sup_{x\in[q,r]}|B_{j}^{[q,r]}(x)|\leq 4^{-1}(1-\mu)\lambda T^{1/2})\geq C^{-1}.

Combining the above estimates, we conclude that

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}^{1,k,(\ell,r),a^{+},a^{-}}_{\textup{free}}[{W}^{\prime}_{+}]\geq C^{-1}e^{-CT^{3}}.

The assertion follows by taking
C_{1}
large enough.
∎

Let
m+1=|P^{\prime}|
be the number of elements in
P^{\prime}
. We label them as
\{p_{0}<p_{1}<\dots<p_{m}\}.
Let
C_{2}
be a large number to be determined. Consider the event

\displaystyle\mathsf{Gap}\coloneqq\{|\mathcal{L}_{j}(p_{i})-\mathcal{L}_{j}(p_{0})|\leq C_{2}T^{2}\ \textup{for all}\ j\in[1,k]_{\mathbb{Z}}\ \textup{and}\ i\in[1,m]_{\mathbb{Z}}\}.

In the lemma below, we show that for
C_{2}
large enough, it holds that
\mathbb{P}_{\mathcal{L}^{\prime}_{+}}(\mathsf{Gap})\geq 2^{-1}.

Lemma 5.9
.

There exists a constant
C_{2}
depending on constants in (
5.1
), (
5.2
) such that

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}_{\mathcal{L}^{\prime}_{+}}(\mathsf{Gap})\geq 2^{-1}.

Proof.

From the view of Lemma
5.8
, it suffices to show that for large enough
C_{2}
, we have

\mathbb{P}^{1,k,(\ell,r),a^{+},a^{-}}_{\textup{free}}(\mathsf{Gap}^{\textup{c}})\leq 2^{-1}C_{1}^{-1}e^{-C_{1}T^{3}},

where
C_{1}
is the constant in Lemma
5.8
. From the assumptions (
5.3
) and (
5.4
),

\displaystyle\left|B_{j}(p_{i})-B_{j}(p_{0})\right|\leq

\displaystyle\left|B^{[\ell,r]}_{j}(p_{i})-B^{[\ell,r]}_{j}(p_{0})\right|+\left|\frac{(p_{i}-p_{0})(a_{j}^{+}-a_{j}^{-})}{r-\ell}\right|

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\left|B^{[\ell,r]}_{j}(p_{i})-B^{[\ell,r]}_{j}(p_{0})\right|+b_{2}T^{2}.

Hence for given
j\in[1,k]_{\mathbb{Z}}
and
i\in[1,m]_{\mathbb{Z}}
,

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}^{1,k,(\ell,r),a^{+},a^{-}}_{\textup{free}}(\left|B_{j}(p_{i})-B_{j}(p_{0})\right|>C_{2}T^{2})

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}^{1,k,(\ell,r),a^{+},a^{-}}_{\textup{free}}\left(\left|B_{j}^{[\ell,r]}(p_{i})-B_{j}^{[\ell,r]}(p_{0})\right|>(C_{2}-b_{2})T^{2}\right)

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}^{1,k,(\ell,r),a^{+},a^{-}}_{\textup{free}}\left(\sup_{x\in[\ell,r]}\left|B_{j}^{[\ell,r]}(x)\right|>2^{-1}(C_{2}-b_{2})T^{2}\right)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}\left(\sup_{x\in[0,1]}\left|B^{[0,1]}(x)\right|>2^{-1}(C_{2}-b_{2})(r-\ell)^{-1/2}T^{2}\right)

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}\left(\sup_{x\in[0,1]}\left|B^{[0,1]}(x)\right|>2^{-1}b_{0}^{-1/2}(C_{2}-b_{2})T^{3/2}\right).

By taking
C_{2}
large enough, we can obtain

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}^{1,k,(\ell,r),a^{+},a^{-}}_{\textup{free}}(\left|B_{j}(p_{i})-B_{j}(p_{0})\right|>C_{2}T^{2})\leq 2^{-1}k^{-1}m^{-1}C_{1}^{-1}e^{-C_{1}T^{3}}.

Taking union over
j\in[1,k]_{\mathbb{Z}}
and
i\in[1,m]_{\mathbb{Z}}
, it holds that

\mathbb{P}^{1,k,(\ell,r),a^{+},a^{-}}_{\textup{free}}(\mathsf{Gap}^{\textup{c}})\leq 2^{-1}C_{1}^{-1}e^{-C_{1}T^{3}}.

As a result, we conclude that

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}_{\mathcal{L}^{\prime}_{+}}(\mathsf{Gap}^{\textup{c}})=\frac{\mathbb{E}^{1,k,(\ell,r),a^{+},a^{-}}_{\textup{free}}[\mathbbm{1}_{\mathsf{Gap}^{\textup{c}}}\cdot W^{\prime}_{+}]}{\mathbb{E}^{1,k,(\ell,r),a^{+},a^{-}}_{\textup{free}}[W^{\prime}_{+}]}\leq\frac{\mathbb{P}^{1,k,(\ell,r),a^{+},a^{-}}_{\textup{free}}(\mathsf{Gap}^{\textup{c}})}{\mathbb{E}^{1,k,(\ell,r),a^{+},a^{-}}_{\textup{free}}[W^{\prime}_{+}]}\leq 2^{-1}.

∎

From now on we fix the choice of
C_{2}
.

We write
\mathbb{P}_{0}
for the law of
\mathbb{P}^{1,k,(\ell,r),a^{+},a^{-}}_{\textup{free}}
conditioned on
\mathsf{Gap}
and write
\mathbb{E}_{0}
for the expectation of
\mathbb{P}_{0}
. To simplify the notation, we further take a realization of
\mathsf{Gap}
. That is, for
j\in[1,k]_{\mathbb{Z}}
and
i\in[1,m]_{\mathbb{Z}}
, we fix
c_{j,i}\in\mathbb{R}
with
|c_{j,i}|\leq C_{2}T^{2}
. We abuse the notation and still use
\mathbb{P}_{0}
to denote the law of
\mathbb{P}^{1,k,(\ell,r),a^{+},a^{-}}_{\textup{free}}
conditioned on

\displaystyle B_{j}(p_{i})-B(p_{0})=c_{j,i}.

The estimates below will be uniform in any such choice.

Under the law of
\mathbb{P}_{0}
,
B_{j}(p_{0})
are independent Gaussian random variables with mean and variance

\displaystyle m_{j}=

\displaystyle\frac{a^{-}_{j}}{1+(r-p_{m})^{-1}(p_{0}-\ell)}+\frac{a^{+}_{j}-c_{j,m}}{1+(p_{0}-\ell)^{-1}(r-p_{m})},

\displaystyle\sigma_{0}^{2}=

\displaystyle\frac{1}{(p_{0}-\ell)^{-1}+(r-p_{m})^{-1}}.

To see this, by a direct computation, the p.d.f. of
B_{j}(p_{0})
is proportional to

\displaystyle\exp\left(-\frac{(x-a^{-}_{j})^{2}}{2(p_{0}-\ell)}-\frac{(a^{+}_{j}-c_{j,m}-x)^{2}}{2(r-p_{m})}\right)\propto\exp\left({-\frac{(x-m_{j})^{2}}{2\sigma_{0}^{2}}}\right).

Furthermore,
{W}^{\prime}_{+}=1
if and only if

\displaystyle B_{k}(p_{0})

\displaystyle>\max_{0\leq i\leq m}\{g(p_{i})-c_{k,i}\}=:h_{k},

\displaystyle B_{j}(p_{0})-B_{j+1}(p_{0})

\displaystyle>\max_{0\leq i\leq m}\{c_{j+1,i}-c_{j,i}\}=:h_{j}.

Here we adapt the convention that
c_{j,0}=0
. Denote such event by
\mathsf{Q}
. Explicitly,

\displaystyle\mathsf{Q}\coloneqq\{B_{k}(p_{0})>h_{k},\ \textup{and}\ B_{j}(p_{0})-B_{j+1}(p_{0})>h_{j}\ \textup{for all}\ j\in[1,k-1]_{\mathbb{Z}}\}.

We check that

(5.5)

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}_{\mathcal{L}^{\prime}_{+}}(\mathsf{G}\,|\,\mathsf{Gap})=\mathbb{P}_{0}(\mathsf{G}\,|\,\mathsf{Q}).

Lemma 5.10
.

There exists a constant
C_{3}
depending on constants in (
5.1
) and (
5.2
) such that

\mathbb{P}_{0}(\mathsf{Q})\geq C_{3}^{-1}e^{-C_{3}\sigma_{0}^{-2}T^{4}}.

Proof.

It can be checked directly that the event

\displaystyle B_{j}(p_{0})\in\sum_{i=j}^{k}|h_{i}|+2(k-j)\sigma_{0}+(0,\sigma_{0}]\ \textup{for all}\ j\in[1,k]_{\mathbb{Z}}

is contained in
\mathsf{Q}
. Let
N
be a Gaussian random variable with mean
0
and variance
1
.

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}_{0}\left(B_{j}(p_{0})\in\sum_{i=j}^{k}|h_{i}|+2(k-j)\sigma_{0}+(0,\sigma_{0}]\right)=\mathbb{P}\left(N\in\sigma_{0}^{-1}\left(\sum_{i=j}^{k}|h_{i}|-m_{j}\right)+2(k-j)+(0,1]\right).

Under the assumptions (
5.3
) and (
5.4
), for
j\in[1,k]_{\mathbb{Z}}
it holds that

|h_{j}|\leq(2C_{2}+b_{0}b_{1})T^{2},

|m_{j}|\leq(b_{2}+C_{2})T^{2}.

Because
T^{-2}\leq p_{0}-\ell,r-p_{m}\leq b_{0}T
, it holds that

2^{1/2}b^{-1/2}_{0}T^{-1/2}\leq\sigma_{0}^{-1}\leq 2^{1/2}T^{-1/4}.

Therefore,

\displaystyle\left|\sigma_{0}^{-1}\left(\sum_{i=j}^{k}|h_{i}|-m_{j}\right)+2(k-j)\right|\leq C\sigma_{0}^{-1}T^{2}.

And

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}_{0}\left(B_{j}(p_{0})\in\sum_{i=j}^{k}|h_{i}|+2(k-j)\sigma_{0}+(0,\sigma_{0}]\right)\geq\mathbb{P}\left(N\in C\sigma_{0}^{-1}T^{2}+(0,1]\right)\geq C^{-1}e^{-C\sigma_{0}^{-2}T^{4}}.

Because
B_{j}(p_{0})
are independent under the law
\mathbb{P}_{0}
, we conclude that

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}_{0}(\mathsf{Q})\geq C^{-1}e^{-C\sigma_{0}^{-2}T^{4}}.

The assertion follows by taking
C_{3}
large enough.
∎

Let
C_{4}
be a large constant to be determined.

Lemma 5.11
.

Assume that
C_{4}\geq 2(b_{2}+C_{2})
. Then there exists a universal constant
C_{5}
such that

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}_{0}(B_{1}(p_{0})>C_{4}T^{2})\leq C_{5}e^{-C_{5}^{-1}C_{4}^{2}\sigma_{0}^{-2}T^{4}}.

Proof.

Let
N
be a Gaussian random variable with mean
0
and variance
1
. It holds that

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}_{0}(B_{1}(p_{0})>C_{4}T^{2})=\mathbb{P}(N>\sigma_{0}^{-1}(C_{4}T^{2}-m_{1})).

Using
|m_{1}|\leq(b_{2}+C_{2})T^{2}
, we have

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}_{0}(B_{1}(p_{0})>C_{4}T^{2})\leq

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}(N>\sigma_{0}^{-1}(C_{4}-C_{2}-b_{2})T^{2})

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}(N>2^{-1}\sigma_{0}^{-1}C_{4}T^{2}).

Hence the assertion follows.
∎

Lemma 5.12
.

Assume that
C_{4}\geq 2(b_{2}+C_{2})
. Then there exists a constant
C_{6}
depending on constants in (
5.1
) such that

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}_{0}(\mathsf{G}^{\textup{c}}\cap\{B_{1}(p_{0})\leq C_{4}T^{2}\}|\mathsf{Q})\leq 1-C_{6}^{-1}e^{-C_{6}C_{4}T^{5/2}}.

Proof.

Let
\mathsf{J}=\mathsf{G}^{\textup{c}}\cap\mathsf{Q}\cap\{B_{1}(p_{0})\leq C_{4}T^{2}\}
and
\Omega\subset\mathbb{R}^{k}
be the set such that

\mathsf{J}=\{(B_{1}(p_{0}),B_{2}(p_{0}),\dots,B_{k}(p_{0}))\in\Omega\}.

If
\Omega
is of measure zero, the assertion is clearly true. From now on we assume
\Omega
has positive measure.

Let
\alpha=\lambda_{0}T^{1/2}
and
\beta=\lambda_{1}T
. Define the map
\Phi:\mathbb{R}^{k}\to\mathbb{R}^{k}
by

\displaystyle\Phi_{j}(x_{1},x_{2}\dots,x_{k})\coloneqq x_{j}+\alpha(k-j)+\beta.

Define

\displaystyle\Phi(\mathsf{J})\coloneqq\{(B_{1}(p_{0}),B_{2}(p_{0}),\dots,B_{k}(p_{0}))\in\Phi(\Omega)\}.

It holds that
\Phi(\mathsf{J})\cup\mathsf{J}\subset\mathsf{Q}
and
\Phi(\mathsf{J})\cap\mathsf{G}^{\textup{c}}=\phi
. Hence we have

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}_{0}(\mathsf{G}^{\textup{c}}\cap\{B_{1}(p_{0})\leq M\}|\mathsf{Q})\leq\frac{\mathbb{P}_{0}(\mathsf{J})}{\mathbb{P}_{0}(\mathsf{J})+\mathbb{P}_{0}(\Phi(\mathsf{J}))}.

Recall that
m_{j}
and
\sigma_{0}^{2}
are the means and variance of
B_{j}(p_{0})
under the law
\mathbb{P}_{0}
.

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}_{0}(\mathsf{J})=\int_{\Omega}\prod_{j=1}^{k}\rho_{\sigma_{0}^{2},m_{j}}(x_{j})\,\prod_{j=1}^{k}{d}{x}_{j}.

Because the Jacobian of
\Phi
equals
1
,

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}_{0}(\Phi(\mathsf{J}))

\displaystyle=\int_{\Phi(\mathsf{\Omega})}\prod_{j=1}^{k}\rho_{\sigma_{0}^{2},m_{j}}(x_{j})\,\prod_{j=1}^{k}{d}{x}_{j}=\int_{\Omega}\prod_{j=1}^{k}\rho_{\sigma_{0}^{2},m_{j}}(x_{j}+\alpha(k-j)+\beta)\,\prod_{j=1}^{k}{d}{x}_{j}.

Thus

\displaystyle\frac{\mathbb{P}_{0}(\Phi(\mathsf{J}))}{\mathbb{P}_{0}(\mathsf{J})}\geq

\displaystyle\inf_{\Omega}\prod_{j=1}^{k}\frac{\rho_{\sigma_{0}^{2},m_{j}}(x_{j}+\alpha(k-j)+\beta)}{\rho_{\sigma_{0}^{2},m_{j}}(x_{j})}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\inf_{\Omega}\prod_{j=1}^{k}\exp\left(-\sigma_{0}^{-2}\left[(x_{j}-m_{j})(\alpha(k-j)+\beta)+2^{-1}(\alpha(k-j)+\beta)^{2}\right]\right).

For
(x_{1},x_{2},\dots,x_{k})\in\Omega
and
C_{4}\geq 2(b_{2}+C_{2})
, we have
x_{j}-m_{j}\leq 2C_{4}T^{2}
. Together with
\sigma_{0}^{-2}\leq 2T^{-1/2}
, the above is bounded from below by
C^{-1}e^{-CC_{4}T^{5/2}}
for some large constant
C
.
As a result,

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}_{0}(\mathsf{G}^{\textup{c}}\cap\{B_{1}(p_{0})\leq M\}|\mathsf{Q})\leq\frac{1}{1+C^{-1}e^{-CC_{4}T^{5/2}}}\leq 1-C^{-1}e^{-CC_{4}T^{5/2}}.

∎

We are ready to prove Lemma
5.7
.

Proof of Lemma
5.7
.

From Lemma
5.9
,

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}_{\mathcal{L}^{\prime}_{+}}(\mathsf{G})\geq\mathbb{P}_{\mathcal{L}^{\prime}_{+}}(\mathsf{G}\cap\mathsf{Gap})=\mathbb{P}_{\mathcal{L}^{\prime}_{+}}(\mathsf{G}|\mathsf{Gap})\times\mathbb{P}_{\mathcal{L}^{\prime}_{+}}(\mathsf{Gap})\geq 2^{-1}\mathbb{P}_{\mathcal{L}^{\prime}_{+}}(\mathsf{G}|\mathsf{Gap}).

From (
5.5
), Lemmas
5.10
,
5.11
and
5.12
, we have

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}_{\mathcal{L}^{\prime}_{+}}(\mathsf{G}|\mathsf{Gap})=

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}_{0}(\mathsf{G}|\mathsf{Q})\geq\mathbb{P}_{0}(\mathsf{G}\cap\{B_{1}(p_{0})\leq C_{4}T^{2}\}|\mathsf{Q})

\displaystyle\geq

\displaystyle 1-\mathbb{P}_{0}(\mathsf{G}^{\textup{c}}\cap\{B_{1}(p_{0})\leq C_{4}T^{2}\}|\mathsf{Q})-\mathbb{P}_{0}(B_{1}(p_{0})>C_{1}T^{2}|\mathsf{Q})

\displaystyle\geq

\displaystyle 1-(1-C_{6}e^{-C_{6}^{-1}C_{4}T^{5/2}})-C_{3}C_{5}e^{-\sigma_{0}^{2}T^{4}(C_{5}^{-1}C_{4}^{2}-C_{3})}.

Requiring
C_{4}^{2}\geq 2^{-1}C_{3}C_{5}
and using
\sigma_{0}^{-2}\geq 2b_{0}^{-1}T^{-1}
, we have

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}_{\mathcal{L}^{\prime}_{+}}(\mathsf{G}|\mathsf{Gap})\geq C_{6}^{-1}e^{-C_{6}C_{4}T^{5/2}}-C_{3}C_{5}e^{-b_{0}^{-1}C_{5}^{-1}C_{4}^{2}T^{3}}.

Further requiring
C_{4}
to be large enough, we can obtain
2^{-1}C_{6}^{-1}e^{-C_{6}C_{4}T^{5/2}}\geq C_{3}C_{5}e^{-b_{0}^{-1}C_{5}^{-1}C_{4}^{2}T^{3}}
and thus

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}_{\mathcal{L}^{\prime}_{+}}(\mathsf{G}|\mathsf{Gap})\geq 2^{-1}C_{6}^{-1}e^{-C_{6}C_{4}T^{5/2}}.

For concreteness, taking

\displaystyle C_{4}\coloneqq\max\{2(b_{2}+C_{2}),2^{-1/2}C_{3}^{1/2}C_{5}^{1/2},b_{0}C_{5}(C_{6}+1),\log(2C_{3}C_{5}C_{6})\},

We have

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}_{\mathcal{L}^{\prime}_{+}}(\mathsf{G})\geq 4^{-1}C_{6}^{-1}e^{-C_{6}C_{4}T^{5/2}}.

The proof is finished.
∎

6.
The ”soft” Jump ensemble

In this section, we introduce the jump ensemble
J
. Inspired by the jump ensemble
[
Ham1
]
,
J
is the intermediary between the scaled KPZ line ensemble
\mathfrak{H}^{t}
and the Brownian bridge ensemble. We design
J
to be adapted to the intersecting nature of
\mathfrak{H}^{t}
; moreover
J
is built to meet the following requirements:

(1)

The scaled KPZ line ensemble
\mathfrak{H}^{t}
is well represented by
J
.

(2)

J
is comparable to Brownian bridge ensemble on
[0,s]
.

Requirement (1) is realized in Proposition
3.5
and requirement (2) is entailed in Proposition
3.6
. These requirements are competing with each other. We find an appropriate balance between these two. We proceed to explain the design concept of
J
in detail.

Recall that
\textup{dom}(\bar{\ell},\bar{r}),\textup{bdd}(\bar{\ell},\bar{r})
and
\textup{ext}(\bar{\ell},\bar{r})
are regions in
\mathbb{N}\times\mathbb{R}
defined in Section
3
. We run Gibbs resampling for
\mathfrak{H}^{t}
in
\textup{dom}(\bar{\ell},\bar{r})
. The law of
\mathfrak{H}_{t}|_{\textup{dom}(\bar{\ell},\bar{r})}
is then given by
\mathbb{P}^{\textup{dom}(\bar{\ell},\bar{r}),f}_{\mathbf{H}_{t}}
with
f=\mathfrak{H}^{t}|_{\textup{bdd}(\bar{\ell},\bar{r})}
. More precisely,
\mathbb{P}^{\textup{dom}(\bar{\ell},\bar{r}),f}_{\mathbf{H}_{t}}
is specified via the following Radon-Nikodym derivative relation,

\frac{{d}\mathbb{P}^{\textup{dom}(\bar{\ell},\bar{r}),f}_{\mathbf{H}_{t}}}{{d}\mathbb{P}^{\textup{dom}(\bar{\ell},\bar{r}),f}_{\textup{free}}}(\mathcal{L})=\frac{W^{\textup{dom}(\bar{\ell},\bar{r}),f}_{\mathbf{H}_{t}}(\mathcal{L})}{\mathbb{E}^{\textup{dom}(\bar{\ell},\bar{r}),f}_{\textup{free}}\left[W^{\textup{dom}(\bar{\ell},\bar{r}),f}_{\mathbf{H}_{t}}\right]}.

Here
\mathbb{P}^{\textup{dom}(\bar{\ell},\bar{r}),f}_{\textup{free}}
is the law of independent Brownian bridges in
\textup{dom}(\bar{\ell},\bar{r})
. The Boltzmann weight
W^{\textup{dom}(\bar{\ell},\bar{r}),f}_{\mathbf{H}_{t}}
is defined in (
3.8
) as

\begin{split}W_{\mathbf{H}_{t}}^{\textup{dom}(\bar{\ell},\bar{r}),f}(\mathcal{L})=&\prod_{j=1}^{k-1}\exp\left(-\int_{[\ell_{j},\ell_{j+1}]\cup[r_{j+1},r_{j}]}\mathbf{H}_{t}(f_{j+1}(x)-\mathcal{L}_{j}(x))\,dx\right)\\
&\times\prod_{j=1}^{k-1}\exp\left(-\int_{[\ell_{j+1},r_{j+1}]}\mathbf{H}_{t}(\mathcal{L}_{j+1}(x)-\mathcal{L}_{j}(x))\,dx\right)\\
&\times\exp\left(-\int_{[\ell_{k},r_{k}]}\mathbf{H}_{t}(f_{k+1}(x)-\mathcal{L}_{k}(x))\,dx\right).\end{split}

The jump ensemble
J
is obtained by replacing
W^{\textup{dom}(\bar{\ell},\bar{r}),f}_{\mathbf{H}_{t}}
with a new weight
W_{\textup{jump}}
. In order to achieve the requirement (2),
W_{\textup{jump}}
is designed in the way that

(i)

The interaction in
W_{\textup{jump}}
is only activated near a finite subset of
\mathbb{R}
, (the Pole set).

(ii)

There is no self-interaction within
J
. The interaction is only present between curves in
J
and the boundary data
f
.

In order to achieve (1), the location where we activate the interaction in
W_{\textup{jump}}
needs to be carefully chosen. Our choice is inspired by the following crucial observation of Hammond
[
Ham1
]
.

If a function
g(x)
is close to a parabola
-2^{-1}x^{2}
, its concave majorant
\mathfrak{c}(x)
is also close to
-2^{-1}x^{2}
with derivative close to
-x
, see Lemma
A.8
. At extreme points of
\mathfrak{c}(x)
,
\mathfrak{c}
agrees with
g
. Let
P
a discrete subset of extreme points of
\mathfrak{c}(x)
. A linear interpolation between points in
P
, denoted by Tent
(x)
, gives a good approximation to
\mathfrak{c}
, see Lemma
A.4
. As a result, a Brownian bridge conditioned on jumping over
g(x)
in an interval is well-approximated by a Brownian bridge conditioned on jumping over
g(x)
only at points in
P
. See Figure
3
for an illustration.

Figure 3.
Illustration of the Tent as a good approximation to the concave majorant
\mathfrak{c}(x)
.

There is an extra difficulty. In the current context, different indexed jump curves
J_{j}
travel in different intervals, e.g.
({\ell}_{j},{r}_{j})
for
J_{i}
. Near
x=\ell_{j}
, we need to have Tent
(x)
, up to a controllable error, bounded from above by
f_{j}(\ell_{j})
. This scenario allows us to raise
J_{j}
above Tent
(x)
within a short amount of travel time. Therefore, we need to construct multiple Pole sets
P_{j}
and multiple tent maps
\textup{Tent}_{j}(x)
, one for each
J_{j}
.

In subsection
6.1
we list essential properties of
P_{j+1}
needed in the rest of the paper. The construction of
P_{j+1}
and the verification of those properties are postponed to subsection
6.2
. Skipping subsection
6.2
will not affect the reader’s understanding for the rest of the paper. The construction of
W_{\textup{jump}}
and the jump ensemble
J
are done in subsection
6.3
.

6.1.
Pole sets

Throughout this section, we fix
(\bar{\ell},\bar{r},f_{\textup{J}})\in\mathcal{G}
. Denote
\bar{\ell}=(\ell_{1},\ell_{2}\dots,\ell_{k})
,
\bar{r}=(r_{1},r_{2}\dots,r_{k})
and
f_{\textup{J}}=(f_{1},f_{2}\dots,f_{k+1})
.

Define
\underline{g}(x)
by

(6.1)

\begin{split}\underline{g}(x)\coloneqq\left\{\begin{array}[]{cc}f_{2}(x)&x\in[\ell_{0},{\ell}_{2})\cup({r}_{2},r_{0}],\\
f_{i+1}(x)&x\in({\ell}_{i},{\ell}_{i+1})\cup({r}_{i+1},{r}_{i}),\ i\in[2,k-1]_{\mathbb{Z}},\\
f_{k+1}(x)&x\in({\ell}_{k},{r}_{k}),\\
\max\{f_{i}(x),f_{i+1}(x)\}&x\in\{{\ell}_{i},{r}_{i}\},\ i\in[2,k]_{\mathbb{Z}}.\end{array}\right.\end{split}

The function
\underline{g}
consists of parts in
f_{\textup{J}}
that interact
\mathfrak{H}
. At
x=\ell_{j}
or
x=r_{j}
, the value of
\underline{g}(x)
is chosen such that
\underline{g}(x)
is upper semi-continuous. See Figure
4
for an illustration.

Figure 4.
An illustration of
\underline{g}(x)
when
k=3
. Recall that the resampling domain resembles an upside-down pyramid and the the resampled curves only interact with values on the boundary of the resampling domain, i.e.
f_{2}
on
[\ell_{1},\ell_{2}]
,
f_{3}
on
[\ell_{2},\ell_{3}]
,
f_{3}
on
[\ell_{3},\ell_{4}]
.

For all
j\in[1,k]_{\mathbb{Z}}
and
x\in(\ell_{j},r_{j})
, we have

(6.2)

\begin{split}\underline{g}(x)\leq&f_{j+1}(\ell_{j})+(k+1-j)T(x-\ell_{j}),\\
\underline{g}(x)\leq&f_{j+1}(r_{j})+(k+1-j)T(r_{j}-x).\end{split}

See Figure
5
for an illustration. The proof can be found in the next subsection.

Figure 5.
This picture illustrates (
6.2
) near
\ell_{j}
. Note that for
x\in(\ell_{j},\ell_{j+1}),\ \underline{g}(x)=f_{j+1}(x)
.

In the next subsection, we will construct Pole sets
P_{j+1}
for
j\in[1,k]_{\mathbb{Z}}
.
P_{j+1}
consists of some extreme points of a concave function which captures
f_{j+1}
well near
x=\ell_{j}
and
x=r_{j}
.
P_{j+1}
satisfies the following requirements.

P_{j+1}
is a subset of
[\ell_{j},r_{j}]
which includes
\ell_{j}
and
r_{j}
. The number of pole points in
P_{j+1}
is at most of order
T
.

(6.3)

\displaystyle P_{j+1}\subset[\ell_{j},r_{j}],\ \ell_{j},r_{j}\in P_{j+1}\ \textup{and}\ |P_{j+1}|\leq|r_{j}-\ell_{j}|+1.

We require different indexed
P_{j+1}
agree each other in
(-2^{-1}T,2^{-1}T)
. For
i,j\in[1,k]_{\mathbb{Z}}
,

(6.4)

\displaystyle P_{i+1}\cap(-2^{-1}T,2^{-1}T)=P_{j+1}\cap(-2^{-1}T,2^{-1}T).

In order to make
J
close to Brownian bridges in the interval
[0,s]
, the number of pole points in
[0,s]
is limited to
1
. Suppose
p_{0}\in P_{j+1}\cap[0,s]
. Let
p_{-}=\max\{p\in P_{j+1}\,|\,p<p_{0}\}
and
p_{+}=\min\{p\in P_{j+1}\,|\,p>p_{0}\}
be the elements in
P_{j+1}
next to
p_{0}
. We require that

(6.5)

\displaystyle s\leq|p_{0}-p_{\pm}|<4^{-1}T.

Near
\ell_{j}
and
r_{j}
, we need to keep the full interaction
-\mathbf{H}_{t}(f_{j+1}(x)-\mathcal{L}_{j}(x))
unchanged in
W_{\textup{jump}}
. See subsection
6.3
for more explanation. We want to make sure this full interaction is not interrupted by the pole points. To do so, we require
P_{j+1}
to avoid
\ell_{i}
and
r_{i}
. Define

(6.6)

d^{\prime}\coloneqq T^{-3/2}.

d^{\prime}
determines the width of intervals near
\ell_{j}
and
r_{j}
in which
-\mathbf{H}_{t}(f_{j+1}(x)-\mathcal{L}_{j}(x))
is preserved in
W_{\textup{jump}}
. We will explain the choice of scaling in more detail in Section
7
. For all
j\in[1,k]_{\mathbb{Z}}
and
i\in[j,k]_{\mathbb{Z}}
, we ask

(6.7)

\displaystyle P_{j+1}\cap(\ell_{i},\ell_{i}+2d^{\prime})=P_{j+1}\cap(r_{i}-2d^{\prime},r_{i})=\phi.

Define the tent maps

(6.8)

\textup{Tent}_{j+1}(x)\coloneqq\left\{\begin{array}[]{cc}f_{j+1}(x)&x=\ell_{j}\ \textup{or}\ x=r_{j},\\
\underline{g}(x)&x\in P_{j+1}\setminus\{\ell_{j},r_{j}\},\\
\textup{linear inteperlation}&x\in[\ell_{j},r_{j}]\setminus P_{j+1}.\end{array}\right.

We need
\textup{Tent}_{j+1}(x)
to be a good approximation of
\underline{g}(x)
in the following sense. For all
x\in(\ell_{j},r_{j})
,

(6.9)

\textup{Tent}_{j+1}(x)\geq\underline{g}(x)-3(k+1-j)sT.

Moreover, we require that the slope of
\textup{Tent}_{j+1}(x)
is of order
T
.

(6.10)

\sup_{x\neq y\in[\ell_{j},r_{j}]}\left|\frac{\textup{Tent}_{j+1}(x)-\textup{Tent}_{j+1}(y)}{x-y}\right|\leq 2(k+1-j)T.

The construction of
P_{j+1}
and the verification of above properties can be found in the next subsection.

6.2.
Construction of pole sets

For
j\in[1,k]_{\mathbb{Z}}
, define

(6.11)

\begin{split}g_{j+1}(x)\coloneqq\left\{\begin{array}[]{cc}f_{j+1}(x)&x\in[\ell_{0},{\ell}_{j}]\cup[{r}_{j},r_{0}],\\
\underline{g}(x)&x\in(\ell_{j},r_{j}).\end{array}\right.\end{split}

Let
\mathfrak{c}_{j+1}
be the concave majorant of
g_{j+1}
in
[\ell_{0},r_{0}]
.

\displaystyle\mathfrak{c}_{j+1}(x_{0})\coloneqq\inf\{ax_{0}+b\ |\ ax+b\geq g_{j+1}(x)\ \textup{for all}\ x\in[\ell_{0},r_{0}]\}.

Define

\begin{split}\textup{xExt}_{j+1}\coloneqq&\left\{x\in(\ell_{0},r_{0})\ |\ \mathfrak{c}_{j+1}\ \textup{is not linear near }\ x\right\}\cup\{\ell_{0},r_{0}\}.\end{split}

The reason to use
g_{j+1}(x)
instead of
\underline{g}(x)
to construct the concave majorant is to obtain a better fit to
f_{j+1}
near
x=\ell_{j}
and
x=r_{j}
. Especially, we have the following lemma.

Lemma 6.1
.

For all
j\in[1,k]_{\mathbb{Z}}
,

\ell_{j},r_{j}\in\textup{xExt}_{j+1}
and (
6.2
) holds.

Proof.

From Condition
C1
and the construction of
(\mathbf{l},\mathbf{r})
in Section
4
, we have

\displaystyle|\ell_{j}+(k-j+1)T|\leq 5^{-1}T,|r_{j}-(k-j+1)T|\leq 5^{-1}T.

From (
6.11
),
g_{j+1}
and
f_{j+1}
agree on
R_{j+1,\pm}=[\pm(k+1-j)T-2^{-1}T,\pm(k+1-j)T+2^{-1}T]
. Recall that
\mathfrak{f}_{j+1}
, defined in section
4
, is the concave majorant of
f_{j+1}
on
R_{j+1,\pm}
. By Condition
C2
, we can apply Corollary
A.10
with
\delta=10^{-1}T
to show that
\mathfrak{c}_{j+1}
and
\mathfrak{f}_{j+1}
agree on
\bar{R}_{j+1,\pm}=[\pm(k+1-j)T-5^{-1}T,\pm(k+1-j)T+5^{-1}T]
. In particular,
\ell_{j}
and
r_{j}
are extreme points of
\mathfrak{c}_{j}
. See Lemmas
A.5
and
A.8
.

Next, we prove (
6.2
). From Lemma
A.7
and (
6.11
),

\mathfrak{c}_{j+1}(\ell_{j})=g_{j+1}(\ell_{j})=f_{j+1}(\ell_{j}).

Moreover, Condition
C2
and Lemma
A.8
imply

\displaystyle|\ell_{j}+(k+1-j)T|\leq 10^{-1}T,|r_{j}-(k+1-j)T|\leq 10^{-1}T.

In particular,
(\mathfrak{c}^{\prime}_{j+1})_{+}(\ell_{j})\leq(k+1-j)T
. By the concavity of
\mathfrak{c}_{j+1}
, we have for all
x\in(\ell_{j},r_{j})

\displaystyle\underline{g}(x)=

\displaystyle g_{j+1}(x)\leq\mathfrak{c}_{j+1}(x)

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\mathfrak{c}_{j+1}(\ell_{j})+(k+1-j)T(x-\ell_{j})

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle f_{j+1}(\ell_{j})+(k+1-j)T(x-\ell_{j}).

This proves the first part of (
6.2
). The other part can be proved similarly.
∎

Remark 6.2
.

From Condition
C2
and Corollary
A.10
,
\mathfrak{c}_{j+1}(x)
agrees each other on
[-2^{-1}T,2^{-1}T]
for all
j\in[1,k]_{\mathbb{Z}}
. Hence
\textup{xExt}_{j+1}\cap(-2^{-1}T,2^{-1}T)
are also the same.

For
j\in[1,k]_{\mathbb{Z}}
, let
\mathring{P}_{j+1}\subset\textup{xExt}_{j+1}\cap[\ell_{j},r_{j}]
be a subset satisfying the following properties.

•

\mathring{P}_{j+1}\cap(-2^{-1}T,2^{-1}T)
are identical for all
j\in[1,k]_{\mathbb{Z}}
.

•

\ell_{j},r_{j}\in\mathring{P}_{j+1}

•

For any
x_{1}\neq x_{2}\in\mathring{P}_{j+1}
,
|x_{1}-x_{2}|\geq s

•

For any
y\in\textup{xExt}_{j+1}\cap[\ell_{j},r_{j}]
, there exists
x\in\mathring{P}_{j+1}
such that
|x-y|<s
.

The sets
\mathring{P}_{j+1}
satisfy the conditions (
6.3
), (
6.4
) and (
6.5
). To get (
6.7
), we need further modification. Let

\displaystyle A_{j+1}^{-}\coloneqq\{i\in[j+1,k]_{\mathbb{Z}}\,|\,P_{j+1}\cap(\ell_{i},\ell_{i}+2d^{\prime})\neq\phi\},

\displaystyle A_{j+1}^{+}\coloneqq\{i\in[j+1,k]_{\mathbb{Z}}\,|\,P_{j+1}\cap(r_{i}-2d^{\prime},r_{i})\neq\phi\}.

The sets
A_{j+1}^{\pm}
record the indices of
i
for which
P_{j+1}
has a pole point near
x=\ell_{i}
or
x=r_{i}
respectively. If
i\in A_{j+1}^{-}
, we replace the pole points in
\mathring{P}_{j+1}\cap(\ell_{i},\ell_{i}+2d^{\prime})
by
\ell_{i}
. The same replacement is also done for
i\in A^{+}_{j+1}
. Concretely, we define the Pole set by

(6.12)

\begin{split}{P}_{j+1}\coloneqq&\mathring{P}_{j+1}\cup\{\ell_{i}\,|\,i\in A^{-}_{j+1}\}\cup\{r_{i}\,|\,i\in A^{+}_{j+1}\}\setminus\bigcup_{i=j+1}^{k}\bigg((\ell_{i},\ell_{i}+2d^{\prime})\cup(r_{i}-2d^{\prime},r_{i})\bigg).\end{split}

By the construction, (
6.7
) holds.

From the view of Lemma
A.8
and condition
C2
, two consecutive elements in
\textup{xExt}_{j+1}
have distance less than
10^{-1}T
. Together with
T\geq 10
, (
6.5
) holds.

Next, we aim to show (
6.9
) and (
6.10
). Define the tent map from
\mathring{P}_{j+1}
as

\mathring{\textup{T}}\textup{ent}_{j+1}(x)\coloneqq\left\{\begin{array}[]{cc}f_{j+1}(x)&x=\ell_{j}\ \textup{or}\ x=r_{j},\\
\underline{g}(x)&x\in\mathring{P}_{j+1}\setminus\{\ell_{j},r_{j}\},\\
\textup{linear inteperlation}&x\in[\ell_{j},r_{j}]\setminus\mathring{P}_{j+1}.\end{array}\right.

Because
|(\mathfrak{c}_{j+1})^{\prime}_{\pm}(x)|\leq(k+1-j)T
for
x\in[\ell_{j},r_{j}]
, we have from Lemma
A.4
,

\displaystyle\mathring{\textup{T}}\textup{ent}_{j+1}(x)\geq

\displaystyle\mathfrak{c}_{j+1}(x)-2(k+1-j)sT\geq g_{j+1}(x)-2(k+1-j)sT.

In particular, for
x\in(\ell_{j},r_{j})
, we have

\displaystyle\mathring{\textup{T}}\textup{ent}_{j+1}(x)\geq\underline{g}(x)-2(k+1-j)sT.

Moreover,

\sup_{x\neq y\in[\ell_{j},r_{j}]}\left|\frac{\mathring{\textup{T}}\textup{ent}_{j+1}(x)-\mathring{\textup{T}}\textup{ent}_{j+1}(y)}{x-y}\right|\leq(k+1-j)T.

The strategy of proving (
6.9
) and (
6.10
) is to compare
{\textup{T}}\textup{ent}_{j+1}
with
\mathring{\textup{T}}\textup{ent}_{j+1}
using Lemma
B.3
.

Lemma 6.3
.

Suppose
p\in\mathring{P}_{j+1}\cap(\ell_{i},\ell_{i}+2d^{\prime}]
for some
j\in[1,k]_{\mathbb{Z}}
and
i\in[j+1,k]_{\mathbb{Z}}
. We have

|\underline{g}(\ell_{i})-\underline{g}(p)|\leq 2(k+1-j)T^{-1/2}.

Similarly, suppose
p\in\mathring{P}_{j}\cap[r_{i}-2d^{\prime},r_{i})
for some
j\in[1,k]_{\mathbb{Z}}
and
i\in[j+1,k]_{\mathbb{Z}}
. Then

|\underline{g}(r_{i})-\underline{g}(p)|\leq 2(k+1-j)T^{-1/2}.

We postpone the proof of Lemma
6.3
and show (
6.9
) and (
6.10
) first. For (
6.9
), it remains to prove

\displaystyle\textup{Tent}_{j+1}(x)\geq\mathring{\textup{T}}\textup{ent}_{j+1}(x)-(k+1-j)sT.

Note that
\textup{Tent}_{j+1}(x)
agrees with
\mathring{\textup{T}}\textup{ent}_{j+1}(x)
except for
x
near
\ell_{i}
and
r_{i}
.

We give the proof for the case
x
close to
\ell_{i}
. We may assume that
\mathring{P}_{j}\cap(\ell_{i},\ell_{i}+2d^{\prime})\neq\phi
. Otherwise,
\textup{Tent}_{j+1}(x)=\mathring{\textup{T}}\textup{ent}_{j+1}(x)
near
\ell_{i}
. Let
p=\mathring{P}_{j}\cap(\ell_{i},\ell_{i}+2d^{\prime})
,
q=\ell_{i}
,
p_{-}<p<p_{+}
be the elements in
\mathring{P}_{j+1}
next to
p
. In the interval
[p_{-},p_{+}]
, the tent maps
\mathring{\textup{T}}\textup{ent}_{j+1}(x)
and
\mathring{\textup{T}}\textup{ent}_{j+1}(x)
are piecewise linear functions given by

\mathring{\textup{T}}\textup{ent}_{j+1}(x)=\left\{\begin{array}[]{cc}\underline{g}(x)&x=p\ \textup{or}\ x=p_{\pm},\\
\textup{linear inteperlation}&x\in(p_{-},p)\cup(p,p_{+}).\end{array}\right.

{\textup{T}}\textup{ent}_{j+1}(x)=\left\{\begin{array}[]{cc}\underline{g}(x)&x=q\ \textup{or}\ x=p_{\pm},\\
\textup{linear inteperlation}&x\in(p_{-},q)\cup(q,p_{+}).\end{array}\right.

To compare
\mathring{\textup{T}}\textup{ent}_{j+1}(x)
and
{\textup{T}}\textup{ent}_{j+1}(x)
, we apply Lemma
B.3
. From Lemma
6.3
,

\displaystyle|\underline{g}(\ell_{i})-\underline{g}(p)|\leq 2(k+1-j)T^{-1/2}.

Note that
|p-q|\leq 2d^{\prime}=2T^{-3/2}
and
|p_{\pm}-p|\geq s\geq 1
. Applying Lemma
B.3
yields

\displaystyle\textup{Tent}_{j+1}(x)-\mathring{\textup{T}}\textup{ent}_{j+1}(x)\geq-2(k+1-j)T^{-1/2}-2T^{-3/2}\max\{|\mathring{m}_{-}|,|\mathring{m}_{+}|\}.

Here
\mathring{m}_{\pm}
is the slope of
\mathring{\textup{T}}\textup{ent}_{j+1}
in
[p_{-},p]
and
[p,p_{+}]
. Because the slope of
\mathring{\textup{T}}\textup{ent}_{j+1}
is bounded by
(k+1-j)T
, we get

\textup{Tent}_{j+1}(x)\geq\mathring{\textup{T}}\textup{ent}_{j+1}(x)-4(k+1-j)T^{-1/2}.

The proof of (
6.9
) near
\ell_{i}
is finished.

Next, we turn to (
6.10
). We again apply Lemma
B.3
. The difference between the slopes of
\mathring{\textup{T}}\textup{ent}_{j+1}
and
{\textup{T}}\textup{ent}_{j+1}
is bounded by
8(k+1-j)T^{-1/2}.
Therefore, (
6.10
) holds true. The argument for the case near
r_{i}
is similar.

Proof of Lemma
6.3
.

By symmetry, it suffices to prove the case
p\in\mathring{P}_{j+1}\cap(\ell_{i},\ell_{i}+2d^{\prime})
. We start by showing
\underline{g}(\ell_{i})\leq\underline{g}(p)
. By the concavity of
\mathfrak{c}_{j+1}
,

\displaystyle\underline{g}(\ell_{i})=

\displaystyle g_{j+1}(\ell_{i})\leq\mathfrak{c}_{j+1}(\ell_{i})\leq\mathfrak{c}_{j+1}(p)+(\mathfrak{c}^{\prime}_{j+1})_{+}(p)(\ell_{i}-p).

Because
p
is an extreme point of
\mathfrak{c}_{j+1}
,

\displaystyle\mathfrak{c}_{j+1}(p)+(\mathfrak{c}^{\prime}_{j+1})_{+}(p)(\ell_{i}-p)=g_{j+1}(p)-(\mathfrak{c}^{\prime}_{j+1})_{+}(p)(p-\ell_{i}).

From the definitions of
\underline{g}
and and
g_{j+1}
, see (
6.1
) and (
6.11
),

\displaystyle g_{j+1}(p)-(\mathfrak{c}^{\prime}_{j+1})_{+}(p)(p-\ell_{i})=

\displaystyle\underline{g}(p)-(\mathfrak{c}^{\prime}_{j+1})_{+}(p)(p-\ell_{i}).

By Lemma
A.8
,
(\mathfrak{c}^{\prime}_{j+1})_{+}(p)\geq-p-10^{-1}T\geq 0
. We conclude
\underline{g}(\ell_{i})\leq\underline{g}(p)
.

Next, we show that
\underline{g}(p)\leq\underline{g}(\ell_{i})+2(k+1-j)T^{-1/2}.
Because
p\in(\ell_{i},\ell_{i}+2d^{\prime})
,

\displaystyle\underline{g}(p)=f_{i+1}(p)=g_{i+1}(p).

By the concavity of
\mathfrak{c}_{i+1}
,

\displaystyle g_{i+1}(p)\leq\mathfrak{c}_{i+1}(p)\leq\mathfrak{c}_{i+1}(\ell_{i})+(\mathfrak{c}_{i+1}^{\prime})_{+}(\ell_{i})(p-\ell_{i}).

Because
\ell_{i}
is an extreme point of
\mathfrak{c}_{i+1}
, it holds that

\mathfrak{c}_{i+1}(\ell_{i})=g_{i+1}(\ell_{i})=f_{i+1}(\ell_{i})\leq\underline{g}(\ell_{i}).

From Lemma
A.8
, we have

\mathfrak{c}_{i+1}^{\prime}(\ell_{i})\leq-\ell_{i}+10^{-1}T\leq(k+2-i)T\leq(k+1-j)T.

Together with
p-\ell_{i}\leq 2T^{-3/2}
, we conclude that

\displaystyle\underline{g}(p)\leq\underline{g}(\ell_{i})+2(k+1-j)T^{-1/2}.

∎

6.3.
Jump ensemble

In this subsection, we use the Pole set
P_{j+1}
to construct the jump ensemble. We put all
P_{j+1}
together to form the joint Pole set.

(6.13)

P^{*}\coloneqq\bigcup_{j=1}^{k}P_{j+1}.

Recall that
d=64T^{-4}(\log T)^{2}
. For
j\in[1,k-1]_{\mathbb{Z}}
, define

\textup{Int}_{j}\coloneqq\bigg(\bigcup_{p\in P^{*}\cap(\ell_{j},\ell_{j+1}]}[p-d,p]\bigg)\cup\bigg(\bigcup_{p\in P^{*}\cap[r_{j+1},r_{j})}[p,p+d]\bigg)

and

\textup{Int}_{k}\coloneqq\bigg(\bigcup_{p\in P^{*}\cap(\ell_{k},0)}[p-d,p]\bigg)\cup\bigg(\bigcup_{p\in P^{*}\cap[0,r_{k})}[p,p+d]\bigg)

From (
6.7
), we have
\textup{Int}_{j}\subset(\ell_{j},\ell_{j+1}]\cup[r_{j+1},r_{j})
for
j\in[1,k-1]_{\mathbb{Z}}
and
\textup{Int}_{k}\subset(\ell_{k},r_{k})
.
\textup{Int}_{j}
and
\textup{Int}_{k}
indicates the region where
J
interacts with the boundary. The index
j
in
\textup{Int}_{j}
records the layer of the boundary data. For instance, in region
\textup{Int}_{j}
, the lower boundary is given by
f_{j+1}
.

Figure 6.
This picture illustrates
W^{j}_{\textup{jump}}
near
\ell_{j}
. In the thick pillar with width
d^{\prime}
, we use the Hamiltonian
\textbf{H}_{t}
as in the scaled KPZ line ensemble. In the thin pillars with width
d
, a weaker Hamiltonian
\textbf{H}_{t}^{j,j+1}
is used. There is no interaction on other places.

Now we are ready to define the jump weight
W_{\textup{jump}}
. Recall that we fix the triple
(\bar{\ell},\bar{r},f_{\textup{J}})\in\mathcal{G}
. Fix
j\in[1,k]_{\mathbb{Z}}
. Let

\begin{split}W^{j}_{\textup{jump}}(\mathcal{L}_{j};f|_{\textup{J}})\coloneqq&\exp\left(-\int_{[\ell_{j},\ell_{j}+d^{\prime}]\cup[r_{j}-d^{\prime},r_{j}]}\mathbf{H}_{t}(\underline{g}(x)-\mathcal{L}_{j}(x))dx\right)\\
&\times\exp\left(-\sum_{i=j}^{k}\int_{\textup{Int}_{i}}\mathbf{H}_{t}^{j,i+1}(\underline{g}(x)-\mathcal{L}_{j}(x))dx\right).\end{split}

Here
\underline{g}(x)
is the lower boundary defined in (
6.1
) and
\mathbf{H}_{t}^{i,{j+1}}(x)
is the Hamiltonian defined in (
B.1
).
\mathbf{H}_{t}^{i,{j+1}}(x)
is chosen to guarantee Lemma
6.4
.

For any
\ell<r
, we define the truncated version

(6.14)

\begin{split}W^{j,[\ell,r]}_{\textup{jump}}(\mathcal{L}_{j};f|_{\textup{J}})\coloneqq&\exp\left(-\int_{([\ell_{j},\ell_{j}+d^{\prime}]\cup[r_{j}-d^{\prime},r_{j}])\cap[\ell,r]}\mathbf{H}_{t}(\underline{g}(x)-\mathcal{L}_{j}(x))dx\right)\\
&\times\exp\left(-\sum_{i=j}^{k}\int_{\textup{Int}_{i}\cap[\ell,r]}\mathbf{H}_{t}^{j,i+1}(\underline{g}(x)-\mathcal{L}_{j}(x))dx\right).\end{split}

The jump weight
W_{\textup{jump}}
is defined by

(6.15)

\displaystyle W_{\textup{jump}}(\mathcal{L};f_{\textup{J}})\coloneqq

\displaystyle\prod_{j=1}^{k}W^{j}_{\textup{jump}}(\mathcal{L}_{j};f_{\textup{J}}).

In
W_{\textup{jump}}
, the random curves don’t interact with each other. In particular, the jump curves
J_{i}
and
J_{j}
, defined in (
3.13
), are independent of each other if
i\neq j
.

The
j
-th jump curve
J_{j}
interacts with
\underline{g}
near
\ell_{j}
and
r_{j}
through
\mathbf{H}_{t}
. This is the same Hamiltonian in
W_{\mathbf{H}_{t}}^{\textup{dom}(\bar{\ell},\bar{r}),f}
. See (
3.8
). The reason to keep the Hamiltonian function comes from the possibility that
f_{\textup{J}}
may go out of order. If
f_{j+1}>f_{j}
near
\ell_{j}
, the interaction in
-\mathbf{H}_{t}(f_{j+1}(x)-\mathcal{L}_{j}(x))
near
\ell_{j}
becomes so drastic such that any mollification would make a huge difference. Thus we preserve the original Hamiltonian.

The other location where
J_{j}
interacts with
\underline{g}(x)
is determined by the Pole set
P^{*}
, which depends on
f_{\textup{J}}
.

Having the jump weight
W_{\textup{jump}}
,
W_{\textup{rest}}
is defined by

(6.16)

\displaystyle W_{\textup{rest}}(\mathcal{L};f_{\textup{J}})\coloneqq

\displaystyle W^{\textup{dom}(\bar{\ell},\bar{r}),f}_{\mathbf{H}_{t}}(\mathcal{L})/W_{\textup{jump}}(\mathcal{L};f_{\textup{J}}).

Here
f=f_{\textup{J}}|_{\textup{bdd}(\bar{\ell},\bar{r})}
.

We finish this section by the following lemma.

Lemma 6.4
.

It holds that

\displaystyle W_{\textup{rest}}(\mathcal{L};f_{\textup{J}})\leq 1.

Proof.

This is equivalent to
W^{\textup{dom}(\bar{\ell},\bar{r}),f}_{\mathbf{H}_{t}}(\mathcal{L})\leq W_{\textup{jump}}(\mathcal{L};f_{\textup{J}})
.
From (
6.1
), we can rewrite
W_{\mathbf{H}_{t}}^{\textup{dom}(\bar{\ell},\bar{r}),f}
in (
3.8
) as

\begin{split}W_{\mathbf{H}_{t}}^{\textup{dom}(\bar{\ell},\bar{r}),f}(\mathcal{L})=&\prod_{j=1}^{k-1}\exp\left(-\int_{[\ell_{j},\ell_{j+1}]\cup[r_{j+1},r_{j}]}\mathbf{H}_{t}(\underline{g}(x)-\mathcal{L}_{j}(x))\,dx\right)\\
&\times\prod_{j=1}^{k-1}\exp\left(-\int_{[\ell_{j+1},r_{j+1}]}\mathbf{H}_{t}(\mathcal{L}_{j+1}(x)-\mathcal{L}_{j}(x))\,dx\right)\\
&\times\exp\left(-\int_{[\ell_{k},r_{k}]}\mathbf{H}_{t}(\underline{g}(x)-\mathcal{L}_{k}(x))\,dx\right).\end{split}

Suppose
x_{0}\in(\ell_{j},\ell_{j+1})
for some
j\in[1,k]_{\mathbb{Z}}
, the integrand of
\log W_{\mathbf{H}_{t}}^{\textup{dom}(\bar{\ell},\bar{r}),f}
at
x_{0}
is

(6.17)

\displaystyle-\mathbf{H}_{t}(\underline{g}(x_{0})-\mathcal{L}_{j}(x_{0}))-\sum_{i=1}^{j-1}\mathbf{H}_{t}(\mathcal{L}_{i+1}(x_{0})-\mathcal{L}_{i}(x_{0})).

We claim that it is smaller than the integrand of
\log W_{\textup{jump}}(\mathcal{L};f_{\textup{J}})
. Suppose
x_{0}\in(\ell_{j},\ell_{j}+d^{\prime}]
. Because of (
6.7
), for any
i\in[1,k]_{\mathbb{Z}}
,

\textup{Int}_{i}\cap(\ell_{j},\ell_{j}+d^{\prime}]=\phi.

Hence the integrand of
\log W_{\textup{jump}}(\mathcal{L};f_{\textup{J}})
at
x_{0}
is
-\mathbf{H}_{t}(\underline{g}(x_{0})-\mathcal{L}_{j}(x_{0})))
, which is larger than (
6.17
). Suppose
x_{0}\in\textup{Int}_{j}
, the integrand of
\log W_{\textup{jump}}(\mathcal{L};f_{\textup{J}})
at
x_{0}
is

\displaystyle-\sum_{i=1}^{j}\mathbf{H}_{t}^{i,j+1}(\underline{g}(x_{0})-\mathcal{L}_{i}(x_{0})).

In light of Lemma
B.2
, it is larger than (
6.17
). Suppose
x_{0}\in(\ell_{j}+d^{\prime},\ell_{j+1})\setminus\textup{Int}_{j}
. Then the integrand of
\log W_{\textup{jump}}(\mathcal{L};f_{\textup{J}})
at
x_{0}
is zero, which is also larger than (
6.17
). This completes the discussion for the case
x_{0}\in(\ell_{j},\ell_{j+1})
. Other cases,
x_{0}\in(r_{j+1},r_{j})
and
x_{0}\in(\ell_{k+1},r_{k+1})
can be treated similarly and we omit the detail. The remaining end points
x_{0}=\ell_{i}
or
x_{0}=r_{i}
have zero contribution. The proof is finished.
∎

7.
Proof of Proposition
3.5

In this section, we aim to show that

\displaystyle\inf\{\mathbb{E}_{J}[W_{\textup{rest}}]\,|\,(\bar{\ell},\bar{r},f_{\textup{J}})\in\mathcal{G}\}\geq D^{-1}e^{-DT^{5/2}}.

Here
\mathcal{G}
is defined in Definition
4.5
and
W_{\textup{rest}}
is defined in (
6.16
). Throughout this section, we fix
(\bar{\ell},\bar{r},f_{\textup{J}})\in\mathcal{G}
. In below we explain the strategy of the proof.

Consider the event
\mathsf{NoTouch}
that requires the jump ensemble
J
to stay ordered and to lie above
\underline{g}
except in neighborhoods near
\ell_{j}
or
r_{j}
. Concretely,

\displaystyle\mathsf{NoTouch}\coloneqq\{J_{j}(x)\geq\underline{g}(x)\ \textup{for}\ j\in[1,k]_{\mathbb{Z}}\ \textup{and}\ x\in[\ell_{j}+d^{\prime},r_{j}-d^{\prime}],

\displaystyle J_{j}(x)\geq J_{j+1}(x)\ \textup{for}\ j\in[1,k-1]_{\mathbb{Z}}\ \textup{and}\ x\in[\ell_{j+1},r_{j+1}]\}.

When
\mathsf{NoTouch}
occurs, the weight
W_{\textup{rest}}
is bounded from below by
e^{-T}
. See Lemma
7.1
. Therefore Proposition
3.5
is reduced to showing
\mathbb{P}_{J}(\mathsf{NoTouch})\geq e^{-T^{5/2}}
.

To begin, we show that each curve
J_{j}
can jump over
\underline{g}
in the interval
[\ell_{j}+d^{\prime},r_{j}-d^{\prime}]
with probability
e^{-T^{5/2}}
. Moreover,a gap of size
T
between
J_{j}
and
\underline{g}
can be created in the interval
[\ell_{j}+1,r_{j}-1]
. This event is denoted by
\mathsf{A}_{j}
.

Next, we apply induction on the number of curves. Take
j\in[2,k]_{\mathbb{Z}}
. We write
\mathsf{F}_{j+1}
for the event that
J_{1},J_{2},\dots,J_{j-1}
stay ordered and that
J_{j-1}\geq\underline{g}+T
in
[\ell_{j-1}+1,r_{j-1}-1]
. Suppose that
\mathbb{P}(\mathsf{F}_{j-1})\geq e^{-T^{5/2}}
. Because the
j
-th curve
J_{j}
is independent of other curves,
\mathbb{P}(\mathsf{F}_{j-1}\cap\mathsf{A}_{j})\geq e^{-T^{5/2}}
. To get bounds on
\mathbb{P}_{J}(\mathsf{F}_{j})
, we need to make sure
J_{j}
does not raise too high and does not touch
J_{j-1}
.

To do so, we apply a stopping domain argument. Consider the leftest and the rightest locations where
J_{j-1}=J_{j}+2^{-1}T
. Denote them by
\mathfrak{l}^{\prime\prime}_{j}
and
\mathfrak{r}^{\prime\prime}_{j}
respectively. At these two points, we know
J_{1}\geq J_{2}\geq\dots\geq J_{j-1}>J_{j}>\underline{g}.
Furthermore,
J_{j-1}-J_{j}=2^{-1}T
and
J_{j}-\underline{g}\geq 2^{-1}T
. Applying the strong
\mathbf{H}_{t}
-Brownian Gibbs property, we resample
J_{1},\dots J_{j}
in
[\mathfrak{l}^{\prime\prime}_{j},\mathfrak{r}^{\prime\prime}_{j}]
. Because the boundary data is now well spaced, we can show that the
J_{1},\dots,J_{j}
, with probability
e^{-T^{5/2}}
, remain ordered and lie beyond
\underline{g}
in
[\mathfrak{l}^{\prime\prime}_{j},\mathfrak{r}^{\prime\prime}_{j}]
. This finishes the proof.

Lemma 7.1
.

There exists
D=D(k,s)
such that for all
t\geq 1
, it holds that

\displaystyle\mathbbm{1}_{\mathsf{NoTouch}}\cdot W_{\textup{rest}}(;f_{\textup{J}})\geq D^{-1}e^{-DT}\cdot\mathbbm{1}_{\mathsf{NoTouch}}.

Proof.

From the definition of
W_{\textup{rest}}
in (
6.16
) and
\mathbf{H}_{t}^{i,j}\geq 0
, we have

\begin{split}W_{\textup{rest}}(\mathcal{L};f_{\textup{J}})\geq&\prod_{j=1}^{k-1}\exp\left(-\int_{[\ell_{j}+d^{\prime},\ell_{j+1}]\cup[r_{j+1},r_{j}-d^{\prime}]}\mathbf{H}_{t}(\underline{g}(x)-\mathcal{L}_{j}(x))\,dx\right)\\
&\times\prod_{j=1}^{k-1}\exp\left(-\int_{[\ell_{j+1},r_{j+1}]}\mathbf{H}_{t}(\mathcal{L}_{j+1}(x)-\mathcal{L}_{j}(x))\,dx\right)\\
&\times\exp\left(-\int_{[\ell_{k}+d^{\prime},r_{k}-d^{\prime}]}\mathbf{H}_{t}(\underline{g}(x)-\mathcal{L}_{k}(x))\,dx\right).\end{split}

When
\mathsf{NoTouch}
occurs, the integrands above are all bounded from below by
-1
. Hence

\displaystyle W_{\textup{rest}}(\mathcal{L};f_{\textup{J}})\geq e^{-k(r_{1}-\ell_{1})}\geq e^{-2k(k+1)T}.

∎

Proposition 7.2
.

There exists a constant
D=D(k,s)
such that for
E
large enough, it holds that

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}_{J}(\mathsf{NoTouch})\geq D^{-1}e^{-DT^{5/2}}.

Proof of Proposition
3.5
.

From Lemma
7.1
and Proposition
7.2

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}_{J}[W_{\textup{rest}}(J;f_{\textup{J}})]\geq\mathbb{P}_{J}(\mathsf{NoTouch})\times D^{-1}e^{-DT}\geq D^{-1}e^{-DT^{5/2}}.

∎

The rest of the section is devoted to prove Proposition
7.2
. In subsection
7.1
, we deal with a single curve
J_{j}
and show that
\mathsf{A}_{j}
occurs with probability at least
e^{-T^{5/2}}
. In subsection
7.2
, we show that, provided the boundary data is well separated,
J
remains ordered and lying beyond
\underline{g}
under resampling. Lastly, we combine the two and finish the induction argument in subsection
7.3
.

7.1.
Single curve

In this subsection, we focus on a single curve
J_{j}
and prove Proposition
7.3
. Roughly speaking, Proposition
7.3
says the probability that
J_{j}
is larger than
\underline{g}
in
[\ell_{j}+d^{\prime},r_{j}-d^{\prime}]
is at least
e^{-T^{5/2}}
.

We start by defining
\mathsf{A}_{j}
. Fix
j\in[1,k]_{\mathbb{Z}}
and let
\mathsf{A}_{j}
be the event that

•

J_{j}(x)>\underline{g}(x)\ \textup{for all}\ x\in[\ell_{j}+d^{\prime},r_{j}-d^{\prime}]
,

•

J_{j}(x)>\textup{Tent}_{j+1}(x)+16k(k+1-j)sT\ \textup{for all}\ x\in[\ell_{j}+1,r_{j}-1]
.

Proposition 7.3
.

There exists
D=D(k)
such that for
E
large enough, we have

\mathbb{P}_{J}(\mathsf{A}_{j})\geq D^{-1}e^{-DT^{5/2}}.

We prove Proposition
7.3
by gradually lift the curve
J_{j}
. In below we define the events which record the lifting process.

Let
\mathsf{A}_{j,1}
be the event such that

•

J_{j}(\ell_{j}+d^{\prime})>f_{j}(\ell_{j})+kT^{1/2},J_{j}(r_{j}-d^{\prime})>f_{j}(r_{j})+kT^{1/2}
,

•

J_{j}(\ell_{j}+1)>f_{j}(\ell_{j})+20k(k+1-j)sT
,

•

J_{j}(r_{j}-1)>f_{j}(r_{j})+20k(k+1-j)sT
.

Let
\mathsf{A}_{j,2}
be the event such that

•

J_{j}(x)>f_{j+1}(x)\ \textup{for all}\ x\in[\ell_{j}+d^{\prime},\ell_{j}+1]\cup[r_{j}-1,r_{j}-d^{\prime}]
,

•

J_{j}(\ell_{j}+1)>\textup{Tent}_{j+1}(\ell_{j}+1)+18k(k+1-j)sT

•

J_{j}(r_{j}-1)>\textup{Tent}_{j+1}(r_{j}-1)+18k(k+1-j)sT
.

Let
\mathsf{A}_{j,3}
be the event such that

•

J_{j}(x)>\textup{Tent}_{j+1}(x)+16k(k+1-j)sT\ \textup{for all}\ x\in[\ell_{j}+1,r_{j}-1]
.

Recall that
J_{j}
is sampled in
[\ell_{j},r_{j}]
with boundary given by
J_{j}(\ell_{j})=f(\ell_{j})
and
J_{j}(r_{j})=f(r_{j})
. In the event
\mathsf{A}_{j,1}
,
J_{j}
goes up by
T^{1/2}
within distance
d^{\prime}
and then goes up by
sT
within distance
1
. We then resample
J_{j}
in
[\ell_{j}+d^{\prime},\ell_{j}+1]
and
[r_{j}-1,r_{j}-d^{\prime}]
. Conditioned on
\mathsf{A}_{j,1}
the boundary data is high enough to ensure
\mathsf{A}_{j,2}
occurs with probability
D^{-1}
. After that having
\mathsf{A}_{j,2}
, we resample
J_{j}
in
[\ell_{j}+1,r_{j}-1]
. Applying Theorem
5.1
ensures that conditioned on
\mathsf{A}_{j,2}
,
\mathsf{A}_{j,3}
occurs with probability
e^{-T^{5/2}}
. This argument is entailed in the following three lemmas. To simplify the notation, we denote
\mathbb{P}^{\textup{dom}(\bar{\ell},\bar{r}),f}_{\textup{free}}
and
\mathbb{E}^{\textup{dom}(\bar{\ell},\bar{r}),f}_{\textup{free}}
by
\mathbb{P}_{\textup{free}}
and
\mathbb{E}_{\textup{free}}
respectively.

Lemma 7.4
.

There exists
D=D(k,s)
such that

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}_{\textup{free}}(\mathsf{A}_{j,1})\geq D^{-1}e^{-DT^{5/2}}.

Lemma 7.5
.

There exists
D=D(k,s)
such that

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}_{\textup{free}}(\mathsf{A}_{j,2}\,|\,\mathsf{A}_{j,1})\geq D^{-1}.

Lemma 7.6
.

There exists
D=D(k,s)
such that for
E
large enough, it holds that

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}_{J}(\mathsf{A}_{j,3}\,|\,\mathsf{A}_{j,2})\geq D^{-1}e^{-DT^{5/2}}.

Proof of Proposition
7.3
.

From (
6.9
), we check
\mathsf{A}_{j,2}\cap\mathsf{A}_{j,3}\subset\mathsf{A}_{j}
.
By Lemmas
7.4
and
7.5
,

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}_{\textup{free}}(\mathsf{A}_{j,2})\geq\mathbb{P}_{\textup{free}}(\mathsf{A}_{j,2}\,|\,\mathsf{A}_{j,1})\times\mathbb{P}_{\textup{free}}(\mathsf{A}_{j,1})\geq D^{-1}e^{-D^{5/2}}.

By the stochastic monotonicity Lemma
2.7
,

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}_{J}(\mathsf{A}_{j,2})\geq\mathbb{P}_{\textup{free}}(\mathsf{A}_{j,2})\geq D^{-1}e^{-D^{5/2}}.

With Lemma
7.6
, we conclude that

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}_{J}(\mathsf{A}_{j})\geq\mathbb{P}_{J}(\mathsf{A}_{j,2}\cap\mathsf{A}_{j,3})=\mathbb{P}_{J}(\mathsf{A}_{j,3}|\mathsf{A}_{j,2})\times\mathbb{P}_{J}(\mathsf{A}_{j,2})\geq D^{-1}e^{-DT^{5/2}}.

∎

In the rest of this subsection, we prove Lemmas
7.4
,
7.5
and
7.6
.

Proof of Lemma
7.4
.

The first condition in
\mathsf{A}_{j,1}
requires
J_{j}
to raise by an amount
T^{1/2}
within a distance
d^{\prime}=T^{-3/2}.
This event occurs with probability about
e^{-T^{5/2}}.
The second and the third condition requires
J_{j}
to raise by an amount
T
within a distance
1
. This event occurs with probability about
e^{-T^{2}}.
In below we carry out this calculation.

Under the law
\mathbb{P}_{\textup{free}}
,
B_{j}(\ell_{j}+d^{\prime})
is a Gaussian random variable with mean and variance

\displaystyle m=\frac{(r_{j}-\ell_{j}-d^{\prime})f_{j}(\ell_{j})+d^{\prime}f_{j}(r_{j})}{r_{j}-\ell_{j}},\ \sigma^{2}=\frac{(r_{j}-\ell_{j}-d^{\prime})d^{\prime}}{r_{j}-\ell_{j}}.

Let
N
be a Gaussian random variable with mean
0
and variance
1
. It holds that

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}_{\textup{free}}(B_{j}(\ell_{j}+d^{\prime})>f_{j}(\ell_{j})+kT^{1/2})=\mathbb{P}(N>\sigma^{-1}(f_{j}(\ell_{j})-m)+\sigma^{-1}kT^{1/2}).

From Condition
C1
and Remark
4.3
, it holds that
|\ell_{j}+(k+j-1)T|,|r_{j}-(k+j-1)T|\leq 10^{-1}T.
Recall that
d^{\prime}=T^{-3/2}
. Hence
\sigma^{-1}\leq 2^{1/2}T^{3/4}
and then

\sigma^{-1}kT\leq DT^{5/4}.

From Condition
C2
, we have

\left|\frac{f(\ell_{j})-f(r_{j})}{\ell_{j}-r_{j}}\right|\leq DT.
Thus

\displaystyle\sigma^{-1}|f_{j}(\ell_{j})-m|\leq DT^{1/4}.

As a result,

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}_{\textup{free}}(B_{j}(\ell_{j}+d^{\prime})>f_{j}(\ell_{j})+kT^{1/2})\geq\mathbb{P}(N\geq DT^{5/4})\geq D^{-1}e^{-DT^{5/2}}.

Conditioned on
B_{j}(\ell_{j}+d^{\prime})=y
,
B_{j}(\ell_{j}+1)
is a normal distribution with mean and variance

\displaystyle m=\frac{(r_{j}-\ell_{j}-1)y+(1-d^{\prime})f_{j}(r_{j})}{r_{j}-\ell_{j}-d^{\prime}},\ \sigma^{2}=\frac{(r_{j}-\ell_{j}-1)(1-d^{\prime})}{r_{j}-\ell_{j}-d^{\prime}}.

Thus

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}_{\textup{free}}(B_{j}(\ell_{j}+1^{\prime})\geq f_{j}(\ell_{j})+20k(k+1-j)sT\,|\,B_{j}(\ell_{j}+d^{\prime})=y)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}(N>\sigma^{-1}(f_{j}(\ell_{j})-m)+20\sigma^{-1}k(k+1-j)sT)

Because
\sigma\approx 1
,

\displaystyle 20\sigma^{-1}k(k+1-j)sT\leq DT.

When
y\geq f_{j}(\ell_{j})+kT^{1/2}
,

\displaystyle f_{j}(\ell_{j})-m\leq\frac{(1-d^{\prime})(f_{j}(r_{j})-f_{j}(\ell_{j}))}{r_{j}-\ell_{j}-d^{\prime}}-\frac{(r_{j}-\ell_{j}-1)kT^{1/2}}{r_{j}-\ell_{j}-d^{\prime}}\leq DT.

As a result,

\displaystyle\mathbbm{1}\{y\geq f_{j}(\ell_{j})+kT^{1/2}\}\times\mathbb{P}_{\textup{free}}(B_{j}(\ell_{j}+1^{\prime})>f_{j}(\ell_{j})+20k(k+1-j)sT\,|\,B_{j}(\ell_{j}+d^{\prime})=y)

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\mathbbm{1}\{y\geq f_{j}(\ell_{j})+kT^{1/2}\}\times D^{-1}e^{-DT^{2}}.

This finishes the proof for
\ell_{j}+d^{\prime}
and
\ell_{j}+1
. The one for
r_{j}-d^{\prime}
and
r_{j}-1
is similar and we omit the detail.
∎

Proof of Lemma
7.5
.

We first bound
f_{j+1}
by a linear function. By (
6.1
) and (
6.2
), it holds that for
x\in(\ell_{j},\ell_{j}+1]
,

\displaystyle f_{j+1}(x)=\underline{g}(x)\leq f_{j+1}(\ell_{j})+(x-\ell_{j})(k+1-j)T.

By Conditions
C4
, we obtain

\displaystyle f_{j+1}(x)=\underline{g}(x)\leq f_{j}(\ell_{j})+3\Delta+(x-\ell_{j})(k+1-j)T=:L(x).

Next, we show that when
\mathsf{A}_{j,1}
occurs, it holds that

\displaystyle L(\ell_{j}+d^{\prime})+2^{-1}kT^{1/2}<J_{j}(\ell_{j}+d^{\prime}),

\displaystyle L(\ell_{j}+1)+18k(k+1-j)sT<J_{j}(\ell_{j}+1).

They can be deduced by

\displaystyle L(\ell_{j}+d^{\prime})=f_{j}(\ell_{j})+3\Delta+(k+1-j)T^{-1/2}\leq

\displaystyle f_{j}(\ell_{j})+2^{-1}kT^{1/2}

\displaystyle<

\displaystyle J_{j}(\ell_{j}+d^{\prime})-2^{-1}kT^{1/2},

and

\displaystyle L(\ell_{j}+1)=f_{j}(\ell_{j})+3\Delta+(k+1-j)T\leq

\displaystyle f_{j}(\ell_{j})+2k(k+1-j)T

\displaystyle<

\displaystyle J_{j}(\ell_{j}+1)-18k(k+1-j)sT.

In particular, the second condition in
\mathsf{A}_{j,2}
is ensured by
\mathsf{A}_{j,1}
.

Applying a similar argument near
r_{j}
, the third condition in
\mathsf{A}_{j,2}
is also ensured by
\mathsf{A}_{j,1}
. Furthermore,

\displaystyle\mathsf{A}_{j,1}\cap\left\{\left|J_{j}^{[\ell_{j}+d^{\prime},\ell_{j}+1]}\right|\leq 2^{-1}kT^{1/2}\right\}\cap\left\{\left|J_{j}^{[r_{j}-1,r_{j}-d^{\prime}]}\right|\leq 2^{-1}kT^{1/2}\right\}\subset\mathsf{A}_{j,2}.

We conclude that

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}_{\textup{free}}(\mathsf{A}_{j,2}\,|\,\mathsf{A}_{j,1})\geq D^{-1}.

∎

Proof of Lemma
7.6
.

We first replace
\mathsf{A}_{j,2}
by
\mathsf{A}_{j,4}
, which fixes the value of
J_{j}(x)
at
x=\ell_{j}+1
and
x=r_{j}-1
. Let

\displaystyle\mathsf{A}_{j,4}\coloneqq\{J_{j}(x)=\textup{Tent}_{j+1}(x)+18k(k+1-j)sT\ \textup{for}\ x\in\{\ell_{j}+1,r_{j}-1\}\}.

From the stochastic monotonicity Lemma
2.7
, it holds that

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}_{J}(\mathsf{A}_{j,3}\,|\,\mathsf{A}_{j,2})\geq\mathbb{P}_{J}(\mathsf{A}_{j,3}\,|\,\mathsf{A}_{j,4}).

It remains to prove that

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}_{J}(\mathsf{A}_{j,3}\,|\,\mathsf{A}_{j,4})\geq D^{-1}e^{-DT^{5/2}}.

Consider the event

\begin{split}\mathsf{L}_{j}\coloneqq\{\mathcal{L}_{j}(p)\leq&\textup{Tent}_{j+1}(p)-\Delta_{k}\ \textup{for some}\ p\in P_{j+1}\cap[\ell_{j}+1,r_{j}-1]\}.\end{split}

This event represents the scenario that
J_{j}
goes below
\underline{g}
by an amount
\Delta_{k}
at some point in
P_{j+1}\cap[\ell_{j}+1,r_{j}-1]
.

Step 1
: We show in this step that conditioned on
\mathsf{A}_{j,4}
,
\mathsf{L}_{j}
is unlikely.

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}_{J}(\mathsf{L}_{j}|\mathsf{A}_{j,4})\leq 2^{-1}.

We begin by controlling the local fluctuation of
J_{j}
. Recall that we write
\mathbb{P}_{\textup{free}}
and
\mathbb{E}_{\textup{free}}
for
\mathbb{P}^{\textup{dom}(\bar{\ell},\bar{r}),f}_{\textup{free}}
and
\mathbb{E}^{\textup{dom}(\bar{\ell},\bar{r}),f}_{\textup{free}}
respectively. From (
6.9
) and (
6.10
), we can apply Lemma
B.4
to get

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}_{\textup{free}}(B_{j}(x)\geq\underline{g}(x)\ \textup{for}\ x\in[\ell_{j}+1,r_{j}-1]\,|\,\mathsf{A}_{j,4})\geq D^{-1}e^{-DT^{3}}.

When such event occurs,
W^{j,[\ell_{j}+1,r_{j}-1]}_{\textup{Jump}}
is bounded from below by some constant
D^{-1}
. Therefore,

\mathbb{E}_{\textup{free}}\left[W^{j,[\ell_{j}+1,r_{j}-1]}_{\textup{Jump}}\,|\,\mathsf{A}_{j,4}\right]\geq D^{-1}e^{-DT^{3}}.

Let

\mathsf{Flct}_{j}\coloneqq\{|{J}_{j}(x)-{J}_{j}(y)|\leq\Delta\ \textup{for all}\ x,y\in[\ell_{j}+1,r_{j}-1]\ \textup{with}\ |x-y|\leq d\}.

When
\mathsf{A}_{j,4}
occurs,
J_{j}(x)=\textup{Tent}_{j+1}(x)+18k(k+1-j)sT
for
x\in\{\ell_{j}+1,r_{j}-1\}
. From (
6.10
), we have

\displaystyle\left|\frac{J_{j}(\ell_{j}+1)-J_{j}(r_{j}-1)}{r_{j}-\ell_{j}-2}\right|=\left|\frac{\textup{Tent}_{j+1}(\ell_{j}+1)-\textup{Tent}_{j+1}(r_{j}-1)}{r_{j}-\ell_{j}-2}\right|\leq 2kT.

Hence

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}_{\textup{free}}(\mathsf{Flct}^{\textup{c}}_{j}\,|\,\mathsf{A}_{j,4})\leq

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}_{\textup{free}}(\omega_{d,[\ell,r]}(B^{[\ell,r]})\geq\Delta-2kdT)

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}_{\textup{free}}(\omega_{d,[\ell,r]}(B^{[\ell,r]})\geq 2^{-1}\Delta)\leq De^{-D^{-1}T^{4}}.

Here

\displaystyle\omega_{d,[\ell,r]}(B^{[\ell,r]})\coloneqq\max_{x,y\in[\ell,r],\ |x-y|\leq d}\left|B^{[\ell,r]}(x)-B^{[\ell,r]}(y)\right|.

As a result, for
E
large enough, we have

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}_{J}(\mathsf{Flct}^{\textup{c}}_{j}\,|\,\mathsf{A}_{j,4})\leq De^{DT^{3}-D^{-1}T^{4}}\leq 4^{-1}.

Next, we show that
\mathsf{L}_{j}\cap\mathsf{Flct}_{j}
is unlikely. From Condition
C3
,
\mathsf{L}_{j}
and
\mathsf{Flct}_{j}
imply there exists an interval in
\textup{Int}_{j}\cap[\ell_{j}+1,r_{j}-1]
with length
d
where

\displaystyle J_{j}(x)\leq\underline{g}(x)-k\Delta-k\log k.

Together with

\displaystyle d\,\textbf{H}_{j,k+1}(k\Delta+k\log k)\geq de^{\Delta}=64T^{4}(\log T)^{2},

it holds that

\displaystyle\mathbbm{1}_{\mathsf{L}_{j}\cap\mathsf{Flct}_{j}}\times W^{j,[\ell_{j}+1,r_{j}-1]}_{\textup{jump}}\leq e^{-64T^{4}(\log T)^{2}}\times\mathbbm{1}_{\mathsf{L}_{j}\cap\mathsf{Flct}_{j}}.

As a result, for
E
large enough, it holds that

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}_{J}\left(\mathsf{L}_{j}\cap\mathsf{Flct}_{j}\,|\,\mathsf{A}_{j,4}\right)\leq\mathbb{E}_{\textup{free}}\left[W^{j,[\ell_{j}+1,r_{j}-1]}_{\textup{jump}}\,|\,\mathsf{A}_{j,4}\right]^{-1}\times e^{-64T^{4}(\log T)^{2}}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle De^{DT^{3}-64T^{4}(\log T)^{2}}\leq 4^{-1}.

Putting the above estimates together, we conclude that

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}_{J}(\mathsf{L}_{j}\,|\,\mathsf{A}_{j,4})\leq\mathbb{P}_{J}(\mathsf{L}_{j}\cap\mathsf{Flct}_{j}\,|\,\mathsf{A}_{j,4})+\mathbb{P}_{J}(\mathsf{Flct}^{\textup{c}}_{j}\,|\,\mathsf{A}_{j,4})\leq 2^{-1}.

Step 2
: In this step, we want to prove

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}_{\textup{free}}(\mathsf{A}_{j,3}\,|\,\mathsf{A}_{j,4}\cap\mathsf{L}^{\textup{c}}_{j})\geq D^{-1}e^{-DT^{5/2}}.

Let

\mathsf{A}_{j,3}^{\prime}\coloneqq\{J_{j}(p)>\textup{Tent}_{j+1}(p)+17k(k+1-j)sT\ \textup{for}\ p\in P_{j+1}\cap(\ell_{j}+1,r_{j}-1)\}.

To apply Theorem
5.1
, we rewrite the following objects. Set
\breve{\ell}=\ell_{j}+1,\breve{r}=r_{j}-1
,

\displaystyle\breve{f}(x)\coloneqq\textup{Tent}_{j+1}(x)-\Delta_{k},\ \breve{\mathcal{L}}(x):={J}_{j}(x),\ \breve{P}=P_{j+1}\cap(\breve{\ell},\breve{r})

and

\displaystyle\breve{a}^{-}=\textup{Tent}_{j+1}(\breve{\ell})+18k(k+1-j)sT,\ \breve{a}^{+}=\textup{Tent}_{j+1}(\breve{r})+18k(k+1-j)sT.

The law of
\breve{\mathcal{L}}
in
[\breve{\ell},\breve{r}]
under
\mathbb{P}_{\textup{free}}(\,|\,\mathsf{A}_{j,4})
is given by
\mathbb{P}^{1,1,(\breve{\ell},\breve{r}),\breve{a}^{-},\breve{a}^{+}}_{\textup{free}}
. Furthermore, it can easily verified that

\displaystyle\mathsf{L}^{\textup{c}}_{j}=

\displaystyle\{\breve{\mathcal{L}}(p)>\breve{f}(p)\ \textup{for}\ p\in\breve{P}\},

\displaystyle\mathsf{A}_{j,3}^{\prime}=

\displaystyle\{\breve{\mathcal{L}}(p)>\breve{f}(p)+17k(k+1-j)sT+\Delta_{k}\ \textup{for}\ p\in\breve{P}\}.

Because

\breve{a}^{-}-\breve{f}(\ell_{j})=\breve{a}^{+}-\breve{f}(r_{j})=18k(k+1-j)sT+\Delta_{k},

we can applying Theorem
5.1
to conclude that

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}_{\textup{free}}(\mathsf{A}_{j,3}^{\prime}\,|\,\mathsf{A}_{j,4}\cap\mathsf{L}^{\textup{c}}_{j})\geq D^{-1}e^{-DT^{5/2}}.

Resample
J_{j}
between pole points in
P_{j+1}\cap[\ell_{j}+1,r_{j}-1]
, we obtain

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}_{\textup{free}}(\mathsf{A}_{j,3}\,|\,\mathsf{A}_{j,4}\cap\mathsf{L}^{\textup{c}}_{j})\geq\mathbb{P}_{\textup{free}}(\mathsf{A}_{j,3}\,|\,\mathsf{A}_{j,4}\cap\mathsf{L}^{\textup{c}}_{j}\cap\mathsf{A}^{\prime}_{j,3})\times\mathbb{P}_{\textup{free}}(\mathsf{A}^{\prime}_{j,3}\,|\,\mathsf{A}_{j,4}\cap\mathsf{L}^{\textup{c}}_{j})\geq D^{-1}e^{-DT^{5/2}}.

Step 3
: We finish the proof in this step. When
\mathsf{A}_{j,3}
occurs,
W^{j,[\ell_{j}+1,r_{j}-1]}_{\textup{Jump}}\geq D^{-1}
. Hence

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}_{J}(\mathsf{A}_{j,3}\,|\,\mathsf{A}_{j,4}\cap\mathsf{L}^{\textup{c}}_{j})\geq D^{-1}e^{-DT^{5/2}}.

In conclusion,

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}_{J}(\mathsf{A}_{j,3}\,|\,\mathsf{A}_{j,4})\geq

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}_{J}(\mathsf{A}_{j,3}\,|\,\mathsf{A}_{j,4}\cap\mathsf{L}^{\textup{c}}_{j})\times\mathbb{P}_{J}(\mathsf{L}^{\textup{c}}_{j}|\mathsf{A}_{j,4})\geq D^{-1}e^{-DT^{5/2}}.

∎

7.2.
Curve separation over the resampling interval

In this subsection, we prove Proposition
7.7
. Roughly speaking, Proposition
7.7
says the following. Suppose at two points
\ell
and
r
, the jump ensemble
J
is ordered and lie above
\underline{g}
. Then the probability that
J
remains ordered and lie above
\underline{g}
in
[\ell,r]
is at least
e^{-T^{5/2}}
.

We begin with introducing the notation in order to resample
J
in the interval
[\ell,r]
. Fix
j\in[1,k]_{\mathbb{Z}}
. Given
\ell_{j}\leq\ell<r\leq r_{j}
and
a^{\pm}=(a^{-}_{1},\dots,a^{-}_{k},a^{+}_{1},\dots,a^{+}_{k})
, define
