\displaystyle\frac{{d}\mathbb{P}^{1,j,[\ell,r],a^{+},a^{-}}_{\textup{jump}}}{{d}\mathbb{P}^{1,j,[\ell,r],a^{+},a^{-}}_{\textup{free}}}(\mathcal{L})\propto\prod_{i=1}^{j}W^{i,[\ell,r]}_{\textup{jump}}(\mathcal{L}_{i};f_{\textup{J}}).

Next, we prescribe the boundary condition which is well-spaced. Let
bS_{j}(\ell,r)
be the collection of
(a^{+},a^{-})\in\mathbb{R}^{j}\times\mathbb{R}^{j}
which satisfies

(7.1)

\displaystyle a^{\pm}_{i}-a^{\pm}_{i+1}\geq(k+2-j)T^{1/2}\ \textup{ for all}\ i\in[1,j-1]_{\mathbb{Z}},

(7.2)

\displaystyle a^{-}_{j}\geq\textup{Tent}_{j+1}(\ell)+8k(2k+3-2j)sT\ \textup{and}\ a^{+}_{j}\geq\textup{Tent}_{j+1}(r)+8k(2k+3-2j)sT,

and

(7.3)

\displaystyle a^{\pm}_{1}\leq jT^{2}.

Let
\underline{a}^{\pm}
be the smallest member is
bS_{j}(\ell,r)
. That is,

\displaystyle\underline{a}^{\pm}_{i}-\underline{a}^{\pm}_{i+1}=

\displaystyle(k+2-j)T^{1/2},\ i\in[1,j-1]_{\mathbb{Z}},

\displaystyle\underline{a}^{-}_{j}=

\displaystyle\textup{Tent}_{j+1}(\ell)+8k(2k+3-2j)sT,

\displaystyle\underline{a}^{+}_{j}=

\displaystyle\textup{Tent}_{j+1}(r)+8k(2k+3-2j)sT.

The following two events capture the scenario that the curves are well-separated in
[\ell,r]
.

(7.4)

\begin{split}\mathsf{S}_{j}(\ell,r)\coloneqq\{\mathcal{L}_{i}(x)>\mathcal{L}_{i+1}(x)+(k+1-j)T^{1/2}\ \textup{for all}\ i\in[1,j-1]_{\mathbb{Z}}\ \textup{and}\ x\in[\ell,r]\}\\
\cap\{\mathcal{L}_{j}(x)>\textup{Tent}_{j+1}(x)+16k(k+1-j)T\ \textup{for all}\ x\in[\ell,r]\}.\end{split}

(7.5)

\begin{split}\mathsf{Up}_{j}(\ell,r)\coloneqq\{\mathcal{L}_{1}(x)<(j+1)T^{2}\ \textup{for all}\ x\in[\ell,r]\}.\end{split}

Proposition 7.7
.

There exists a constant
D=D(k,s)
such that for
E
large enough, the following statement holds. For all
j\in[1,k]_{\mathbb{Z}}
,
\ell,r
in
[\ell_{j},r_{j}]
and
(a^{+},a^{-})\in bS_{j}(\ell,r)
, we have

\mathbb{P}^{1,j,[\ell,r],a^{-},a^{+}}_{\textup{jump}}(\mathsf{S}_{j}(\ell,r)\cap\mathsf{Up}_{j}(\ell,r))\geq D^{-1}e^{-DT^{5/2}}.

Proof.

The proof involves three steps.

Step 1
: We first show that

\mathbb{P}^{1,j,[\ell,r],a^{-},a^{+}}_{\textup{jump}}(\mathsf{Up}^{\textup{c}}_{j}(\ell,r))\leq De^{-D^{-1}T^{3}}.

This can be achieved by a simple stopping domain argument. Consider the intervals

\begin{split}I_{-}\coloneqq\{x\in[\ell,r]\ |\ \mathcal{L}_{1}(y)<(j+2^{-1})T^{2}\ \textup{for all}\ y\in[\ell,x]\},\\
I_{+}\coloneqq\{x\in[\ell,r]\ |\ \mathcal{L}_{1}(y)<(j+2^{-1})T^{2}\ \textup{for all}\ y\in[x,r]\}.\end{split}

Define

\mathfrak{l}^{\prime}\coloneqq\left\{\begin{array}[]{cc}\sup I_{-}&I_{-}\neq\phi,\\
r&I_{-}=\phi.\end{array}\right.

\mathfrak{r}^{\prime}\coloneqq\left\{\begin{array}[]{cc}\inf I_{+}&I_{+}\neq\phi,\\
\ell&I_{+}=\phi.\end{array}\right.

The last condition in
bS_{j}(\ell,r)
ensures
I_{\pm}
are non-empty. Moreover,

\displaystyle\mathsf{Up}_{j}^{\textup{c}}(\ell,r)\subset\{\ell<\mathfrak{l}^{\prime}<\mathfrak{r}^{\prime}<r\}.

Because
(j+2^{-1})T^{2}
is much larger than
\underline{g}(x)
, we have

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}^{1,1,[\mathfrak{l}^{\prime},\mathfrak{r}^{\prime}],(j+2^{-1})T^{2},(j+2^{-1})T^{2}}_{\textup{free}}\left(B_{1}(x)\geq\underline{g}(x)\ \textup{for all}\ x\in[\mathfrak{l}^{\prime},\mathfrak{r}^{\prime}]\right)\geq D^{-1}.

And then

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}^{1,1,[\mathfrak{l}^{\prime},\mathfrak{r}^{\prime}],(j+2^{-1})T^{2},(j+2^{-1})T^{2}}_{\textup{free}}\left[W^{1,[\mathfrak{l}^{\prime},\mathfrak{r}^{\prime}]}_{\textup{jump}}\right]\geq D^{-1}.

Therefore,

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}^{1,j,[\mathfrak{l}^{\prime},\mathfrak{r}^{\prime}],(j+2^{-1})T^{2},(j+2^{-1})T^{2}}_{\textup{jump}}(\mathsf{Up}^{\textup{c}}_{j}(\mathfrak{l}^{\prime},\mathfrak{r}^{\prime}))

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}^{1,1,[\mathfrak{l}^{\prime},\mathfrak{r}^{\prime}],(j+2^{-1})T^{2},(j+2^{-1})T^{2}}_{\textup{free}}\left[W^{1,[\mathfrak{l}^{\prime},\mathfrak{r}^{\prime}]}_{\textup{jump}}\right]^{-1}

\displaystyle\times\mathbb{P}^{1,j,[\mathfrak{l}^{\prime},\mathfrak{r}^{\prime}],(j+2^{-1})T^{2},(j+2^{-1})T^{2}}_{\textup{free}}(\mathsf{Up}^{\textup{c}}_{j}(\mathfrak{l}^{\prime},\mathfrak{r}^{\prime}))\leq De^{-D^{-1}T^{3}}.

Through the strong Markov property,

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}^{1,j,[\ell,r],a^{-},a^{+}}_{\textup{jump}}(\mathsf{Up}^{\textup{c}}_{j}(\ell,r))\leq

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}^{1,j,[\ell,r],a^{-},a^{+}}_{\textup{jump}}(\mathsf{Up}^{\textup{c}}_{j}(\mathfrak{l}^{\prime},\mathfrak{r}^{\prime})\cap\{\ell<\mathfrak{l}^{\prime}<\mathfrak{r}^{\prime}<r\})

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle De^{-D^{-1}T^{3}}.

This finishes Step 1.

Step 2
:
Define the event

\begin{split}\mathsf{Low}_{j}(\ell,r)\coloneqq\bigcup_{i=1}^{j}\{\mathcal{L}_{i}(p)\leq&\textup{Tent}_{j+1}(p)-\Delta_{k}\ \textup{for some}\ p\in P_{j+1}\cap[\ell,r]\}.\end{split}

In Step 2, we show that

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}^{1,j,[\ell,r],a^{-},a^{+}}_{\textup{jump}}(\mathsf{Low}_{j}(\ell,r))\leq 2^{-1}.

By the stochastic monotonicity Lemma
2.7
, it suffices to prove

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}^{1,j,[\ell,r],\underline{a}^{-},\underline{a}^{+}}_{\textup{jump}}(\mathsf{Low}_{j}(\ell,r))\leq 2^{-1}.

Fix
i\in[1,j]_{\mathbb{Z}}
. Because
\underline{a}^{-}_{i}\geq\textup{Tent}_{j+1}(\ell)+8k(2k+3-2j)sT
,
\underline{a}^{+}_{i}\geq\textup{Tent}_{j+1}(r)+8k(2k+3-2j)sT
and (
6.10
), it’s straightforward to show that

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}^{i,i,[\ell,r],\underline{a}^{-}_{i},\underline{a}^{+}_{i}}_{\textup{free}}(B_{i}(x)\geq\textup{Tent}_{j+1}(x)+7k(2k+3-2j)sT\ \textup{for all}\ x\in[\ell,r])\geq D^{-1}e^{-DT^{3}}.

This can be done by applying Lemma
B.4
with
x_{1}=\ell
,
x_{2}=r
and
m=2kT
. Together with (
6.9
),

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}^{1,j,[\ell,r],\underline{a}^{-},\underline{a}^{-}}_{\textup{free}}(B_{i}(x)\geq\underline{g}(x)\ \textup{for all}\ x\in[\ell,r]\ \textup{and}\ i\in[1,j]_{\mathbb{Z}})\geq D^{-1}e^{-DT^{3}}.

Thus,

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}^{1,j,[\ell,r],\underline{a}^{-},\underline{a}^{-}}_{\textup{free}}\left[\prod_{i=1}^{j}W^{i,[\ell,r]}_{\textup{jump}}\right]\geq D^{-1}e^{-DT^{3}}.

Recall that
\Delta=8\log T
and
d=64T^{-4}(\log T)^{2}
. Let

(7.6)

\mathsf{Flct}_{i}(\ell,r)\coloneqq\{|\mathcal{L}_{i}(x)-\mathcal{L}_{i}(y)|\leq\Delta\ \textup{for all}\ x,y\in[\ell,r]\ \textup{with}\ |x-y|\leq d\}.

Because
\mathcal{L}_{i}(\ell)=\underline{a}^{-}_{i}
,
\mathcal{L}_{i}(r)=\underline{a}^{+}_{i}
and (
6.10
),

\displaystyle\left|\frac{\mathcal{L}_{i}(r)-\mathcal{L}_{i}(\ell)}{r-\ell}\right|=\left|\frac{\textup{Tent}_{j+1}(r)-\textup{Tent}_{j+1}(\ell)}{r-\ell}\right|\leq 2kT.

Hence

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}^{1,j,[\ell,r],\underline{a}^{-},\underline{a}^{-}}_{\textup{free}}(\mathsf{Flct}^{\textup{c}}_{i}(\ell,r))\leq

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}_{\textup{free}}(\omega_{d,[\ell,r]}(B^{[\ell,r]})\geq\Delta-2kdT)

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}_{\textup{free}}(\omega_{d,[\ell,r]}(B^{[\ell,r]})\geq 2^{-1}\Delta)\leq De^{-D^{-1}T^{4}}.

As a result,

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}^{1,j,[\ell,r],\underline{a}^{-},\underline{a}^{+}}_{\textup{jump}}(\bigcup_{i=1}^{j}\mathsf{Flct}^{\textup{c}}_{i}(\ell,r))\leq De^{DT^{3}-D^{-1}T^{4}}\leq 4^{-1}.

From Condition
C3
,
\mathsf{Low}_{j}(\ell,r)
and
\bigcap_{i=1}^{j}\mathsf{Flct}_{i}(\ell,r)
imply there exists
i\in[1,j]_{\mathbb{Z}}
and an interval in
\textup{Int}_{j}\cap[\ell,r]
with length
d
in which

\displaystyle\mathcal{L}_{i}(x)\leq\underline{g}(x)-k\Delta-k\log k.

Together with

\displaystyle d\textbf{H}_{i,j+1}(k\Delta+k\log k)\geq de^{\Delta}=64T^{4}(\log T)^{2},

we deduce that when
\mathsf{Low}_{j}(\ell,r)\cap\bigcap_{i=1}^{j}\mathsf{Flct}_{i}(\ell,r)
occurs,

\displaystyle\prod_{i=1}^{j}W^{i,[\ell,r]}_{\textup{jump}}\leq e^{-64T^{4}(\log T)^{2}}.

As a result,

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}^{1,j,[\ell,r],\underline{a}^{-},\underline{a}^{+}}_{\textup{jump}}\left(\mathsf{Low}_{j}(\ell,r)\cap\bigcap_{i=1}^{j}\mathsf{Flct}_{i}(\ell,r)\right)\leq\mathbb{E}^{1,j,[\ell,r],\underline{a}^{-},\underline{a}^{+}}_{\textup{free}}\left[\prod_{i=1}^{j}W^{i,[\ell,r]}_{\textup{jump}}\right]^{-1}\times e^{-64T^{4}(\log T)^{2}}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle De^{DT^{3}-64T^{4}(\log T)^{2}}\leq 4^{-1}.

Putting the above estimates together, we conclude that

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}^{1,j,[\ell,r],\underline{a}^{-},\underline{a}^{+}}_{\textup{jump}}(\mathsf{Low}_{j}(\ell,r))

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}^{1,j,[\ell,r],\underline{a}^{-},\underline{a}^{+}}_{\textup{jump}}\left(\mathsf{Low}_{j}(\ell,r)\cap\bigcap_{i=1}^{j}\mathsf{Flct}_{i}(\ell,r)\right)+\mathbb{P}^{1,j,[\ell,r],\underline{a}^{-},\underline{a}^{+}}_{\textup{jump}}\left(\bigcup_{i=1}^{j}\mathsf{Flct}^{\textup{c}}_{i}(\ell,r)\right)

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle 2^{-1}.

Step 3
: In this step, we show that

\mathbb{P}^{1,j,[\ell,r],a^{-},a^{+}}_{\textup{jump}}(\mathsf{S}_{j}(\ell,r)\,|\,\mathsf{Low}^{\textup{c}}_{j}(\ell,r))\geq D^{-1}e^{-DT^{5/2}}.

Consider the event
\mathsf{S}_{j}^{\prime}(\ell,r)
being defined by

•

\mathcal{L}_{i}(x)\geq\mathcal{L}_{i+1}(x)+(k+3/2-j)T^{1/2}\ \textup{for all}\ i\in[1,j-1]_{\mathbb{Z}}\ \textup{and}\ x\in P_{j+1}\cap(\ell,r)
,

•

\mathcal{L}_{j}(x)\geq\textup{Tent}_{j+1}(x)+8k(2k+5/2-2j)sT\ \textup{for all}\ x\in P_{j+1}\cap(\ell,r).

Compared to
\mathsf{S}_{j}(\ell,r)
,
\mathsf{S}_{j}^{\prime}(\ell,r)
requires a larger gap between
\mathcal{L}
at points in
P_{j+1}\cap(\ell,r)
.

In order to apply Theorem
5.1
, we rewrite the following objects. Set

\displaystyle\breve{f}(x)\coloneqq\textup{Tent}_{j+1}(x)-\Delta_{k},\ \breve{\mathcal{L}}_{i}(x):=\mathcal{L}_{i}(x),\ \breve{P}=P_{j+1}\cap(\ell,r),\ \breve{a}^{\pm}_{i}={a}^{\pm}_{i}.

It can easily verified that

\displaystyle\mathsf{Low}_{j}^{\textup{c}}=

\displaystyle\{\breve{\mathcal{L}}_{i}(p)>\breve{f}(p)\ \textup{for}\ i\in[1,\mathbb{Z}]\ \textup{and}\ p\in\breve{P}\},

\displaystyle\mathsf{S}^{\prime}_{j}(\ell,r)=

\displaystyle\{\breve{\mathcal{L}}_{i}(p)\geq\breve{\mathcal{L}}_{i+1}(p)+(k+3/2-j)T^{1/2}\ \textup{for all}\ i\in[1,j-1]_{\mathbb{Z}}\ \textup{and}\ p\in\breve{P}\}

\displaystyle\cap\{\breve{\mathcal{L}}_{j}(x)\geq\breve{f}(x)+8k(2k+5/2-2j)sT+\Delta_{k}\ \textup{for all}\ x\in\breve{P}_{j+1}.\}.

From the conditions in
bS_{j}(\ell,r)

\displaystyle\breve{a}^{\pm}_{i}-\breve{a}^{\pm}_{i+1}\geq(k+2-j)T^{1/2}\ \textup{ for all}\ i\in[1,j-1]_{\mathbb{Z}},

\displaystyle\breve{a}^{-}_{j}-\breve{f}(\breve{\ell}),\breve{a}^{+}_{j}-\breve{f}(\breve{r})\geq 8k(2k+3-2j)sT+\Delta_{k}

and

\displaystyle\breve{a}^{\pm}_{1}\leq jT^{2}.

Together with (
6.3
) and (
6.10
), the assumptions (
5.3
) and (
5.4
) hold. By Theorem
5.1
, we conclude that

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}^{1,j,[\ell,r],a^{-},a^{+}}_{\textup{free}}

\displaystyle(\mathsf{S}^{\prime}_{j}(\ell,r)\,|\,\mathsf{Low}^{\textup{c}}_{j}(\ell,r))\geq D^{-1}e^{-DT^{5/2}}.

Resampling the curves between pole points in
P_{j+1}\cap(\ell,r)
, we deduce

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}^{1,j,[\ell,r],a^{-},a^{+}}_{\textup{free}}

\displaystyle(\mathsf{S}_{j}(\ell,r)\,|\,\mathsf{Low}^{\textup{c}}_{j}(\ell,r))\geq D^{-1}e^{-DT^{5/2}}.

When
\mathsf{S}_{j}(\ell,r)
occurs,
\prod_{i=1}^{j}W_{\textup{jump}}^{i,[\ell,r]}\geq D^{-1}
. Therefore

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}^{1,j,[\ell,r],a^{-},a^{+}}_{\textup{jump}}(\mathsf{S}_{j}(\ell,r)\,|\,\mathsf{Low}^{\textup{c}}_{j}(\ell,r))\geq D^{-1}e^{-DT^{5/2}}.

In conclusion,

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}^{1,j,[\ell,r],a^{-},a^{+}}_{\textup{jump}}(\mathsf{S}_{j}(\ell,r)\cap\mathsf{Up}_{j}(\ell,r))\geq\mathbb{P}^{1,j,[\ell,r],a^{-},a^{+}}_{\textup{jump}}(\mathsf{S}_{j}(\ell,r))-\mathbb{P}^{1,j,[\ell,r],a^{-},a^{+}}_{\textup{jump}}(\mathsf{Up}^{\textup{c}}_{j}(\ell,r))

\displaystyle\geq

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}^{1,j,[\ell,r],a^{-},a^{+}}_{\textup{jump}}(\mathsf{S}_{j}(\ell,r)\,|\,\mathsf{Low}^{\textup{c}}_{j}(\ell,r))\times\mathbb{P}^{1,j,[\ell,r],a^{-},a^{+}}_{\textup{jump}}(\mathsf{Low}^{\textup{c}}_{j}(\ell,r))-\mathbb{P}^{1,j,[\ell,r],a^{-},a^{+}}_{\textup{jump}}(\mathsf{Up}^{\textup{c}}_{j}(\ell,r))

\displaystyle\geq

\displaystyle D^{-1}e^{-DT^{5/2}}-De^{-D^{-1}T^{3}}\geq D^{-1}e^{-DT^{5/2}}.

The proof is finished.
∎

7.3.
Proof of Proposition
3.5

In this subsection, we combine Propositions
7.3
and
7.7
to prove Proposition
7.8
. Proposition
7.2
follows easily from Proposition
7.8
.

For
j\in[1,k]_{\mathbb{Z}}
, Let
\mathsf{F}_{j}
be the event

•

J_{i}(x)>\underline{g}(x)\ \textup{for all}\ i\in[1,j]_{\mathbb{Z}}\ \textup{and}\ x\in[\ell_{i}+d^{\prime},r_{i}-d^{\prime}]
,

•

J_{j}(x)>\textup{Tent}_{j+1}(x)+16k(k+1-j)sT\ \textup{for all}\ x\in[\ell_{j}+1,r_{j}-1]
,

•

J_{i}(x)>J_{i+1}(x)+(k+1-j)T^{1/2}\ \textup{for all}\ i\in[1,j-1]_{\mathbb{Z}}\ \textup{and}\ x\in[\ell_{i+1},r_{i+1}]
,

•

J_{1}(x)<(j+1)T^{2}\ \textup{for all}\ x\in[\ell_{1},r_{1}]
.

Roughly speaking,
\mathsf{F}_{j}
is the event that
J_{i}>\underline{g}
in
[\ell_{i}+d^{\prime},r_{i}-d^{\prime}]
and
(J_{1},J_{2},\dots J_{j})
stays ordered.

Proposition 7.8
.

There exists a constant
D=D(k,s)
such that for
E
large enough, the following statement holds. For all
j\in[1,k]_{\mathbb{Z}}
,

(7.7)

\mathbb{P}_{J}(\mathsf{F}_{j})\geq D^{-1}e^{-DT^{5/2}}.

Proof of Proposition
7.2
.

It follows from
\mathsf{F}_{k}\subset\mathsf{NoTouch}
.
∎

Proof of Proposition
7.8
.

We use induction on
j
and start with
j=1
. Comparing
\mathsf{A}_{1}
and
\mathsf{F}_{1}
, the extra requirement in
\mathsf{F}_{1}
is that
J_{1}(x)<2T^{2}
. By a stopping domain argument we can show that
\mathbb{P}_{J}(J_{1}(x)<2T^{2})\geq 1-De^{-D^{-1}T^{3}}
. See Step 1 in the proof of Proposition
7.7
for a similar argument. We omit the detail here. For
T
large enough, it holds that

\mathbb{P}(\mathsf{F}_{1})\geq\mathbb{P}(\mathsf{A}_{1})-De^{-D^{-1}T^{3}}\geq D^{-1}e^{-DT^{5/2}}.

Let
j\in[2,k]_{\mathbb{Z}}
and assume that

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}_{J}(\mathsf{F}_{j-1})\geq D^{-1}e^{-DT^{5/2}}.

Under the law of
\mathbb{P}_{J}
,
\mathsf{F}_{j-1}
and
\mathsf{A}_{j}
are independent. From Propositions
7.3
, it holds that

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}_{J}(\mathsf{F}_{j-1}\cap\mathsf{A}_{j})=\mathbb{P}_{J}(\mathsf{F}_{j-1})\times\mathbb{P}_{J}(\mathsf{A}_{j})\geq D^{-1}e^{-DT^{5/2}}.

In other words, with probability
e^{-T^{5/2}}
,
J_{1},\dots,J_{j+1}
are well-separated and
J_{j}
jumps over
\underline{g}
.

Comparing
\mathsf{F}_{j-1}\cap\mathsf{A}_{j}
with
\mathsf{F}_{j}
, the only requirement in
\mathsf{F}_{j}
which is not guaranteed by
\mathsf{F}_{j-1}\cap\mathsf{A}_{j}
is

(7.8)

\displaystyle J_{j-1}(x)>J_{j}(x)+(k+1-j)T^{1/2}\ \textup{for all}\ x\in[\ell_{j},r_{j}].

In other words, the only situation
\mathsf{F}_{j-1}\cap\mathsf{A}_{j}\cap\mathsf{F}^{\textup{c}}_{j}
can occur is that
J_{j}
raises to high and intersects with
J_{j-1}
. We use a stopping domain argument to resolve this issue. In particular, we will consider the stopping domain given by the leftmost and rightmost locations where (
7.8
) fails.

Define a random set
I^{\prime}_{j,-}\subset[\ell_{1},r_{1}]
which captures the requirements in
\mathsf{F}_{j-1}\cap\mathsf{A}_{j}
from the left. Explicitly,
I^{\prime}_{j,-}\subset[\ell_{1},r_{1}]
is the the collection of
x_{0}\in[\ell_{1},r_{1}]
such that

•

J_{i}(x)>\underline{g}(x)\ \textup{for all}\ i\in[1,j]_{\mathbb{Z}}\ \textup{and}\ x\in[\ell_{i}+d^{\prime},r_{i}-d^{\prime}]\cap(-\infty,x_{0}]
.

•

J_{i}(x)>J_{i+1}(x)+(k+2-j)T^{1/2}\ \textup{for all}\ i\in[1,j-2]_{\mathbb{Z}}\ \textup{and}\ x\in[\ell_{i+1},r_{i+1}]\cap(-\infty,x_{0}]
.

•

J_{j-1}(x)>\textup{Tent}_{j}(x)+16k(k+2-j)sT\ \textup{for all}\ x\in[\ell_{j-1}+1,r_{j-1}-1]\cap(-\infty,x_{0}]
.

•

J_{j}(x)>\textup{Tent}_{j+1}(x)+16k(k+1-j)sT\ \textup{for all}\ x\in[\ell_{j}+1,r_{j}-1]\cap(-\infty,x_{0}]
.

•

J_{1}(x)<jT^{2}\ \textup{for all}\ x\in[\ell_{1},r_{1}]\cap(-\infty,x_{0}]
.

Furthermore, we define
I^{\prime\prime}_{j,-}
to be the subset of
I^{\prime}_{j,-}
with an extra requirement that

\displaystyle\ J_{j-1}(x)>J_{j}(x)+(k+2-j)T^{1/2}\ \textup{for all}\ x\in[\ell_{j},r_{j}]\cap(-\infty,x_{0}].

Define
I^{\prime}_{j,+},I^{\prime\prime}_{j,+}\subset[\ell_{1},r_{1}]
by replacing
(-\infty,x_{0}]
with
[x_{0},\infty)
.
Define

\displaystyle\mathfrak{l}^{\prime\prime}_{j}\coloneqq\left\{\begin{array}[]{cc}\sup I^{\prime\prime}_{j,-},&\textup{if}\ I^{\prime\prime}_{j,-}\neq\phi\\
\ell_{1},&\textup{if}\ I^{\prime\prime}_{j,-}=\phi.\end{array}\right.\ \textup{and}\ \ \mathfrak{r}^{\prime\prime}_{j}\coloneq\left\{\begin{array}[]{cc}\inf I^{\prime\prime}_{j,+},&\textup{if}\ I^{\prime\prime}_{j,+}\neq\phi\\
r_{1},&\textup{if}\ I^{\prime\prime}_{j,+}=\phi.\end{array}\right.

As
\mathsf{F}_{j-1}\cap\mathsf{A}_{j}
occurs,
I^{\prime}_{j,\pm}=[\ell_{1},r_{1}]
. Moreover,
\ell_{i}\in I^{\prime\prime}_{j,-}
. It can be shown as the following. By the second requirement of
\mathsf{F}_{j-1}
, it holds that

\displaystyle J_{j-1}(\ell_{j})\geq

\displaystyle\textup{Tent}_{j}(\ell_{j})+16k(k+2-j)sT.

By (
6.9
), it holds that

\displaystyle\textup{Tent}_{j}(\ell_{j})+16k(k+2-j)sT\geq

\displaystyle\underline{g}(\ell_{j})-3ksT+16k(k+2-j)sT.

Because
\underline{g}(\ell_{j})=\max\{f_{j}(\ell_{j}),f_{j+1}(\ell_{j})\}\geq f_{j}(\ell_{j})=J_{j}(\ell_{j})
, we have

\displaystyle\underline{g}(\ell_{j})-3ksT+16k(k+2-j)sT\geq J_{j}(\ell_{j})-3ksT+16k(k+2-j)sT>J_{j}(\ell_{j})+(k+2-j)T^{1/2}.

Similarly,
\mathsf{F}_{j-1}\cap\mathsf{A}_{j}
implies
r_{i}\in I^{\prime\prime}_{j,+}
.

Next, we discuss the implications of
\mathsf{F}_{j-1}\cap\mathsf{A}_{j}\cap\mathsf{F}^{\textup{c}}_{j}
.
\mathsf{F}_{j-1}\cap\mathsf{A}_{j}\cap\mathsf{F}^{\textup{c}}_{j}
implies (
7.8
) fails at a point in
(\ell_{j},r_{j})
. In particular,
\ell_{j}<\mathfrak{l}^{\prime\prime}_{j}<\mathfrak{r}^{\prime\prime}_{j}<r_{j}
,

\displaystyle J_{j}(\mathfrak{l}^{\prime\prime}_{j})=J_{j-1}(\mathfrak{l}^{\prime\prime}_{j})-(k+2-j)T^{1/2},

and

\displaystyle J_{j}(\mathfrak{r}^{\prime\prime}_{j})=J_{j-1}(\mathfrak{r}^{\prime\prime}_{j})-(k+2-j)T^{1/2}.

Moreover, all the conditions in
I^{\prime}_{j,\pm}
hold at
\mathfrak{l}^{\prime\prime}_{j}
and
\mathfrak{r}^{\prime\prime}_{j}
.

Define the event
\mathsf{K}_{j}
by

\displaystyle\mathsf{K}_{j}\coloneqq\{\ell_{j}<\mathfrak{l}^{\prime\prime}_{j}<\mathfrak{r}^{\prime\prime}_{j}<r_{j}\}\cap\{(J_{1}(\mathfrak{l}^{\prime\prime}_{j}),\dots,J_{j}(\mathfrak{l}^{\prime\prime}_{j}),J_{1}(\mathfrak{r}^{\prime\prime}_{j}),\dots,J_{j}(\mathfrak{r}^{\prime\prime}_{j}))\in bS_{j}(\mathfrak{l}^{\prime\prime}_{j},\mathfrak{r}^{\prime\prime}_{j})\}.

Here
bS_{j}(\ell,r)
defined by (
7.1
), (
7.2
) and (
7.3
). We claim that
\mathsf{F}_{j-1}\cap\mathsf{A}_{j}\cap\mathsf{F}^{\textup{c}}_{j}\subset\mathsf{{K}}_{j}
. Comparing the condition in
bS_{j}(\mathfrak{l}^{\prime\prime}_{j},\mathfrak{r}^{\prime\prime}_{j})
and in
I^{\prime}_{j,\pm}
, we only need to check

\displaystyle\mathsf{F}_{j-1}\cap\mathsf{A}_{j}\cap\mathsf{F}^{\textup{c}}_{j}\subset

\displaystyle\{J_{j}(\mathfrak{l}^{\prime\prime}_{j})\geq\textup{Tent}_{j+1}(\mathfrak{l}^{\prime\prime}_{j})+8k(k+3-2j)sT\}

\displaystyle\cap\{J_{j}(\mathfrak{r}^{\prime\prime}_{j})\geq\textup{Tent}_{j+1}(\mathfrak{r}^{\prime\prime}_{j})+8k(k+3-2j)sT\}.

We give the proof for
\mathfrak{l}^{\prime\prime}_{j}
. The argument for
\mathfrak{r}^{\prime\prime}_{j}
is similar. Suppose
\mathfrak{l}^{\prime\prime}_{j}\in[\ell_{j}+1,r_{j}-1]
, then the assertion holds by the fourth requirement of
I^{\prime}_{j,-}
. Assume
\mathfrak{l}^{\prime\prime}_{j}\in[\ell_{j},\ell_{j}+1)
. By the third requirement of
I_{j,-}^{\prime}
,

\displaystyle J_{j-1}(\mathfrak{l}^{\prime\prime}_{j})\geq

\displaystyle\textup{Tent}_{j}(\mathfrak{l}^{\prime\prime}_{j})+16k(k+2-j)sT.

Because of (
6.10
),

\displaystyle\textup{Tent}_{j}(\mathfrak{l}^{\prime\prime}_{j})+16k(k+2-j)sT\geq

\displaystyle\textup{Tent}_{j}(\ell_{j})-2ksT+16k(k+2-j)sT.

From (
6.9
) and
\underline{g}(\ell_{j})\geq f_{j+1}(\ell_{j})=\textup{Tent}_{j+1}(\ell_{j})
,

\displaystyle\textup{Tent}_{j}(\ell_{j})-2ksT+16k(k+2-j)sT\geq\textup{Tent}_{j+1}(\ell_{j})-5ksT+16k(k+2-j)sT.

Using (
6.10
), again, we have

\displaystyle J_{j-1}(\mathfrak{l}^{\prime\prime}_{j})\geq

\displaystyle\textup{Tent}_{j+1}(\mathfrak{l}^{\prime\prime}_{j})-7ksT+16k(k+2-j)sT.

As a result,
\mathsf{F}_{j-1}\cap\mathsf{A}_{j}\cap\mathsf{F}^{\textup{c}}_{j}
implies

\displaystyle J_{j}(\mathfrak{l}^{\prime\prime}_{j})=

\displaystyle J_{j-1}(\mathfrak{l}^{\prime\prime}_{j})-(k+2-j)T^{1/2}

\displaystyle\geq

\displaystyle\textup{Tent}_{j+1}(\mathfrak{l}^{\prime\prime}_{j})-8ksT+16k(k+2-j)sT

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\textup{Tent}_{j+1}(\mathfrak{l}^{\prime\prime}_{j})+8k(2k+3-2j)sT.

The argument for
\mathfrak{l}^{\prime\prime}_{j}\in(r_{j}-1,r_{j}]
is the similar.

Next, we check that

\mathsf{S}_{j}(\mathfrak{l}^{\prime\prime}_{j},\mathfrak{r}^{\prime\prime}_{j})\cap\mathsf{Up}_{j}(\mathfrak{l}^{\prime\prime}_{j},\mathfrak{r}^{\prime\prime}_{j})\cap\mathsf{K}_{j}\subset\mathsf{F}_{j}\cap{\mathsf{K}}_{j}.

The events
\mathsf{S}_{j}(\mathfrak{l}^{\prime\prime}_{j},\mathfrak{r}^{\prime\prime}_{j})
and
\mathsf{Up}_{j}(\mathfrak{l}^{\prime\prime}_{j},\mathfrak{r}^{\prime\prime}_{j})
are defined in (
7.4
) and (
7.5
). The definition of
\mathsf{K}_{j}
ensures the requirements in
\mathsf{F}_{j}
holds outside the interval
(\mathfrak{l}^{\prime\prime}_{j},\mathfrak{r}^{\prime\prime}_{j})
.
\mathsf{S}_{j}(\mathfrak{l}^{\prime\prime}_{j},\mathfrak{r}^{\prime\prime}_{j})\cap\mathsf{Up}_{j}(\mathfrak{l}^{\prime\prime}_{j},\mathfrak{r}^{\prime\prime}_{j})
then takes cares of the interval
[\mathfrak{l}^{\prime\prime}_{j},\mathfrak{r}^{\prime\prime}_{j}]
.

We are ready to give a lower bound for
\mathbb{P}_{J}(\mathsf{F}_{j})
. Let
\delta\coloneqq\mathbb{P}_{J}(\mathsf{F}_{j-1}\cap\mathsf{A}_{j}\cap\mathsf{F}^{\textup{c}}_{j}).
From the above discussion,

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}_{J}(\mathsf{F}_{j})\geq\mathbb{P}_{J}(\mathsf{F}_{j}\cap{\mathsf{K}}_{j})\geq

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}_{J}(\mathsf{S}_{j}(\mathfrak{l}^{\prime\prime}_{j},\mathfrak{r}^{\prime\prime}_{j})\cap\mathsf{Up}_{j}(\mathfrak{l}^{\prime\prime}_{j},\mathfrak{r}^{\prime\prime}_{j})\cap{\mathsf{K}}_{j}).

From the strong Markov property and Proposition
7.7
, it holds that

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}_{J}(\mathsf{S}_{j}(\mathfrak{l}^{\prime\prime}_{j},\mathfrak{r}^{\prime\prime}_{j})\cap\mathsf{Up}_{j}(\mathfrak{l}^{\prime\prime}_{j},\mathfrak{r}^{\prime\prime}_{j})\cap{\mathsf{K}}_{j})=

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}_{J}(\mathsf{S}_{j}(\mathfrak{l}^{\prime\prime}_{j},\mathfrak{r}^{\prime\prime}_{j})\cap\mathsf{Up}_{j}(\mathfrak{l}^{\prime\prime}_{j},\mathfrak{r}^{\prime\prime}_{j})\,|\,{\mathsf{K}}_{j})\times\mathbb{P}_{J}({\mathsf{K}}_{j})

\displaystyle\geq

\displaystyle D^{-1}e^{-DT^{5/2}}\times\mathbb{P}_{J}(\mathsf{K}_{j}).

Together with
\mathsf{F}_{j-1}\cap\mathsf{A}_{j}\cap\mathsf{F}^{\textup{c}}_{j}\subset\mathsf{K}_{j}
, we have

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}_{J}(\mathsf{F}_{j})\geq\delta\times D^{-1}e^{-DT^{5/2}}.

On the other hand,

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}_{J}(\mathsf{F}_{j})\geq\mathbb{P}_{J}(\mathsf{F}_{j-1}\cap\mathsf{A}_{j}\cap\mathsf{F}_{j})=\mathbb{P}_{J}(\mathsf{F}_{j-1}\cap\mathsf{A}_{j})-\delta\geq D^{-1}e^{-DT^{5/2}}-\delta,

We conclude that

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}_{J}(\mathsf{F}_{j})\geq\inf_{\delta\geq 0}\max\{\delta\times D^{-1}e^{-DT^{5/2}},D^{-1}e^{-DT^{5/2}}-\delta\}\geq D^{-1}e^{-DT^{5/2}}.

The induction argument is finished.
∎

8.
Proof of Proposition
3.6

In this section we prove Proposition
3.6
. The jump ensemble
J
is defined through (
3.13
). Recall that
A\subset{C}_{0,0}([0,s])
is a Borel set and
\varepsilon=\mathbb{P}_{\textup{free}}(B^{[0,s]}\in A).
The goal is to prove

\displaystyle\sup\{\mathbb{P}_{J}(J_{k}^{[0,s]}\in A)\,|\,(\bar{\ell},\bar{r},f_{\textup{J}})\in\mathcal{G}\}\leq D\varepsilon\exp\left(D\left(\log\varepsilon^{-1}\right)^{5/6}\right).

Starting from now, we fix a realization of the favorable event
(\bar{\ell},\bar{r},f_{\textup{J}})\in\mathcal{G}
(see Definition
4.5
). Suppose
P^{*}\cap(0,s)=\phi
, i.e. no pole in
(0,s)
. By the construction of the jump ensemble
J
, the law of
J_{k}^{[0,s]}
is equivalent to that of a free Brownian bridge. Then
\mathbb{P}_{J}(J_{k}^{[0,s]}\in A)=\varepsilon
and Proposition
3.6
follows easily.

From now on we assume
P^{*}\cap(0,s)\neq\phi
. From the construction of the pole set
P^{*}
, see (
6.5
) and (
6.13
),
P^{*}\cap(0,s)
consists of only one element, denoted by
p_{0}
. We enlarge the interval from
[0,s]
to
[q_{1},q_{2}]
to prevent
p_{0}
from being too close to the boundary. Let

\displaystyle q_{1}

\displaystyle\coloneqq p_{0}-\max\{p_{0},\,2^{-1}s\},

\displaystyle q_{2}

\displaystyle\coloneqq p_{0}+\max\{s-p_{0},\,2^{-1}s\}.

Let
\pi:{C}_{0,0}([q_{1},q_{2}])\to{C}_{0,0}([0,s])
be a restriction map, defined as

\displaystyle\pi[h](x):=h(x)-s^{-1}xh(s)-s^{-1}(s-x)h(0).

Note that
\pi\circ B^{[q_{1},q_{2}]}{=}B^{[0,s]}
and
\pi\circ J_{k}^{[q_{1},q_{2}]}{=}J_{k}^{[0,s]}
. For
A^{\prime}=\pi^{-1}(A)
, it holds that

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}_{\textup{free}}(B^{[q_{1},q_{2}]}\in A^{\prime})=

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}_{\textup{free}}(B^{[0,s]}\in A)=\epsilon,

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}_{J}(J_{k}^{[q_{1},q_{2}]}\in A^{\prime})=

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}_{J}(J_{k}^{[0,s]}\in A).

In the remaining of this section, we consider the event
\{J_{k}^{[q_{1},q_{2}]}\in A^{\prime}\}
instead of
\{J_{k}^{[0,s]}\in A\}
.

Let
p_{-}=\max\{p\in P^{*}\,|\,p<p_{0}\}
and
p_{+}=\min\{p\in P^{*}\,|\,p>p_{0}\}
be the elements in
P^{*}
next to
p_{0}
.
From (
6.5
), it holds that

-2^{-1}T<p_{-}<q_{1}<p_{0}<q_{2}<p_{+}<2^{-1}T.

Recall
\underline{g}(x)
in (
6.1
). Define the tent map

\textup{Tent}(x)\coloneqq\left\{\begin{array}[]{cc}\underline{g}(x)&x=p_{0}\ \textup{or}\ x=p_{\pm},\\
\textup{linear inteperlation}&x\in(p_{-},p_{0})\cup(p_{0},p_{+}).\end{array}\right.

We remark that
Tent
agrees with
\textup{Tent}_{j+1}
in
[p_{-},p_{+}]
for all
j\in[1,k]_{\mathbb{Z}}
.

Recall that
\Delta_{k}=(k+2)\Delta+k\log k
. Define the quantities (see Figure
7
for an illustration).

\displaystyle\underline{m}_{0}=

\displaystyle\textup{Tent}(p_{0})-\Delta_{k},\ \underline{m}_{\pm}=\textup{Tent}(p_{\pm})-\Delta_{k},

\displaystyle\underline{m}_{1}=

\displaystyle\textup{Tent}(q_{1})-\Delta_{k},\ \underline{m}_{2}=\textup{Tent}(q_{2})-\Delta_{k},

\displaystyle\sigma_{1}^{2}=

\displaystyle\frac{(p_{0}-q_{1})(q_{1}-{p}_{-})}{{p_{0}}-{p}_{-}},\ \sigma_{2}^{2}=\frac{({p}_{+}-q_{2})(q_{2}-{p_{0}})}{{p}_{+}-{p_{0}}},\ \sigma_{3}^{2}=\frac{(q_{2}-p_{0})(p_{0}-q_{1})}{q_{2}-q_{1}}.

Figure 7.
Tent is constructed using values of
\underline{g}
at poles
p_{-},p_{0},p_{+}
with
p_{0}
being the single pole in
[0,s]
. We expect the values of
J_{k}
stay around Tent, hence above Tent
-\Delta_{k}
with high probability.

The following lemma shows that
J_{k}({p_{0}})\geq\underline{m}_{0}
and
J_{k}({p_{\pm}})\geq\underline{m}_{\pm}
occur with high probability. Define the event

\displaystyle\mathsf{Y}\coloneqq\left\{J_{k}({p_{0}})\geq\underline{m}_{0},\ J_{k}({p}_{\pm})\geq\underline{m}_{\pm}\right\}.

Lemma 8.1
.

There exists a constant
D=D(k)
such that for
E
large enough, we have

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}_{J}(\mathsf{Y}^{\textup{c}})\leq De^{-D^{-1}T^{4}}.

Proof.

The idea is that if
J_{k}({p_{0}})<\underline{m}_{0}
or
J_{k}({p_{\pm}})<\underline{m}_{\pm}
occurs, then with high probability
J_{k}
will stay below
\underline{g}-k\Delta-k\log k
in an interval with length
d
. Because
d\,\mathbf{H}_{t}^{k,k+1}(k\Delta+k\log k)\approx T^{4}
, the Boltzmann weight
W_{\textup{jump}}^{k}
is less than
e^{-T^{4}}
which is unlikely. In below we fill in details of this argument.

From the definition of
\mathbb{P}_{J}
( see (
3.13
) and (
6.15
)),
J_{k}
is independent of other curves and is distributed according to

\displaystyle\frac{{d}\mathbb{P}_{J_{k}}}{{d}\mathbb{P}^{k,k,(\ell_{k},r_{k}),x_{k},y_{k}}_{\textup{free}}}(\mathcal{L})=\frac{W^{k}_{\textup{jump}}(\mathcal{L})}{\mathbb{E}^{k,k,(\ell_{k},r_{k}),x_{k},y_{k}}_{\textup{free}}[W^{k}_{\textup{jump}}]}.

Here
x_{k}=f_{k}(\ell_{k})
and
y_{k}=f_{k}(r_{k})
. We know that when
\mathsf{Y}^{\textup{c}}
occurs,
W^{k}_{\textup{jump}}
is, with high probability, less than
e^{-T^{4}}
. To conclude
\mathsf{Y}^{\textup{c}}
is unlikely, we need to bound the normalizing constant. To do so, we turn off the interaction in
W^{k}_{\textup{jump}}
except near
p_{0}
and
p_{\pm}
to get a new curve
J^{\prime}_{k}
. The law of
J^{\prime}_{k}
satisfies the following Radon-Nikodym derivative relation,

\displaystyle\frac{{d}\mathbb{P}_{J^{\prime}_{k}}}{{d}\mathbb{P}^{k,k,(\ell_{k},r_{k}),x_{k},y_{k}}_{\textup{free}}}(\mathcal{L})=\frac{W^{\prime k}_{\textup{jump}}(\mathcal{L})}{\mathbb{E}^{k,k,(\ell_{k},r_{k}),x_{k},y_{k}}_{\textup{free}}[W^{\prime k}_{\textup{jump}}]}.

Here the weight
W^{\prime k}_{\textup{jump}}
is defined by

\begin{split}W^{\prime k}_{\textup{jump}}(\mathcal{L})\coloneqq&\exp\left(-\int_{[p_{-}-d,p_{-}]\cup[p_{0},p_{0}+d]\cup[p_{+},p_{+}+d]}\textbf{H}_{t}^{k,k+1}(\underline{g}(x)-\mathcal{L}(x))dx\right).\end{split}

By the stochastic monotonicity Lemma
2.7
, it holds that

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}_{J_{k}}(\mathsf{Y}^{\textup{c}})\leq\mathbb{P}_{J^{\prime}_{k}}(\mathsf{Y}^{\textup{c}}).

From condition
C2
and Remark
4.3
, it is straightforward to check that

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}^{k,k,(\ell_{k},r_{k}),x_{k},y_{k}}_{\textup{free}}(\{B(x)\geq\underline{g}(x)\ \textup{for}\ x\in[p_{-}-d,p_{-}]\cup[p_{0},p_{0}+d]\cup[p_{+},p_{+}+d]\})\geq C^{-1}e^{-CT^{3}}.

Here
x_{k}=f_{k}(\ell_{k})
and
y_{k}=f_{k}(r_{k})
. When this event occurs,
W^{\prime k}_{\textup{jump}}
is bounded from below. As a result,

\mathbb{E}^{k,k,(\ell_{k},r_{k}),x_{k},y_{k}}_{\textup{free}}[W^{\prime k}_{\textup{jump}}]\geq D^{-1}e^{-DT^{3}}.

Next, we bound the local fluctuation of
J^{\prime}_{k}
. Recall the event
\mathsf{Flct}_{k}(\ell_{k},r_{k})
is defined in (
7.6
) as

\mathsf{Flct}_{k}(\ell_{k},r_{k})\coloneqq\{|\mathcal{L}_{k}(x)-\mathcal{L}_{k}(y)|\leq\Delta\ \textup{for all}\ x,y\in[\ell_{k},r_{k}]\ \textup{with}\ |x-y|\leq d\}.

Because
\Delta^{2}/d=T^{2},

\mathbb{P}^{k,k,(\ell_{k},r_{k}),x_{k},y_{k}}_{\textup{free}}(\mathsf{Flct}^{\textup{c}}_{k}(\ell_{k},r_{k}))\leq De^{-D^{-1}T^{4}}.

With the bound on the normalizing constant, it holds that

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}_{J^{\prime}_{k}}(\mathsf{Flct}_{k}^{\textup{c}}(\ell_{k},r_{k}))\leq De^{DT^{3}-D^{-1}T^{4}}\leq De^{-D^{-1}T^{4}}.

When
\mathsf{Flct}_{k}(\ell_{k},r_{k})
and
\mathsf{Y}^{\textup{c}}
both occur, there exists an interval with length
d
and on which
\underline{g}(x)-J_{k}(x)\geq k\Delta+k\log k
. From

\displaystyle d\,\textbf{H}_{t}^{k,k+1}(k\Delta+k\log k)\geq de^{\Delta}=64T^{4}(\log T)^{2},

we get

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}^{k,k,(\ell_{k},r_{k}),x_{k},y_{k}}_{\textup{free}}[W^{\prime k}_{\textup{jump}}\cdot\mathbbm{1}_{\mathsf{Y}^{\textup{c}}}\cdot\mathbbm{1}_{\mathsf{Flct}_{k}(\ell_{k},r_{k})}]\leq De^{-D^{-1}T^{4}(\log T)^{2}}.

Using the bound on the normalizing constant again, we conclude that

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}_{J^{\prime}_{k}}(\mathsf{Y}^{\textup{c}})\leq

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}_{J^{\prime}_{k}}(\mathsf{Y}^{\textup{c}}\cap\mathsf{Flct}_{k}(\ell_{k},r_{k}))+\mathbb{P}_{J^{\prime}}(\mathsf{Flct}_{k}^{\textup{c}}(\ell_{k},r_{k}))

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle De^{-D^{-1}T^{4}(\log T)^{2}}+De^{-D^{-1}T^{4}}.

∎

We now start to derive the expression (
8.1
) about
\mathbb{P}_{J}(J_{k}^{[q_{1},q_{2}]}\in A^{\prime})
, which will be the starting point for the estimates. For any
(x_{1},x_{2})\in\mathbb{R}^{2}
, define

\displaystyle F(x_{1},x_{2})\coloneqq\mathbb{P}_{J}(J_{k}^{[q_{1},q_{2}]}\in A^{\prime}|J_{k}(q_{1})=x_{1},J_{k}(q_{2})=x_{2}).

Let
Q(x_{1},x_{2})
be the joint p.d.f. of
(J_{k}(q_{1}),J_{k}(q_{2}))
. Then

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}_{J}(J_{k}^{[q_{1},q_{2}]}\in A^{\prime})=\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}F(x_{1},x_{2})Q(x_{1},x_{2})dx_{1}dx_{2}.

From the definition (
6.14
),
W^{k,[p_{-},p_{0}]}_{\textup{jump}}(\mathcal{L}_{k};f_{\textup{J}})=1
. Also,

\displaystyle W^{k,[p_{0},p_{+}]}_{\textup{jump}}(\mathcal{L}_{k};f_{\textup{J}})=\exp\left(-\int_{[p_{0},p_{0}+d]}\textbf{H}_{t}^{k,k+1}(\underline{g}(x)-\mathcal{L}_{k})\,dx\right).

For any
\textbf{b}=(b_{0},b_{\pm})\in\mathbb{R}^{3}
, let

\displaystyle m_{1}(\mathbf{b})\coloneqq\frac{b_{-}({p}_{0}-q_{1})+b_{0}(q_{1}-{p}_{-})}{{p}_{0}-{p}_{-}},\ m_{2}(\mathbf{b})\coloneqq\frac{b_{0}({p}_{+}-q_{2})+b_{+}(q_{2}-{p}_{0})}{{p}_{+}-{p}_{0}}.

Conditioned on
J_{k}({p}_{0})=b_{0}
and
J_{k}({p}_{\pm})=b_{\pm}
,
J_{k}(q_{1})
and
J_{k}(q_{2})
are independent. Let
\rho_{\sigma,m}(x)=(2\pi\sigma^{2})^{-1/2}e^{-x^{2}/(2\sigma^{2})}
be the p.d.f. of a normal distribution with mean
m
and variance
\sigma^{2}
. The p.d.f. of
J_{k}(q_{1})
is given by

\displaystyle\rho_{\sigma_{1},m_{1}(\mathbf{b})}(x_{1})=:\rho_{\sigma_{1},\underline{m}_{1}}(x_{1})f_{1}(x_{1};\mathbf{b}).

The function
f_{1}(x_{1};\mathbf{b})
is defined through the last equality. Note that as
m_{1}(\mathbf{b})\geq\underline{m}_{1}
, it is straightforward to check that
f_{1}(x_{1};\mathbf{b})
is non-decreasing in
x_{1}
. Similarly, the p.d.f. of
J_{k}(q_{2})
is given by

\displaystyle\rho_{\sigma_{2},m_{2}(\mathbf{b})}(x_{2})\left(\mathbb{E}_{\textup{free}}^{1,1,({p}_{0},q_{2}),b_{0},x_{2}}[W^{k,[p_{0},p_{0}+d]}_{\textup{jump}}]\right)/\mathbb{E}_{\textup{free}}^{1,1,({p}_{0},{p}_{+}),b_{0},b_{+}}[W^{k,[p_{0},p_{0}+d]}_{\textup{jump}}]

\displaystyle=:

\displaystyle\rho_{\sigma_{2},\underline{m}_{2}}(x_{2})f_{2}(x_{2};\mathbf{b}).

The function
f_{2}(x_{2};\mathbf{b})
is defined through the last equality and is non-decreasing provided
m_{2}(\mathbf{b})\geq\underline{m}_{2}
. Write
\bar{Q}(\mathbf{b})
for the joint p.d.f of
(J(p_{0}),J(p_{\pm}))
. Then

\displaystyle Q(x_{1},x_{2})=\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}\rho_{\sigma_{1},\underline{m}_{1}}(x_{1})\rho_{\sigma_{2},\underline{m}_{2}}(x_{2})f_{1}(x_{1};\mathbf{b})f_{2}(x_{2};\mathbf{b})\bar{Q}(\mathbf{b}){d}\mathbf{b}.

The following expression serves as a starting point for proving Proposition
3.6
.

(8.1)

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}_{J}(J_{k}^{[q_{1},q_{2}]}\in A^{\prime})=\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}F(x_{1},x_{2})\rho_{\sigma_{1},\underline{m}_{1}}(x_{1})\rho_{\sigma_{2},\underline{m}_{2}}(x_{2})f_{1}(x_{1};\mathbf{b})f_{2}(x_{2};\mathbf{b})\bar{Q}(\mathbf{b})\,{d}x_{1}{d}x_{2}{d}\mathbf{b}.

Let
\underline{\mathbf{m}}\coloneqq(\underline{{m}},\underline{{m}}_{\pm})
and denote
\{\mathbf{b}\geq\underline{\mathbf{m}}\}\coloneqq\{b_{0}\geq\underline{m}_{0},b_{\pm}\geq\underline{m}_{\pm}\}
. Because
F(x_{1},x_{2})\leq 1
, together with Lemma
8.1
, we could bound
\mathbb{P}_{J}(J_{k}^{[q_{1},q_{2}]}\in A^{\prime})
by

\displaystyle\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}F(x_{1},x_{2})\rho_{\sigma_{1},\underline{m}_{1}}(x_{1})\rho_{\sigma_{2},\underline{m}_{2}}(x_{2})f_{1}(x_{1};\mathbf{b})f_{2}(x_{2};\mathbf{b})\mathbbm{1}_{\{\mathbf{b}\geq\underline{\mathbf{m}}\}}\bar{Q}(\mathbf{b}){d}x_{1}{d}x_{2}{d}\mathbf{b}+\mathbb{P}_{J}(\mathsf{Y}^{\textup{c}})

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\sup_{\mathbf{b}\geq\underline{\mathbf{m}}}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}F(x_{1},x_{2})\rho_{\sigma_{1},\underline{m}_{1}}(x_{1})\rho_{\sigma_{2},\underline{m}_{2}}(x_{2})f_{1}(x_{1};\mathbf{b})f_{2}(x_{2};\mathbf{b}){d}x_{1}{d}x_{2}+\mathbb{P}_{J}(\mathsf{Y}^{\textup{c}}).

From now on we fix
\mathbf{b}\geq\underline{\mathbf{m}}
. The estimates we derive below are uniform in any such choice of
\mathbf{b}
. By the Gibbs property,
F(x_{1},x_{2})
can be rewritten as

\displaystyle F(x_{1},x_{2})=

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}_{\textup{free}}^{k,k,(q_{1},q_{2}),x_{1},x_{2}}\left[\mathbbm{1}{\{B^{[q_{1},q_{2}]}\in A\}}W^{k,[p_{0},p_{0}+d]}_{\textup{jump}}\right]\left/\mathbb{E}_{\textup{free}}^{k,k,(q_{1},q_{2}),x_{1},x_{2}}\left[W^{k,[p_{0},p_{0}+d]}_{\textup{jump}}\right]\right.

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}^{k,k,(q_{1},q_{2}),0,0}_{\textup{free}}(\{B^{[q_{1},q_{2}]}\in A\})\bigg(\mathbb{E}_{\textup{free}}^{k,k,(q_{1},q_{2}),x_{1},x_{2}}[W^{k,[p_{0},p_{0}+d]}_{\textup{jump}}]\bigg)^{-1}.

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\varepsilon\bigg(\mathbb{E}_{\textup{free}}^{1,1,(q_{1},q_{2}),x_{1},x_{2}}[W^{k,[p_{0},p_{0}+d]}_{\textup{jump}}]\bigg)^{-1}.

Here we have used
W^{k,[p_{0},p_{0}+d]}_{\textup{jump}}\leq 1
.

Let

\bar{F}(x_{1},x_{2})\coloneqq\min\left\{1,\varepsilon\bigg(\mathbb{E}_{\textup{free}}^{k,k,(q_{1},q_{2}),x_{1},x_{2}}[W^{k,[p_{0},p_{0}+d]}_{\textup{jump}}]\bigg)^{-1}\right\}.

Hence
F(x_{1},x_{2})\leq\bar{F}(x_{1},x_{2})
. Since
\bar{F}(x_{1},x_{2})
is non-increasing in
x_{1}
for fixed
x_{2}
and and
f_{1}(x_{1};\mathbf{b})
is non-decreasing, for any
x_{2}\in\mathbb{R}
,

\displaystyle\int_{\mathbb{R}}\rho_{\sigma_{1},\underline{m}_{1}}(x_{1})\bar{F}(x_{1},x_{2})f_{1}(x_{1};\mathbf{b}){d}x_{1}-\int_{\mathbb{R}}\rho_{\sigma_{1},\underline{m}_{1}}(x_{1})\bar{F}(x_{1},x_{2}){d}x_{1}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\left(\int_{\mathbb{R}}\rho_{\sigma_{1},\underline{m}_{1}}(x_{1})\bar{F}(x_{1},x_{2})f_{1}(x_{1};\mathbf{b}){d}x_{1}\right)\left(\int_{\mathbb{R}}\rho_{\sigma_{1},\underline{m}_{1}}(x_{1}^{\prime})\,{d}x^{\prime}_{1}\right)

\displaystyle-\int_{\mathbb{R}}\rho_{\sigma_{1},\underline{m}_{1}}(x_{1})\bar{F}(x_{1},x_{2}){d}x_{1}\left(\int_{\mathbb{R}}\rho_{\sigma_{1},\underline{m}_{1}}(x_{1}^{\prime})f_{1}(x^{\prime}_{1};\mathbf{b})\,{d}x^{\prime}_{1}\right)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle 2^{-1}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}\rho_{\sigma_{1},\underline{m}_{1}}(x_{1})\rho_{\sigma_{1},\underline{m}_{1}}(x^{\prime}_{1})(\bar{F}(x_{1},x_{2})-\bar{F}(x^{\prime}_{1},x_{2}))(f_{1}(x_{1};\mathbf{b})-f_{1}(x^{\prime}_{1};\mathbf{b})){d}x_{1}{d}x_{1}^{\prime}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle 0.

Since
\bar{F}(x_{1},x_{2})
is non-increasing in
x_{2}
for fixed
x_{1}
and and
f_{2}(x_{2};\mathbf{b})
is non-decreasing, we can repeat the same deduction to get

\displaystyle\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}\bar{F}(x_{1},x_{2})\rho_{\sigma_{1},\underline{m}_{1}}(x_{1})\rho_{\sigma_{2},\underline{m}_{2}}(x_{2})f_{1}(x_{1};\mathbf{b})f_{2}(x_{2};\mathbf{b}){d}x_{1}{d}x_{2}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}\bar{F}(x_{1},x_{2})\rho_{\sigma_{1},\underline{m}_{1}}(x_{1})\rho_{\sigma_{2},\underline{m}_{2}}(x_{2})f_{2}(x_{2};\mathbf{b}){d}x_{1}{d}x_{2}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}\bar{F}(x_{1},x_{2})\rho_{\sigma_{1},\underline{m}_{1}}(x_{1})\rho_{\sigma_{2},\underline{m}_{2}}(x_{2}){d}x_{1}{d}x_{2}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\int_{\underline{m}_{2}-\sigma_{2}T^{3/2}}^{\infty}\int_{\underline{m}_{1}-\sigma_{1}T^{3/2}}^{\infty}\bar{F}(x_{1},x_{2})\rho_{\sigma_{1},\underline{m}_{1}}(x_{1})\rho_{\sigma_{2},\underline{m}_{2}}(x_{2}){d}x_{1}{d}x_{2}+Ce^{-C^{-1}T^{3}}.

We have used
\bar{F}(x_{1},x_{2})\leq 1
and
\displaystyle\int_{-\infty}^{\underline{m}_{i}-\sigma_{i}T^{3/2}}\rho_{\sigma_{i},\underline{m}_{i}}(x)dx\leq Ce^{-C^{-1}T^{3}}
in the last inequality.

Now it suffices to estimate
\bigg(\mathbb{E}_{\textup{free}}^{k,k,(q_{1},q_{2}),x_{1},x_{2}}[W^{k,[p_{0},p_{0}+d]}_{\textup{jump}}]\bigg)^{-1}
. Let
B
be a Brownian bridge with
B(q_{1})=x_{1}
and
B(q_{2})=x_{2}
. We start by considering an event which ensures
B(x)\geq\underline{g}(x)
for
x\in[p_{0},p_{0}+d]
.

Lemma 8.2
.

For
E
large enough, the following statement holds. Let
B:[q_{1},q_{2}]\to\mathbb{R}
be a continuous curve with
B(q_{2})=x_{2}
. Suppose that

•

x_{2}\geq\underline{m}_{2}-\sigma_{2}T^{3/2}
,

•

B({p}_{0})\geq\underline{g}({p}_{0})+3\Delta
,

•

\displaystyle\inf_{x\in[p_{0},q_{2}]}B^{[p_{0},q_{2}]}(x)>-\Delta
.

Then
B(x)\geq\underline{g}(x)
in
[{p}_{0},{p}_{0}+d]
.

Proof.

It is sufficient to prove the assertion for the case
x_{2}=\underline{m}_{2}-\sigma_{2}T^{3/2}
and
B(p_{0})=g(p_{0})+3\Delta
.

From Condition
C3
and (
6.1
), for all
x\in[p_{0},p_{0}+d]
we have

\displaystyle\underline{g}(x)\leq\underline{g}(p_{0})+\Delta.

Under the assumptions of this lemma, we bound the slope of the secant of
B
in
[p_{0},q_{2}]
as

\displaystyle\left|\frac{x_{2}-B(p_{0})}{q_{2}-p_{0}}\right|=\left|\frac{(\underline{m}_{2}-\sigma_{2}T^{3/2})-(g(p_{0})+3\Delta)}{q_{2}-p_{0}}\right|

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\left|\frac{\textup{Tent}(q_{2})-\textup{Tent}(p_{0})-\sigma_{2}T^{3/2}-(k+5)\Delta-k\log k}{q_{2}-p_{0}}\right|.

Using (
6.10
),
\sigma_{2}\leq s^{1/2}
and
q_{2}-p_{0}\geq 2^{-1}s
, the above is bounded by

\displaystyle 2T+2T^{3/2}+2(k+5)\Delta+2(k\log k)T^{2}.

For large enough
T
, it holds that

\left|\frac{x_{2}-B(p_{0})}{q_{2}-p_{0}}\right|\leq 4T^{3/2}.

As a result, for
x\in[p_{0},p_{0}+d]
, we have

\displaystyle B(x)=

\displaystyle B(p_{0})+B^{[{p}_{0},q_{2}]}(x)+\frac{x_{2}-B(p_{0})}{q_{2}-p_{0}}(x-p_{0})

\displaystyle\geq

\displaystyle(\underline{g}(p_{0})+3\Delta)+(-\Delta)-4T^{3/2}d

\displaystyle\geq

\displaystyle\underline{g}(x)+\left(\Delta-4T^{3/2}d\right)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\underline{g}(x)+\left(8\log T-256T^{-5/2}(\log T)^{2}\right).

Here we have used
\Delta=8\log T
and
d=64T^{-4}(\log T)^{2}
. For
T
large enough, we conclude that
B(x)\geq\underline{g}(x)
for all
x\in[p_{0},p_{0}+d]
.
∎

When
B(x)\geq g(x)
in
[p_{0},p_{0}+d]
, we have

W^{k,[p_{0},p_{0}+d]}_{\textup{jump}}(B)\geq e^{-d\textbf{H}_{t}^{k,k+1}(0)}\geq 2^{-1}.

Therefore, provided
x_{2}\geq\underline{m}_{2}-\sigma_{2}T^{3/2}
,

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}_{\textup{free}}^{k,k,(q_{1},q_{2}),x_{1},x_{2}}[W^{k,[p_{0},p_{0}+d]}_{\textup{jump}}]

\displaystyle\geq

\displaystyle 2^{-1}\mathbb{P}_{\textup{free}}^{k,k,(q_{1},q_{2}),x_{1},x_{2}}(B(p_{0})\geq\underline{g}(p_{0})+3\Delta,\inf_{x\in[p_{0},q_{2}]}B^{[p_{0},q_{2}]}(x)>-\Delta\ )

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle 2^{-1}\mathbb{P}_{\textup{free}}^{k,k,(q_{1},q_{2}),x_{1},x_{2}}(B(p_{0})\geq\underline{g}(p_{0})+3\Delta)\,\mathbb{P}_{\textup{free}}(\inf_{x\in[p_{0},q_{2}]}B^{[p_{0},q_{2}]}(x)>-\Delta\ )

\displaystyle\geq

\displaystyle 4^{-1}\mathbb{P}_{\textup{free}}^{k,k,(q_{1},q_{2}),x_{1},x_{2}}(B(p_{0})\geq\underline{g}(p_{0})+3\Delta).

Under the law
\mathbb{P}_{\textup{free}}^{k,k,(q_{1},q_{2}),x_{1},x_{2}}
,
B(p_{0})
is a normal distribution with variance
\sigma_{3}^{2}
and mean

m_{3}(x_{1},x_{2})\coloneqq\frac{x_{1}(q_{2}-p_{0})+x_{2}(p_{0}-q_{1})}{q_{2}-q_{1}}.

Therefore,

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}_{\textup{free}}^{k,k,(q_{1},q_{2}),x_{1},x_{2}}[W^{[p_{0},p_{0}+d]}_{\textup{jump}}]\geq 4^{-1}\int^{\infty}_{\underline{g}({p}_{0})+3\Delta-m_{3}(x_{1},x_{2})}\rho_{\sigma_{3},0}(z_{3})\,{d}z_{3}

\displaystyle=:4^{-1}\nu_{\sigma_{3},0}\left[\underline{g}({p}_{0})+3\Delta-m_{3}(x_{1},x_{2})\right].

This implies

\displaystyle\bar{F}(x_{1},x_{2})\leq 4\varepsilon\nu_{\sigma_{3},0}\left[\underline{g}({p}_{0})+3\Delta-m_{3}(x_{1},x_{2})\right]^{-1}.

Thus

\displaystyle\int_{\underline{m}_{2}-\sigma_{2}T^{3/2}}^{\infty}\int_{\underline{m}_{1}-\sigma_{1}T^{3/2}}^{\infty}\bar{F}(x_{1},x_{2})\rho_{\sigma_{1},\underline{m}_{1}}(x_{1})\rho_{\sigma_{2},\underline{m}_{2}}(x_{2}){d}x_{1}{d}x_{2}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle 4\varepsilon\int_{\underline{m}_{2}-\sigma_{2}T^{3/2}}^{\infty}\int_{\underline{m}_{1}-\sigma_{1}T^{3/2}}^{\infty}\rho_{\sigma_{1},\underline{m}_{1}}(x_{1})\rho_{\sigma_{2},\underline{m}_{2}}(x_{2})\nu_{\sigma_{3},0}\left[\underline{g}({p}_{0})+3\Delta-m_{3}(x_{1},x_{2})\right]^{-1}{d}x_{1}{d}x_{2}.

To simplify the expression, we perform a change of variables
z_{i}=x_{i}-\underline{m}_{i}
.

\displaystyle m_{3}(x_{1},x_{2})=

\displaystyle\frac{z_{1}(q_{2}-p_{0})+z_{2}(p_{0}-q_{1})}{q_{2}-q_{1}}+\frac{\text{Tent}(q_{1})(q_{2}-p_{0})+\text{Tent}(q_{2})(p_{0}-q_{1})}{q_{2}-q_{1}}-\Delta_{k}.

From (
6.10
), the slope of
Tent
is bounded by
\pm 2T
. Hence

\displaystyle\frac{\text{Tent}(q_{1})(q_{2}-p_{0})+\text{Tent}(q_{2})(p_{0}-q_{1})}{q_{2}-q_{1}}

\displaystyle\geq

\displaystyle\frac{(\text{Tent}(p_{0})-(p_{0}-q_{1})2T)(q_{2}-p_{0})+(\text{Tent}(p_{0})-(q_{2}-p_{0})2T)(p_{0}-q_{1})}{q_{2}-q_{1}}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\underline{g}(p_{0})-4\sigma_{3}^{2}T.

It follows that

\displaystyle m_{3}(x_{1},x_{2})\geq\frac{z_{1}(q_{2}-p_{0})+z_{2}(p_{0}-q_{1})}{q_{2}-q_{1}}+\underline{g}(p_{0})-4\sigma_{3}^{2}T-\Delta_{k}.

Take
M=\sigma_{3}^{-1}(4\sigma_{3}^{2}T+\Delta_{k}+3\Delta)
. In particular,
4\sigma_{3}^{2}T+\Delta_{k}+3\Delta=\sigma_{3}M.
Then the upper bound becomes

(8.2)

\displaystyle 4\varepsilon\int_{-\sigma_{2}T^{3/2}}^{\infty}\int_{-\sigma_{1}T^{3/2}}^{\infty}\rho_{\sigma_{1},0}(z_{1})\rho_{\sigma_{2},0}(z_{2})\cdot\bigg[\nu_{\sigma_{3},0}\left(-\frac{z_{1}(q_{2}-p_{0})+z_{2}(p_{0}-q_{1})}{q_{2}-q_{1}}+\sigma_{3}M\right)\bigg]^{-1}dz_{1}dz_{2}.

Let
N_{1}
and
N_{2}
be two independent Gaussian random variables with mean zero and variances
\sigma_{1}^{2}
and
\sigma_{2}^{2}
respectively. Then

N_{4}\coloneqq\frac{(q_{2}-p_{0})N_{1}+(p_{0}-q_{1})N_{2}}{q_{2}-q_{1}}

is a Gaussian random variable with mean zero and variance

\sigma^{2}_{4}\coloneqq\left(\frac{q_{2}-p_{0}}{q_{2}-q_{1}}\right)^{2}\sigma_{1}^{2}+\left(\frac{p_{0}-q_{1}}{q_{2}-q_{1}}\right)^{2}\sigma_{2}^{2}.

Moreover, as
N_{1}\geq-\sigma_{1}T^{3/2}
and
N_{2}\geq-\sigma_{2}T^{3/2}
,

N_{4}\geq-\left(\left(\frac{q_{2}-p_{0}}{q_{2}-q_{1}}\right)\sigma_{1}+\left(\frac{p_{0}-q_{1}}{q_{2}-q_{1}}\right)\sigma_{2}\right)T^{3/2}.

Before continuing the estimate, we give a simple bound to the above.

Lemma 8.3
.

(1)

4^{-1}\sigma_{3}^{2}\leq\sigma_{4}^{2}\leq\sigma_{3}^{2}
,

(2)

\left(\frac{q_{2}-p_{0}}{q_{2}-q_{1}}\right)\sigma_{1}+\left(\frac{p_{0}-q_{1}}{q_{2}-q_{1}}\right)\sigma_{2}\leq 2\sigma_{3}

Proof.

(1)
From a direct computation,

\displaystyle\sigma_{4}^{2}=

\displaystyle\frac{(q_{2}-p_{0})^{2}}{(q_{2}-q_{1})^{2}}\times\frac{(p_{0}-q_{1})(q_{1}-p_{-})}{p_{0}-p_{-}}+\frac{(p_{0}-q_{2})^{2}}{(q_{2}-q_{1})^{2}}\times\frac{(p_{+}-q_{2})(q_{2}-p_{0})}{p_{+}-p_{0}}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\sigma_{3}^{2}\left(\frac{q_{1}-p_{-}}{p_{0}-p_{-}}\times\frac{q_{2}-p_{0}}{q_{2}-q_{1}}+\frac{p_{+}-q_{2}}{p_{+}-p_{0}}\times\frac{p_{0}-q_{1}}{q_{2}-q_{1}}\right).

Because
p_{-}<q_{1}<p_{0}<q_{2}<p_{+}
, it holds that

\displaystyle\frac{q_{1}-p_{-}}{p_{0}-p_{-}}\leq 1,\ \frac{p_{+}-q_{2}}{p_{+}-p_{0}}\leq 1.

Therefore,

\displaystyle\sigma_{4}^{2}\leq\sigma_{3}^{2}\left(\frac{q_{2}-p_{0}}{q_{2}-q_{1}}+\frac{p_{0}-q_{1}}{q_{2}-q_{1}}\right)=\sigma_{3}^{2}.

Let
\alpha=\frac{q_{2}-p_{0}}{q_{2}-q_{1}}
and
\beta=\frac{p_{0}-q_{1}}{q_{2}-q_{1}}
. Because
q_{2}-q_{1}\leq\frac{3}{2}s
and
p_{0}-p_{-}\geq s
, it holds that

\displaystyle\frac{q_{1}-p_{-}}{p_{0}-p_{-}}=1-\frac{p_{0}-q_{1}}{p_{0}-p_{-}}\geq 1-\frac{3}{2}\frac{p_{0}-q_{1}}{q_{2}-q_{1}}=1-\frac{3}{2}\beta.

Similarly,

\displaystyle\frac{p_{+}-q_{2}}{p_{+}-p_{0}}\geq 1-\frac{3}{2}\alpha.

Together with
\alpha+\beta=1
, it holds that

\displaystyle\frac{q_{1}-p_{-}}{p_{0}-p_{-}}\times\frac{q_{2}-p_{0}}{q_{2}-q_{1}}+\frac{p_{+}-q_{2}}{p_{+}-p_{0}}\times\frac{p_{0}-q_{1}}{q_{2}-q_{1}}

\displaystyle\geq

\displaystyle\left(1-\frac{3}{2}\beta\right)\alpha+\left(1-\frac{3}{2}\alpha\right)\beta

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle 3(\alpha-2^{-1})^{2}+4^{-1}\geq 4^{-1}.

We conclude that
\sigma_{4}^{2}\geq 4^{-1}\sigma_{3}^{2}
.

(2)
By a direct computation,

\displaystyle\left(\frac{q_{2}-p_{0}}{q_{2}-q_{1}}\right)\sigma_{1}+\left(\frac{p_{0}-q_{1}}{q_{2}-q_{1}}\right)\sigma_{2}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\left(\frac{(q_{2}-p_{0})(p_{0}-q_{1})}{q_{2}-q_{1}}\right)^{1/2}\left(\left(\frac{(q_{2}-p_{0})(q_{1}-p_{-})}{(q_{2}-q_{1})(p_{0}-p_{-})}\right)^{1/2}+\left(\frac{(p_{0}-q_{1})(p_{+}-q_{2})}{(q_{2}-q_{1})(p_{+}-p_{0})}\right)^{1/2}\right)

Using
\sigma_{3}^{2}=\frac{(q_{2}-p_{0})(p_{0}-q_{1})}{q_{2}-q_{1}}
,
\alpha=\frac{q_{2}-p_{0}}{q_{2}-q_{1}}
and
\beta=\frac{p_{0}-q_{1}}{q_{2}-q_{1}}
, it becomes

\displaystyle\sigma_{3}\left(\alpha^{1/2}\left(\frac{q_{1}-p_{-}}{p_{0}-p_{-}}\right)^{1/2}+\beta^{1/2}\left(\frac{p_{+}-q_{2}}{p_{+}-p_{0}}\right)^{1/2}\right)

Because
p_{-}<q_{1}<p_{0}<q_{2}<p_{+}
, it holds that

\displaystyle\frac{q_{1}-p_{-}}{p_{0}-p_{-}}\leq 1,\ \frac{p_{+}-q_{2}}{p_{+}-p_{0}}\leq 1.

Therefore,

\displaystyle\left(\frac{q_{2}-p_{0}}{q_{2}-q_{1}}\right)\sigma_{1}+\left(\frac{p_{0}-q_{1}}{q_{2}-q_{1}}\right)\sigma_{2}\leq\sigma_{3}(\alpha^{1/2}+\beta^{1/2})\leq 2\sigma_{3}.

∎

From Lemma
8.3
, (
8.2
) is bounded by

\displaystyle 4\varepsilon\int_{-2\sigma_{3}T^{3/2}}^{\infty}\rho_{\sigma_{4},0}(z_{4})\cdot\bigg[\nu_{\sigma_{3},0}(-z_{4}+\sigma_{3}M)\bigg]^{-1}\,dz_{4}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle 4\varepsilon\int_{-\infty}^{2\sigma_{3}T^{3/2}}\rho_{\sigma_{4},0}(z_{4})\cdot\bigg[\nu_{\sigma_{3},0}(z_{4}+\sigma_{3}M)\bigg]^{-1}\,dz_{4}.

Because
\bigg[\nu_{\sigma_{3},0}(z_{4}+\sigma_{3}M)\bigg]^{-1}
is non-decreasing in
z_{4}
,

\displaystyle\int_{-\infty}^{0}\rho_{\sigma_{4},0}(z_{4})\cdot\bigg[\nu_{\sigma_{3},0}(z_{4}+\sigma_{3}M)\bigg]^{-1}\,dz_{4}\leq 2^{-1}\bigg[\nu_{\sigma_{3},0}(\sigma_{3}M)\bigg]^{-1}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle 2^{-1}Z^{-1}\int_{0}^{2\sigma_{3}T^{3/2}}\rho_{\sigma_{4},0}(z_{4})\cdot\bigg[\nu_{\sigma_{3},0}(z_{4}+\sigma_{3}M)\bigg]^{-1}\,dz_{4},

Here

\displaystyle Z=\int_{0}^{2\sigma_{3}T^{3/2}}\rho_{\sigma_{4},0}(z_{4})\,dz_{4}.

By Lemma
8.3
, for large enough
T
, it holds that

\displaystyle Z\geq\int_{0}^{2\sigma_{4}T^{3/2}}\rho_{\sigma_{4},0}(z_{4})\,dz_{4}\geq 4^{-1}.

As a result, (
8.2
) is bounded by

\displaystyle 12\varepsilon\int_{0}^{2\sigma_{3}T^{3/2}}\rho_{\sigma_{4},0}(z_{4})\cdot\bigg[\nu_{\sigma_{3},0}(z_{4}+\sigma_{3}M)\bigg]^{-1}\,dz_{4}.

Next, we replace
\nu_{\sigma_{3},0}(z_{4}+\sigma_{3}M)
with
\rho_{\sigma_{3},0}(z_{4}+\sigma_{3}M)
. We first show that
M\geq 1
. Recall that
M=\sigma_{3}^{-1}(4\sigma_{3}^{2}T+\Delta_{k}+3\Delta)
. Because
T\geq 10
,

\displaystyle M\geq 4\sigma_{3}T+\sigma_{3}^{-1}\Delta\geq 4(T\Delta)^{1/2}\geq 1.

For
z\geq 1
, [Wil91, Section 14.8] we have

\displaystyle\nu_{1,0}(z)\geq\frac{z}{z^{2}+1}(2\pi)^{-1/2}e^{-z^{2}/2}\geq(2z)^{-1}(2\pi)^{-1/2}e^{-z^{2}/2}=(2z)^{-1}\rho_{1,0}(z).

By the Brownian scaling, for
z\geq\sigma
it holds that

\nu_{\sigma,0}(z)=\nu_{1,0}(\sigma^{-1}z)\geq\sigma(2z)^{-1}\rho_{1,0}(\sigma^{-1}z)=\sigma^{2}(2z)^{-1}\rho_{\sigma,0}(z).

Because
M\geq 1
and
z_{4}\geq 0
, we deduce

\displaystyle\nu_{\sigma_{3},0}(z_{4}+\sigma_{3}M)\geq 2^{-1}\sigma_{3}^{2}(z_{4}+\sigma_{3}M)^{-1}\rho_{\sigma_{3},0}(z_{4}+\sigma_{3}M).

Hence (
8.2
) is bounded by

\displaystyle 24\varepsilon\sigma_{3}^{-2}\int_{0}^{2\sigma_{3}T^{3/2}}\rho_{\sigma_{4},0}(z_{4})\rho_{\sigma_{3},0}(z_{4}+\sigma_{3}M)^{-1}(z_{4}+\sigma_{3}M)\,dz_{4}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle 24\varepsilon\int_{0}^{2\sigma_{3}T^{3/2}}\rho_{\sigma_{4},0}(z_{4})\rho_{\sigma_{3},0}(z_{4})^{-1}e^{M\sigma_{3}^{-1}z_{4}+M^{2}/2}(\sigma_{3}^{-1}z_{4}+M)\sigma_{3}^{-1}\,dz_{4}.

Because
4^{-1}\sigma_{3}^{2}\leq\sigma_{4}^{2}\leq\sigma_{3}^{2}
,

\rho_{\sigma_{4},0}(z_{4})\rho_{\sigma_{3},0}(z_{4})^{-1}=\sigma_{4}^{-1}\sigma_{3}\exp({-2^{-1}(\sigma_{4}^{-2}-\sigma_{3}^{-2})z_{4}^{2}})\leq 2.

(
8.2
) is bounded by

\displaystyle 48\varepsilon\int_{0}^{2\sigma_{3}T^{3/2}}e^{M\sigma_{3}^{-1}z_{4}+M^{2}/2}(\sigma_{3}^{-1}z_{4}+M)\sigma_{3}^{-1}\,dz_{4}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle 48\varepsilon\times e^{M^{2}/2}\int_{0}^{2T^{3/2}}e^{Mw_{4}}(w_{4}+M)\,dw_{4}.

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle 48\varepsilon\times 2T^{3/2}(2T^{3/2}+M)e^{2MT^{3/2}+M^{2}/2}.

We proceed to bound
M
. Because
\frac{3}{2}s\geq q_{2}-q_{1}
and
q_{2}-p_{0},p_{0}-q_{1}\geq\frac{1}{2}s
, it holds that
\frac{3}{8}s\geq\sigma_{3}^{2}\geq\frac{1}{6}s
. Therefore
M=\sigma_{3}^{-1}(4\sigma_{3}^{2}T+\Delta_{k}+3\Delta)\leq DT
. We arrive at

\displaystyle\eqref{equ:long}\leq\varepsilon\times De^{DT^{5/2}}.

In conclusion,

\displaystyle\sup_{\mathbf{b}\geq\underline{\mathbf{m}}}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}F(x_{1},x_{2})\rho_{\sigma_{1},\underline{m}_{1}}(x_{1})\rho_{\sigma_{2},\underline{m}_{2}}(x_{2})f_{1}(x_{1};\mathbf{b})f_{2}(x_{2};\mathbf{b}){d}x_{1}{d}x_{2}\leq\varepsilon\times De^{DT^{5/2}}+Ce^{-C^{-1}T^{3}}.

We are ready to complete the proof of Proposition
3.6
.

Proof of Proposition
3.6
.

From the above discussion,

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}_{J}(J_{k}^{[q_{1},q_{2}]}\in A^{\prime})\leq

\displaystyle\sup_{\mathbf{b}\geq\underline{\mathbf{m}}}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}F(x_{1},x_{2})\rho_{\sigma_{1},\underline{m}_{1}}(x_{1})\rho_{\sigma_{2},\underline{m}_{2}}(x_{2})f_{1}(x_{1};\mathbf{b})f_{2}(x_{2};\mathbf{b}){d}x_{1}{d}x_{2}+\mathbb{P}_{J}(\mathsf{Y}^{\textup{c}})

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\varepsilon\times De^{DT^{5/2}}+Ce^{-C^{-1}T^{3}}+De^{-D^{-1}T^{4}}.

Recall that
T=E\max\{\left(\log\varepsilon^{-1}\right)^{1/3},s\}.
Therefore by taking
E
large enough,

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}_{J}(J_{k}^{[0,s]}\in A)=\mathbb{P}_{J}(J_{k}^{[q_{1},q_{2}]}\in A^{\prime})\leq\varepsilon\times D\exp\left(D\left(\log\varepsilon^{-1}\right)^{5/6}\right).

∎

Appendix A
Concave functions

In this section we record basic properties of concave functions. Throughout this section,
h:[\ell,r]\to\mathbb{R}
is a continuous concave function.

For
x\in(\ell,r)
, the left/right derivative of
h
at
x
is defined by

\displaystyle h^{\prime}_{\pm}(x)\coloneqq\lim_{y\to x^{\pm}}\frac{h(y)-h(x)}{y-x}.

We may extend
h^{\prime}_{+}(x)
to
x=\ell
by allowing it to take value
\infty
. We may also extend
h^{\prime}_{-}(r)
to
x=r
by allowing it to take value
-\infty
.
h^{\prime}_{\pm}(x)
are monotone non-increasing where they are defined. Moreover, for all
x\in(\ell,r)
,
h^{\prime}_{-}(x)\geq h^{\prime}_{+}(x)
.

Lemma A.1
.

h^{\prime}_{+}(x):[\ell,r)\to\mathbb{R}\cup\{\infty\}
is right continuous and
h^{\prime}_{-}(x):(\ell,r]\to\mathbb{R}\cup\{-\infty\}
is left continuous.

Proof.

Fix
x_{0}\in[\ell,r)
and take
x_{j}\searrow x_{0}
. Given
y>x_{0}
, we have for
x_{j}<y
,

\displaystyle\frac{h(y)-h(x_{j})}{y-x_{j}}\leq h^{\prime}_{+}(x_{j}).

By the continuity of
h(x)
at
x_{0}
,

\displaystyle\frac{h(y)-h(x_{0})}{y-x_{0}}\leq\liminf_{j\to\infty}h^{\prime}_{+}(x_{j}).

Hence
h^{\prime}_{+}(x_{0})\leq\displaystyle\liminf_{j\to\infty}h^{\prime}_{+}(x_{j})
. By the monotonicity of
h^{\prime}_{+}(x)
,
h^{\prime}_{+}(x_{0})\geq\displaystyle\limsup_{j\to\infty}h^{\prime}_{+}(x_{j})
. This shows
h^{\prime}_{+}(x)
is right continuous. Left continuity of
h^{\prime}_{+}(x)
can be derived similarly.
∎

Define the extreme points of
h(x)
as

\displaystyle\text{xExt}(h)\coloneqq\{\ell,r\}\cup\left\{x\in(\ell,r)\ \big|\ h\big|_{[x-\delta,x+\delta]}\ \textup{is not linear for any}\ \delta>0\right\}.

For any
x_{0}\in[\ell,r]\setminus\textup{xExt}(h)
, by considering the largest interval containing
x_{0}
in which
h(x)
is linear, we get the following lemma.

Lemma A.2
.

For all
x_{0}\in[\ell,r]\setminus\textup{xExt}(h)
, there exist
x_{1},x_{2}\in\textup{xExt}(h)
such that
x_{0}\in(x_{1},x_{2})
and
h\big|_{[x_{1},x_{2}]}
is linear.

As a direct corollary, we have

Corollary A.3
.

Let
\bar{h}:[\ell,r]\to\mathbb{R}
be another concave function. Suppose
\bar{h}(x)\geq h(x)
for all
x\in\textup{xExt}(h)
. Then
\bar{h}(x)\geq h(x)
for all
x\in[\ell,r]
.

Let
P\subset\textup{xExt}(h)
be a subset that satisfies the following properties

•

\ell,r\in P

•

For any
y\in\textup{xExt}(h)\cap[\ell,r]
, there exists
x\in P
such that
|x-y|<s
.

Lemma A.4
.

Suppose that
|h^{\prime}_{\pm}|\leq m
for some
m\geq 0
. Further assume that
\bar{h}:[\ell,r]\to\mathbb{R}
is a concave function with
\bar{h}(x)\geq h(x)
for all
x\in P\cap[\ell,r]
. Then
\bar{h}(x)+2ms\geq h(x)
for all
x\in[\ell,r]
.

Proof.

It suffices to show that
\bar{h}(x)+2m\geq h(x)
for all
x\in\textup{xExt}(h)
. Given
x\in\textup{xExt}(h)
. If
x\in P
, by the assumption
\bar{h}(x)\geq h(x)
. Suppose
x\in\textup{xExt}(h)\setminus P
. There exists
y\in P
with
|x-y|<d
. We further assume that
x<y
. Because
\bar{h}(y)\geq h(y)
,
\bar{h}(\ell)\geq h(\ell)
and
\bar{h}
is concave, we have

\displaystyle\bar{h}(x)\geq

\displaystyle\frac{(y-x)\bar{h}(\ell)+(x-\ell)\bar{h}(y)}{y-\ell}\geq\frac{(y-x)h(\ell)+(x-\ell)h(y)}{y-\ell}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle h(y)+\frac{h(\ell)-h(y)}{y-\ell}(y-x)\geq h(y)-ms.

Together with
h(x)\leq h(y)+ms
, we obtain that
\bar{h}(x)\geq h(x)-2ms
. The argument for
y<x
is similar. The proof is finished.
∎

Lemma A.5
.

Let
m\in\mathbb{R}
. Assume that
\{x\in[\ell,r]\ |\ h^{\prime}_{+}(x)\leq m\}
is non-empty. Then

x_{1}\coloneqq\inf\{x\in[\ell,r]\ |\ h^{\prime}_{+}(x)\leq m\}\in\textup{xExt}(h)

and
h^{\prime}_{+}(x_{1})\leq m
. Similarly, assume
\{x\in[\ell,r]\ |\ h^{\prime}_{-}(x)\geq m\}
is non-empty. Then

x_{2}\coloneqq\sup\{x\in[\ell,r]\ |\ h^{\prime}_{-}(x)\geq m\}\in\textup{xExt}(h)

and
h^{\prime}(x_{2})\geq m
.

Proof.

h^{\prime}_{+}(x_{1})\leq m
and
h^{\prime}_{-}(x_{2})\geq m
follows from one-sided continuity of
h^{\prime}_{\pm}.
If
x_{1}\notin\textup{xExt}(h)
, then
h
is linear around
x_{1}
which contradicts the definition of
x_{1}
. The proof for
x_{2}
is similar.
∎

Fix
m\in\mathbb{R}
and
[\ell^{\prime},r^{\prime}]\subset(\ell,r)
. Define

\mathbf{l}(h)\coloneqq\left\{\begin{array}[]{cc}\inf\{x\in[\ell^{\prime},r^{\prime}]\,|\,h^{\prime}_{+}(x)\leq m\}&\{x\in[\ell^{\prime},r^{\prime}]\,|\,h^{\prime}_{+}(x)\leq m\}\neq\phi,\\
r^{\prime}&\{x\in[\ell^{\prime},r^{\prime}]\,|\,h^{\prime}_{+}(x)\leq m\}=\phi.\end{array}\right..

Similarly,

\mathbf{r}(h)\coloneqq\left\{\begin{array}[]{cc}\sup\{x\in[\ell^{\prime},r^{\prime}]\,|\,h^{\prime}_{-}(x)\geq m\}&\{x\in[\ell^{\prime},r^{\prime}]\,|\,h^{\prime}_{-}(x)\geq m\}\neq\phi,\\
\ell^{\prime}&\{x\in[\ell^{\prime},r^{\prime}]\,|\,h^{\prime}_{-}(x)\geq m\}=\phi.\end{array}\right..

The following lemma shows that
l
and
r
are semi-continuous with respect to the uniform topology.

Lemma A.6
.

Given a sequence of concave functions
h_{i}
converging to
h_{0}
uniformly on
[\ell,r]
, we have

\displaystyle\liminf_{i\to\infty}\mathbf{l}(h_{i})\geq\mathbf{l}(h_{0}),\ \limsup_{i\to\infty}\mathbf{r}(h_{i})\leq\mathbf{r}(h_{0}).

Proof.

We give the proof for
\mathbf{l}
. The argument for
\mathbf{r}
is similar. We assume
\mathbf{l}(h_{0})>\ell^{\prime}
otherwise the assertion is clearly true. For any
\ell^{\prime}\leq x_{0}<\mathbf{l}(h_{0}))
, we have
(h_{0})^{\prime}_{+}(x_{0})>m
. Hence there exists
\varepsilon>0
such that

\displaystyle\varepsilon^{-1}(h_{0}(x_{0}+\varepsilon)-h_{0}(x_{0}))>m.

Because
h_{i}
converges to
h_{0}
uniformly, for
i
large enough,

\displaystyle\varepsilon^{-1}(h_{i}(x_{0}+\varepsilon)-h_{i}(x_{0}))>m.

By the concavity of
h_{i}
,
(h_{i})^{\prime}_{+}(x_{0})>m.
For such
i
,
\mathbf{l}(h_{i})\geq x_{0}
. Hence

\displaystyle\liminf_{i\to\infty}\mathbf{l}(h_{i})\geq x_{0}.

Because the above holds for all
\ell^{\prime}\leq x_{0}<\textbf{l}(h_{0}),
we have

\displaystyle\liminf_{i\to\infty}\mathbf{l}(h_{i})\geq\textbf{l}(h_{0}).

The proof is finished.
∎

Let
g:[\ell,r]\to\mathbb{R}
be an upper semi-continuous function. Let
h(x):[\ell,r]\to\mathbb{R}
be the concave majorant of
g
in
[\ell,r]
. In other words, for any
x_{0}\in[\ell,r]

\displaystyle h(x_{0})\coloneqq\inf\left\{ax_{0}+b\ |\ ax+b\geq g(x)\ \textup{for all}\ x\in[\ell,r]\right\}.

We note that
h(x)
is continuous in
[\ell,r]
.

Lemma A.7
.

For all
x\in\textup{xExt}(h)\setminus\{\ell,r\}
, we have
h(x)=g(x)
.

Proof.

Suppose that
x_{0}\in\textup{xExt}(h)\setminus\{\ell,r\}
and that
h(x_{0})-g(x_{0})>0
. Let
L_{\pm}(x)=h^{\prime}_{\pm}(x_{0})(x-x_{0})+h(x_{0})
. We have
L_{\pm}(x)\geq h(x)\geq g(x)
for all
x\in[\ell,r]
. Suppose that
L_{+}(x)>g(x)
for all
x\in(x_{0},r]
. By the upper semi-continuity of
g(x)
,

\varepsilon=\displaystyle\inf_{[x_{0},r]}(L_{+}(x)-g(x))>0.

Then for all
x\in[\ell,r]
,

L_{+}(x)-\frac{\varepsilon}{r-x_{0}}(x-x_{0})\geq g(x).

Hence
L_{+}(x)-\frac{\varepsilon}{r-x_{0}}(x-x_{0})\geq h(x)
. This implies
h^{\prime}_{+}(x_{0})-\frac{\varepsilon}{r-x_{0}}\geq h^{\prime}_{+}(x_{0})
which is a contradiction. Therefore there exists
x_{+}\in(x_{0},r]
such that
L_{+}(x_{+})=g(x_{+})
. Similarly, there exists
x_{-}\in[\ell,x_{0})
such that
L_{-}(x_{-})=g(x_{-})
. Because
x_{0}\in\textup{xExt}(h)
, we must have
h^{\prime}_{+}(x_{0})<h^{\prime}_{-}(x_{0})
. Take
L_{0}(x)=\frac{1}{2}(h^{\prime}_{+}(x_{0})+h^{\prime}_{-}(x_{0}))(x-x_{0})+h(x_{0})
. At
x=x_{0}
,
L_{0}(x_{0})=h(x_{0})>g(x_{0})
. For
x\in(x_{0},r]
,
L_{0}(x)>L_{+}(x)\geq g(x)
. For
x\in[\ell,x_{0})
,
L_{0}(x)>L_{-}(x)\geq g(x)
. In particular,

\displaystyle\varepsilon_{0}=\inf_{x\in[\ell,r]}L_{0}(x)-g(x)>0.

Then
h(x)\leq L_{0}(x)-\varepsilon_{0}
, which is a contradiction at
x=x_{0}
.
∎

Next, we consider the situation that
h(x)
or
g(x)
is close to a parabola
-2^{-1}x^{2}
.

Lemma A.8
.

Let
\delta>0
be a positive number. Let
h:[\ell,r]\to\mathbb{R}
be a continuous concave function with
|h(x)+2^{-1}x^{2}|\leq 4^{-1}\delta^{2}
. Then for all
x\in[\ell+\delta,r-\delta]
, we have

(A.1)

-x-\delta\leq h^{\prime}_{+}(x)\leq h^{\prime}_{-}(x)\leq-x+\delta.

Proof.

Let
x\in[\ell+\delta,r-\delta].
By the concavity of
h
, we have

\displaystyle h^{\prime}_{+}(x)\geq\frac{h(x)-h(x-\delta)}{\delta}\geq\delta^{-1}\left((2^{-1}x^{2}-4^{-1}\delta^{2})-(2^{-1}(x-\delta)^{2}+4^{-1}\delta^{2})\right)=-x-\delta.

Similarly,

\displaystyle h^{\prime}_{-}(x)\leq\frac{h(x+\delta)-h(x)}{\delta}\leq\delta^{-1}\left((2^{-1}(x+\delta)^{2}+4^{-1}\delta^{2})-(2^{-1}x^{2}-4^{-1}\delta^{2})\right)=-x+\delta.

∎

Lemma A.9
.

Let
g:[\ell,r]\to\mathbb{R}
be a function with
|g(x)+2^{-1}x^{2}|\leq 4^{-1}\delta^{2}
. Take
x_{0}\in[\ell+3\delta,r-3\delta]
. Let
h_{1}
and
h_{2}
be the concave majorant of
g(x)
in
[\ell,r]
and
[x_{0}-3\delta,x_{0}+3\delta]
respectively. Then
h_{1}(x_{0})=h_{2}(x_{0})
.

Proof.

Directly from the definition, we have
h_{1}(x_{0})\geq h_{2}(x_{0})
. Consider the line

\displaystyle L(x)\coloneqq h_{2}(x_{0})+(h^{\prime}_{2})_{+}(x_{0})(x-x_{0}).

It suffices to show that
L(x)\geq g(x)
for all
x\in[\ell,r]
. Because
|h_{2}(x)+2^{-1}x^{2}|\leq 4^{-1}\delta^{2}
for all
x\in[x_{0}-\delta,x_{0}+\delta]
, from Lemma
A.8
we have

\displaystyle|(h^{\prime}_{2})_{+}(x_{0})+x_{0}|\leq\delta.

As a result, for all
x\geq x_{0}
, we have

\displaystyle L(x)-g(x)

\displaystyle\geq\left[-2^{-1}x_{0}^{2}-4^{-1}\delta^{2}+(-x_{0}+\delta)(x-x_{0})\right]-[-2^{-1}x^{2}+4^{-1}\delta^{2}]

\displaystyle=2^{-1}(x-x_{0})^{2}-\delta(x-x_{0})-2^{-1}\delta^{2}.

In particular, for
x\geq x_{0}+3\delta
, we have
L(x)\geq g(x)
. Similar argument can show that
\ell(x)\geq g(x)
for all
x\leq x_{0}-3\delta
. Together with
L(x)\geq h_{2}(x)\geq f(x)
for all
x\in[x_{0}-3\delta,x_{0}+3\delta]
, we conclude that
L(x)\geq f(x)
for all
x\in[\ell,r]
. Thus

\displaystyle h_{2}(x_{0})=L(x_{0})\geq h_{1}(x_{0}).

The proof is finished.
∎

Corollary A.10
.

Let
\ell<\ell^{\prime}<r^{\prime}<r
be real numbers and
g:[\ell,r]\to\mathbb{R}
be a function with
|g(x)+2^{-1}x^{2}|\leq 4^{-1}\delta^{2}
. Let
h_{1}
and
h_{2}
be the concave majorant of
g(x)
in
[\ell,r]
and
[\ell^{\prime},r^{\prime}]
respectively. Then
h_{1}(x)=h_{2}(x)
for all
x\in[\ell^{\prime}+3\delta,r^{\prime}-3\delta]
.

Appendix B
Miscellaneous

Lemma B.1
.

For any
k\in\mathbb{N}
and
(y_{1},y_{2},\dots y_{k},y_{k+1})\in\mathbb{R}^{k+1}
,

-\sum_{j=1}^{k}e^{y_{j+1}-y_{j}}\leq-ke^{(y_{k+1}-y_{1})/k}.

Proof.

The assertion holds clearly when
k=1
. Assume
k\geq 2
and the assertion holds for
k-1
. Then

\displaystyle-\sum_{j=1}^{k}e^{y_{j+1}-y_{j}}=-\sum_{j=1}^{k-1}e^{y_{j+1}-y_{j}}-e^{y_{k+1}-y_{k}}\leq-(k-1)e^{(y_{k}-y_{1})/(k-1)}-e^{y_{k+1}-y_{k}}.

It is straightforward to show that

-(k-1)e^{(y_{k}-y_{1})/(k-1)}-e^{y_{k+1}-y_{k}}\leq-ke^{(y_{k+1}-y_{1})/k}

and the inequality is achieved when
y_{k}=\frac{1}{k}y_{1}+\frac{k-1}{k}y_{k+1}
. The proof is finished.
∎

Recall that
\textbf{H}_{t}(x)=e^{t^{1/3}x}
. For integers
1\leq j<k
, define

(B.1)

\mathbf{H}_{t}^{j,k}(x)\coloneqq(k-1)^{-1}e^{t^{1/3}x/{(k-j)}}.

Lemma B.2
.

For any
k\in\mathbb{N}
, and
(y_{1},y_{2},\dots y_{k+1})\in\mathbb{R}^{k+1}
, we have

\displaystyle-\sum_{j=1}^{k}\mathbf{H}_{t}(y_{j+1}-y_{j})\leq-\sum_{j=1}^{k}\mathbf{H}_{t}^{j,k+1}(y_{k+1}-y_{j}).

Proof.

When
k=1
,
\mathbf{H}_{t}^{1,2}(x)=\mathbf{H}_{t}(x)
and the assertion clearly holds. Assume
k\geq 2
and the assertion holds for
k-1
.

\displaystyle-\sum_{j=1}^{k}\mathbf{H}_{t}(y_{j+1}-y_{j})

\displaystyle=-\frac{1}{k}\sum_{j=1}^{k}\mathbf{H}_{t}(y_{j+1}-y_{j})-\frac{k-1}{k}\sum_{j=1}^{k}\mathbf{H}_{t}(y_{j+1}-y_{j})

\displaystyle\leq-\frac{1}{k}\sum_{j=1}^{k}\mathbf{H}_{t}(y_{j+1}-y_{j})-\frac{k-1}{k}\sum_{j=2}^{k}\mathbf{H}_{t}(y_{j+1}-y_{j}).

By Lemma
B.1
,

-k^{-1}\displaystyle\sum_{j=1}^{k}\mathbf{H}_{t}(y_{j+1}-y_{j})\leq-k\textbf{H}_{t}^{1,k+1}(y_{k+1}-y_{1})\leq-\textbf{H}_{t}^{1,k+1}(y_{k+1}-y_{1}).

For
j\in[1,k-1]_{\mathbb{Z}}
, let
z_{j}=y_{j+1}
. Then by the induction hypothesis,

\displaystyle-\frac{k-1}{k}\sum_{j=2}^{k}\mathbf{H}_{t}(y_{j+1}-y_{j})=

\displaystyle-\frac{k-1}{k}\sum_{j=1}^{k-1}\mathbf{H}_{t}(z_{j+1}-z_{j})\leq-\frac{k-1}{k}\sum_{j=1}^{k-1}\mathbf{H}^{j,k}_{t}(z_{k}-z_{j})

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle-\frac{k-1}{k}\sum_{j=2}^{k}\mathbf{H}^{j-1,k}_{t}(y_{k+1}-y_{j})=-\sum_{j=2}^{k}\mathbf{H}^{j,k+1}_{t}(y_{k+1}-y_{j}).

The proof is finished.
∎

Lemma B.3
.

Fix
p_{-}<p<p_{+}
. Let
\mathring{L}:[p_{-},p_{+}]\to\mathbb{R}
be a continuous function which is linear in
[p_{-},p]
and
[p,p_{+}]
. Fix
q\in(p_{-},p_{+})
and
b\in\mathbb{R}
. Let
L
be a continuous function which is linear in
[p_{-},q]
and
[q,p_{+}]
with
L(p_{\pm})=\mathring{L}(p_{\pm})
and
L(q)=b
. Define

\displaystyle\mathring{m}_{\pm}\coloneqq\frac{\mathring{L}(p)-\mathring{L}(p_{\pm})}{p-p_{\pm}},\ {m}_{\pm}\coloneqq\frac{{L}(q)-\mathring{L}(p_{\pm})}{q-p_{\pm}}.

Then for all
x\in[p_{-},p_{+}]
,

(B.2)

\mathring{L}(x)-L(x)\leq|L(p)-b|+|p-q|\max\left\{|\mathring{m}_{-}|,|\mathring{m}_{+}|\right\}

Furthermore, suppose
|p-q|\leq 2^{-1}\min\{p-p_{-},p_{+}-p\}
. Then

\left|{m}_{-}-\mathring{m}_{-}\right|\leq 2\left|\frac{b-L(p)}{p-p_{-}}\right|+2\left|\frac{p-q}{p-p_{-}}\right||\mathring{m}_{-}|.

\left|{m}_{+}-\mathring{m}_{+}\right|\leq 2\left|\frac{b-L(p)}{p_{+}-p}\right|+2\left|\frac{p-q}{p_{+}-p}\right||\mathring{m}_{+}|.

Proof.

Without loss of generality, we assume that
q<p
. It suffice to prove (
B.2
) at
x=p
and
x=q
. For
x=q
,

\displaystyle\mathring{L}(q)-{L}(q)=[\mathring{L}(p)-\mathring{m}_{-}(p-q)]-b\leq|\mathring{L}(p)-b|+|p-q||\mathring{m}_{-}|.

For
x=p
,

\displaystyle\mathring{L}(p)-{L}(p)=

\displaystyle\mathring{L}(p)-\left[\frac{(p_{+}-p)b+(p-q)\mathring{L}(p_{+})}{p_{+}-q}\right]

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{p_{+}-p}{p_{+}-q}(\mathring{L}(p)-b)+(p-q)\frac{p_{+}-p}{p_{+}-q}\mathring{m}_{+}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle|\mathring{L}(p)-b|+|p-q||\mathring{m}_{+}|.

This shows (
B.2
).

Under the assumption that
|p-q|\leq 2^{-1}\min\{p-p_{-},p_{+}-p\}
,

\displaystyle\left|{m}_{-}-\mathring{m}_{-}\right|=

\displaystyle\left|\frac{b-\mathring{L}(p_{-})}{q-p_{-}}-\frac{\mathring{L}(p)-\mathring{L}(p_{-})}{p-p_{-}}\right|

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\left|\frac{b-\mathring{L}(p)}{q-p_{-}}+\frac{(p-q)(\mathring{L}(p)-\mathring{L}(p_{-}))}{(q-p_{-})(p-p_{-})}\right|

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle 2\left|\frac{b-L(p)}{p-p_{-}}\right|+2\left|\frac{p-q}{p-p_{-}}\times\frac{\mathring{L}(p)-\mathring{L}(p_{-})}{p-p_{-}}\right|

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle 2\left|\frac{b-L(p)}{p-p_{-}}\right|+2\left|\frac{p-q}{p-p_{-}}\right||\mathring{m}_{-}|.

The bound for
\left|{m}_{+}-\mathring{m}_{+}\right|
is similar.
∎

Lemma B.4
.

Let
x_{1}<x_{2}\in\mathbb{R}
,
b_{1},b_{2}\in\mathbb{R}
and
m>0
. Assume that
|b_{2}-b_{1}|\leq m(x_{2}-x_{1})
. Let

\displaystyle x_{0}=

\displaystyle 2^{-1}(x_{1}+x_{2})+(2m)^{-1}(b_{2}-b_{1})\in[x_{1},x_{2}],

\displaystyle b_{0}=

\displaystyle 2^{-1}(b_{1}+b_{2})+2^{-1}m(x_{2}-x_{1}).

The point
(x_{0},b_{0})
is determined by

\displaystyle\frac{b_{0}-b_{1}}{x_{0}-x_{1}}=m,\ \frac{b_{2}-b_{0}}{x_{2}-x_{0}}=-m.

Then

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}(B(x_{0})\geq b_{0}\ |B(x_{1})=b_{1},B(x_{2})=b_{2})\geq\mathbb{P}(N\geq(x_{2}-x_{1})^{1/2}m).

Proof.

Then mean and variance of
B(x_{0})
is given by

\displaystyle M=

\displaystyle 2^{-1}(b_{2}+b_{1})+(2m(x_{2}-x_{1}))^{-1}(b_{2}-b_{1})^{2},

\displaystyle\sigma^{2}=

\displaystyle(4m^{2})^{-1}((x_{2}-x_{1})m^{2}-(x_{2}-x_{1})^{-1}(b_{2}-b_{1})^{2}).

Then

\displaystyle\sigma^{-1}(b_{0}-M)=((x_{2}-x_{1})m^{2}-(x_{2}-x_{1})^{-1}(b_{2}-b_{1})^{2})^{1/2}\leq(x_{2}-x_{1})^{1/2}m.

∎
